## Why this document exists

A premortem on the original Phase 2 plan flagged the cost of *not having*
written criteria. The two failure modes that document is designed to
prevent:

1. **Sunk-cost continuation** — two weeks in, the data is mediocre, but
   you have $X invested and a working harness, so you push to "give it one
   more week." Without pre-committed numbers, this happens by default.
2. **Vague go decisions** — Phase 3 binary build is 4–6 weeks of work. If
   Phase 2 produces "looks fine to me" data, Phase 3 is built on hope.
   Pre-committed numbers force the question: *what specific finding from
   Phase 2 changed my Phase 3 design?*

|---|---|---|---|

| Median tok/s — medium_with_system | **17.3** | **22.5** | query B |
| Median tok/s — code_completion | **19.8** | **25.5** | query B |
| Median tok/s — agent_style | **18.3** | **23.8** | query B |
| Streaming TTFT (streaming_check) | **708 ms** | **646 ms** | runs |
| Stop-token leak rate (all workloads) | **0%** | **0%** ← see decision log | query C |
| Adversarial `retry_storm` p95 latency | TBD | TBD | query D |
| `long_context_oom_probe` outcome | TBD | TBD | adversarial_runs |

| Model serving on day 0 | `Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-4bit` (16GB tier) | `llama-3.2-3b-instruct-4bit` (8GB tier) | config-mX.yaml |
| Streaming `usage` field present in SSE | **NO** — confirmed Phase 3 spec item | **NO** — same behavior, cross-model | runs |

**M4 baseline captured:** 2026-05-26T09:33Z via `scripts/m4-cooperative.sh`.
Single batch of 5 workloads through https://m4.streamvc.live → Qwen 7B 4-bit
on M4 hardware. Zero failures, zero stop-token leaks, throughput band
17.3-19.8 tps tight across context sizes 37-343 prompt tokens.

**Notable day-0 finding:** `streaming_check` had no `usage` chunk in the SSE
stream from mlx_lm.server — prompt_tokens and completion_tokens both NULL.
Phase 3 binary spec must synthesize usage on streamed responses.

**Reference queries (copy-paste into `sqlite3 runs.sqlite`):**

```sql
-- A. HTTP 200 rate, cooperative cron
SELECT ROUND(100.0 * SUM(http_status=200) / COUNT(*), 1) AS ok_pct
FROM runs;

-- B. Median tok/s per workload
SELECT workload, ROUND(AVG(throughput_tps), 1) AS tps
FROM runs WHERE throughput_tps IS NOT NULL GROUP BY workload;

-- C. Stop-token leak rate per workload
SELECT workload,
       SUM(stop_token_leak) AS leaks,
       COUNT(*) AS n,
       ROUND(100.0 * SUM(stop_token_leak) / COUNT(*), 1) AS leak_pct
FROM runs GROUP BY workload;

-- D. Adversarial retry_storm p95
SELECT model, ROUND(p95_ms) FROM adversarial_runs
WHERE workload='retry_storm' ORDER BY ts_utc DESC LIMIT 5;

-- E. Tool-call JSON parse rate — see "Day 5 hard probe" below
```

## Day 2 mid-review — continue / pivot signal (2026-05-28)

**Proceed to day 3 final review only if ALL of the following are true:**

- [ ] **Uptime:** ≥2 days had ≥6 hours each with at least one provider reachable.
      *Query:*
      ```sql
      SELECT substr(ts_utc,1,10) AS day, COUNT(DISTINCT substr(ts_utc,1,13)) AS hours_with_data
      FROM runs WHERE http_status=200 GROUP BY day HAVING hours_with_data >= 6;
      ```
      Count the rows; need ≥ 2.

- [ ] **Cooperative reliability:** HTTP 200 rate per provider ≥ **80%**.
      *Query:* group by `tunnel_url`, expect column `ok_pct >= 80`.

- [ ] **Adversarial survival:** ≥1 successful `adversarial_runs` row per
      provider where `n_ok > 0` AND the provider's `mlx_lm.server` was
      still answering cooperative-cron traffic afterward. (Daily adversarial
      cron means each provider gets ≥2 runs by day 2.)

- [ ] **Stop-token detection diagnosed:** day-0 showed 0% leakage on both
      Qwen and Llama — either upstream mlx-lm now strips correctly, or
      our task-#13 per-model regex regressed. By day 2 we must know which
      (run a deliberate leak probe if needed).

- [ ] **No provider went dark for >24 hours** without explanation.

- [ ] **Operator can answer:** "what % of `agent_style` responses contain
      parseable tool-call JSON?" — at least 80% confidence in the number.

**If any FAIL → enter "iterate" loop:** see "What 'iterate' means" below.
Don't just extend day 2 hoping the next 24h fixes it.

---

## Day 3 final decision — Phase 3 go/no-go (2026-05-29)

**Proceed to Phase 3 (Swift binary build) only if ALL of the following:**

- [ ] All day-2 criteria still hold cumulatively over the 3 days (not just
      day-3 data in isolation).

- [ ] **Provider retention:** both providers reachable on day 3 (lid-close
      cycles OK, full silence not OK). One drop-off mid-experiment =
      downgrade to "iterate;" both gone = pivot.

- [ ] **JSON mode reliability:** ≥**90%** valid-JSON tool-call responses
      on at least one model in the rotation. With ~500 cooperative samples
      per provider over 3 days, this is statistically tight enough.

- [ ] **Cross-model differential exists:** Qwen 7B (M4) vs Llama 3B (M1)
      ran the same corpus-sampled prompts during overlapping hours. SQL
      JOIN should produce ≥**100** prompt-matching pairs to compare
      throughput/quality across models.

- [ ] **Specific Phase 3 design decisions changed:** must name ≥**3**
      Phase 3 binary spec lines that changed *because of* Phase 2 data.
      **Day-0 already captured 3** (502/530 routing, post-wake warm-up,
      capacity/quality tradeoff) — bar is already met. Anything more is
      bonus.

**If any FAIL → "iterate" or "pivot."** Don't ship to Phase 3 on hope.

**Contributor wrap-up:** regardless of go/no-go, send both contributors a
"thanks, you can shut down or keep running" message on day 3. The
cloudflared services keep running if they don't act — no urgency for them.

---

## Hard-stop triggers (any one of these — stop immediately, no review)

- Either provider goes dark for >24 hours **without explanation** (silence,
  not "I'm on vacation, back tomorrow"). Tighter than 14-day version because
  3 days has less buffer for outages.
- A provider's Mac **crashes more than once** from Phase 2 workloads
  (Metal OOM kernel panic, hard reboot needed). One is data; two is harm.
- A provider asks to stop. Their decision; no negotiation.
- Operator cannot answer the JSON parse-rate question by day 5 → the
  measurement apparatus is broken; fix or stop.
- Adversarial `concurrent_burst_8way` consistently puts a provider's Mac
  into thermal throttle that persists >30 min after the burst — that's
  past "fans spin" into "we're hurting their hardware."

---

## Soft-stop self-check (day 7 and day 14)

These don't auto-trigger a stop but force a conversation with yourself:

- Am I still finding this interesting, or am I forcing momentum?
- Is the operator-side workload (config tweaks, report reviews,
  contributor pings) under or over the budget I committed to in the
  header?
- Has anything in the **outside world** changed that makes this less
  important? (Anthropic ships local MLX inference, Darkbloom open-sources,
  Antseed changes seller protocol, etc.)
- If I had to **start Phase 2 over today** knowing what I know, would I?

If two or more of these tip negative, treat the day-7/14 review as a
"pivot" by default unless the data is exceptional.

---

## What "iterate" means

Not all failures kill the project — some need a second pass. The iterate
menu, ordered by severity:

| Failure | Iteration |
|---|---|
| Cooperative HTTP 200 rate <80%, root-caused to tunnel instability | Switch tunnel mechanism (named cloudflared ↔ ngrok), extend 1 week |
| Cooperative reliability fine, but one workload type fails reliably | Drop that workload from `batch:`, re-run week with reduced set |
| JSON mode <90% on all models | Add a non-MLX cloud fallback as comparison data point, do NOT pivot |
| Adversarial probes crash one provider's Mac once | Disable that specific adversarial workload, continue beta |
| Day-7 numbers fine but no Phase 3 spec change emerged | Add 1 more week of targeted data collection on the highest-uncertainty Phase 3 question |

**Cap: one iteration cycle.** If iterate-1 also fails its criteria,
pivot.

---

## What "pivot" means (the menu)

Phase 2 isn't load-bearing for anything outside this project — pivot is
real, not theoretical. Options, ordered by reversibility:

1. **Narrow scope:** Drop the second provider, run Phase 2 as N=1.
   Acceptable if the M4 user's data alone is rich enough to inform Phase 3.
2. **Change beachhead:** If Type B (AI power user) data shows poor
   retention, shift the Phase 3 spec target to Type A (passive earner).
   Same code, different pitch.
3. **Defer Phase 3 binary entirely:** Fork Darkbloom under their license
   constraints if the legal/dependency risk is now smaller than the
   re-implementation cost. (Requires re-reading their license.)
4. **Stop and write up findings:** If Phase 2 produces a clear "Mac
   inference at this scale doesn't work" answer, that result is itself
   publishable and informs other people's decisions.

The cost of NOT pivoting when you should: 4–6 weeks of Phase 3 binary
build burned on a foundation you don't trust.

(b) **silent production-breaking regression**: Go stream's per-stream audit fix "pinned providers require validated bearer when token validator is configured" was implemented in `cmd/coordinator/main.go` as `WithTokenValidator(tokenStore)` UNCONDITIONALLY, so server.go line 168 rejected ANY pinned provider (M4/M1 don't send tokens — they predate the token system) with WS close 4005. **And `s.close()` did not log**, so 15 min of production downtime showed only nginx access logs (101 Switching Protocols) with zero coord-side rejection traces. Hotfix shipped: `auth.require_provider_tokens` config flag (default false) + s.close now logs close_code + reason at warn level. After hotfix redeploy, M4 + M1 reconnect loops fired automatically; pool returned to N=2 ready within 30s. Multi-model routable, Llama 3B 1.77s, Qwen 7B 2.52s. | **Three lessons compound:** (1) **Three-stream parallel build pattern works** — saved ~40% wall time vs sequential per honest estimate, no merge conflicts because partitioning was by codebase ownership not by spec. Reusable pattern for SPEC-004+. (2) **Cross-model audit value confirmed at scale.** Per-stream Codex audits caught what each per-stream agent missed; cross-model integration audit (Codex + Claude) caught what BOTH per-stream agents missed (mock-implementation parity failure masking C1; the integration bug class needs both eyes). Specifically: Claude confirmed all 3 of Codex's integration CRITICALs (so we knew they were real) AND added 5 NEW MAJORs Codex missed. The repeated cross-model pattern from SPEC-001/002 generalized to implementation. Cleanest path: stage a 30-line bootstrap site serving only `/.well-known/acme-challenge/` over port 80, `certbot certonly --webroot -w /var/www/html`, then swap to the real config with `sed -i 's|^    # ssl_certificate|    ssl_certificate|g'` and `nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx`. First sed missed `ssl_certificate_key` because of a trailing-space anchor (`ssl_certificate ` vs `ssl_certificate_key `) — fixed second pass. Cert issued: Let's Encrypt YE2 intermediate, valid 2026-05-29 → 2026-08-27 (90 days), auto-renewal verified via `certbot renew --dry-run`. **Final HTTPS surface:** GET /healthz returns 200 with `{"status":"ok"}` over public HTTPS in ~0.9s round-trip; HTTP→HTTPS 301 redirect working; HSTS `max-age=31536000; includeSubDomains` set. **GitHub OAuth app registered:** operator created "Mac Provider" at github.com/settings/applications/new with homepage `https://api.streamvc.live`, callback `https://api.streamvc.live/auth/github/callback`. Five keys still present after patch: `COORDINATOR_OPERATOR_KEY`, `MACPROVIDER_KEY_HASH_SECRET`, `MACPROVIDER_DEMO_SIGNING_SECRET`, `GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID`, `GITHUB_OAUTH_CLIENT_SECRET`. Gateway restarted, picked up new env, `/auth/github/start` now returns 302 to `github.com/login/oauth/authorize?client_id=Ov23liD8Ou69YjDQwmC0&redirect_uri=...&scope=read:user&state=<43-char-csrf>` with HttpOnly Secure SameSite=Lax `mp_oauth_session` cookie (Max-Age 600s). **First live OAuth signup smoke (operator's browser, Augustas11 account):** OAuth round-trip GitHub authorize → /auth/github/callback → /account showed `mp_ETUbLs...` API key on the bare HTML page "Mac Provider account. Your new API key is shown once." Key stored to local file + password manager. **First live buyer round-trip:** `GET /v1/models` with bearer auth returned full model list (Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct-4bit + Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct-4bit) AND the `tier1_disclosure` block (`version:v0.6, plaintext_to_provider:true, model_identity:provider_reported, hardware_attestation:none, tier2_milestone:future`) — **F-C1 verified live on public HTTPS surface for the first time**. Streaming chat completion succeeded in 3.77s through full chain (gateway → coord → augustass-macbook-air provider → MLX) producing 5-fruits SSE stream with final usage block (49 prompt + 15 completion tokens). Non-streaming chat completion hung 300s, returned `coordinator_unavailable`. Initially thought to be a non-stream-specific bug; investigation showed both paths work, but the non-stream attempt got routed to remote partner `air5` (M4) whose WS connection drops on a ~3-5 min cycle (coord journal: `heartbeat stale gap:322s/314s/241s`, `ws relay failed` at periodic intervals). Re-test succeeded in 12.6s. Sequential batch of 5 short non-stream requests succeeded 5/5 at ~1.6s each. Effective success rate ~6/7 ≈ 86% over the brief smoke, with one 5-minute pathological tail concentrated entirely on the dead-WS-mid-inference failure mode. **Three guardrails shipped immediately to bound the worst-case buyer hang from 5 min to 2 min while the underlying fix is queued for Phase 6:** **G1** gateway.yaml `timeouts.coordinator_request_seconds 300→120` (covers ~500-token completions at observed rates, cuts pathological tail in half). **G2** coordinator.yaml `routing.request_timeout_s 300→120` (matches gateway timeout end-to-end; heartbeat config deliberately untouched to preserve M1 lid-close grace which depends on `disconnect_grace_period_s:30` + `heartbeat_interval_s:30`). **G3** per-request gateway log line: added `statusWriter` ResponseWriter wrapper to `internal/router/server.go` and a `defer slog.Info("chat completion", ...)` block in `handleChatCompletions` capturing `request_id`, `account_id`, `model`, `stream`, `wall_ms`, `status`. Cross-compiled linux/amd64 (16.4 MB ELF), SCP'd to Pearl, swapped binary, restarted — three test requests (non-stream, stream, bad-model) all produced clean structured log lines in journal, confirming end-to-end observability. **Final state on Pearl after all 3 guardrails:** gateway active on 127.0.0.1:9443 with new G3-instrumented binary; coordinator active on 127.0.0.1:8443+8444 with G2-tightened timeout; nginx serving HTTPS on 443 + HTTP→HTTPS 301 on 80; pool N=3 (M4/air5 + M1/air8gb + augustass-macbook-air, all v1.2.4 ws_tunneled). | **Four lessons codified from this arc:** (1) **Dry-run does not cover pool-traversing endpoints.** The Entry-26 dry-run smoke-tested only `/healthz`, `/v1/status`, `/v1/models` no-auth, unknown-route 404, and `/admin/*` 401. NONE of those exercise the gateway→coordinator→provider chain. The first buyer chat completion was therefore the first real test of the chain, and it found a real robustness gap. **Action item:** future dry-runs must include at least one chat completion through the pool with a real `mp_` key — even if it requires running OAuth signup as part of the dry-run. (2) **Bug class "single-mode timeout for multi-mode handlers" identified in gateway code.** server.go:814-819 only sets `context.WithTimeout` for `chat.Stream==true`; non-streaming uses bare `r.Context()` with only the HTTP client's 300s as backstop. Streaming has belt-AND-suspenders timeout (context + client); non-streaming has client-only. The G1 config change masks this but doesn't fix it — Phase 6 should make both paths use identical timeout discipline. (3) **Audit discipline doesn't catch pool-flakiness bugs.** The 4-tier Codex audit + 2-round FIX cycle on the gateway found 11 findings, ALL static-analysis-level (missing tier1_disclosure block, OAuth callback rejection, etc.). None of them caught the dead-WS-fast-fail gap because that requires a flaky provider running over a real network — exactly what production exposes. **Phase 6 should add a fault-injection test rig (simulate WS death mid-inference) to the audit checklist.** (4) **Honest framing wins again.** Could have shipped Entry 26 saying "Pearl deploy clean" and claimed launch readiness. The operator's question "every user will have to copy-paste a key into terminal?" surfaced the UX gap (front-door blocks soft-beta open) and the smoke test surfaced the robustness gap (5-min tail). Both are now captured + bounded + scheduled. The product is more credible after admitting these than after hiding them. | **State at Entry-27 commit:** API at `api.streamvc.live` is LIVE on public HTTPS with valid LE cert (auto-renews), OAuth signup mints `mp_*` keys, all SPEC-006 invariants validated live (tier1_disclosure on /v1/models, auth gating, redaction on /v1/status, OpenAI-envelope error shapes), and the pool serves real inference end-to-end. **Three guardrails in place** (G1+G2+G3) bound the worst-case bad-request experience to ~2 min `upstream_error` instead of 5-min silent hang. **NOT opening to public buyers yet** for two reasons: (a) the front-door (Vercel demo migration to call api.streamvc.live + integrate tier1_disclosure UI) is still 1-2 days of design+build work — without it, the buyer acquisition story is a bare HTML page that says "Your new API key is shown once"; (b) Phase 6 backlog includes the real fix for coordinator dead-WS fast-fail, which would let us remove G1/G2 and run the buyer-facing timeout at OpenAI-comparable defaults. **Phase 6 backlog filed:** SPEC-002 v1.1.6 candidate (coordinator fast-fail on dead provider WS for in-flight requests), SPEC-001 v1.2.5 candidate (phase3-binary keepalive defaults investigation — does air5's 3-5 min reconnect cycle indicate a client-side keepalive misconfig, server-side NAT idle, or M4 partner network NAT idle drops?), gateway audit category Z (fault-injection test rig: simulate WS death mid-inference, simulate slow-consumer buyer, simulate coordinator OOM), gateway code change (unify timeout discipline across stream/non-stream paths). **Front-door work** (Vercel demo migration + pretty /account page + "Copy" button + "I saved it" CTA + code-snippet on signup) tracked separately as the credible-UX layer that must ship before public beta open. **Day 5 closes for real, again, this time with a real live API at api.streamvc.live serving real inference to a real `mp_` key under valid TLS, three guardrails, and a Phase 6 backlog grounded in the data we just collected.** |

Live pool data confirms: both providers (augustass-macbook-air 8GB + air5 16GB) report `max_concurrency: 1` regardless of RAM tier. nginx routing block expanded with `limit_req_zone` and `limit_conn_zone` directives; audit category I.Y "default-permissive flag in production deployment" added); SPEC-006 v0.5→v0.6 (§ 1.6 normative Tier 1 disclosure with 4 properties — plaintext to provider, no hardware attestation, model identity provider-reported, no privacy/attestation/integrity claims; § 5 extension adds `tier1_disclosure` block to /v1/models response, non-operator-overrideable; § 1.3 forward-looking guard forbids sticky caching unless 5 explicit privacy preconditions met; § 19 audit category Y "expectation drift between roadmap and current enforcement"; § 22 production launch gate checklist with 8 items adapted verbatim from auditor's recommendations). 363 line insertions across the 3 patched specs; no code changes (verified empty diff for `phase3-binary/`, `phase4-coordinator/`, `phase5-gateway/`). **Dependency-line drift caught + fixed.** During verification: SPEC-002 v1.1.5's "Depends on:" line still said SPEC-001 v1.2.2 (two minor versions stale — the FIX prompt explicitly told the executing session to leave deps unchanged); The independent auditor's "expectation drift" framing — that future Tier 2 promises bleed into current Tier 1 positioning — is a *language-layer* finding our own audits weren't structured to catch. The Codex+Claude audit prompts asked for spec consistency and contract precision; they didn't ask "does the spec promise something the code can't deliver." That's a class only an external reader can surface, because they don't share our internal model of "what we mean by it." **Action item:** add a category to all future audit prompts that explicitly asks "what does this spec promise that the code does NOT deliver today?" — pointed at the language gap, not the consistency gap. (2) **Verifying audit findings against code matters even for high-confidence external auditors.** H-003's verification against `MacProviderCLI.swift:64` took 30 seconds and produced the precise smoking gun (literal comment matching the auditor's claim + the override line). Treating audit findings as confirmed-until-verified instead of confirmed-because-stated avoids both false positives (rare) and false confidence (more common). **Action item:** every audit-response cycle MUST include a code-verification step for at least the highest-impact mechanical findings; the spec patch should cite the verified code reference. (3) **Expectation drift lives at the language layer; spec-text discipline closes it.** SPEC-006 v0.6 § 1.6's 4 normative Tier 1 properties + the `tier1_disclosure` block in /v1/models are the load-bearing fix for H-001, H-004, and H-006 simultaneously. None required architecture change. All three closed by making positioning language explicit and non-overridable. The pattern: when an audit finds "your system promises X but doesn't deliver X," the fix is usually "stop promising X," not "deliver X." This is the same discipline as Entry 21's "no premium positioning" rule — extended now to "no privacy positioning, no attestation positioning, no untrusted-provider positioning." **Action item:** future SPEC-006 + BUILD_PHASE5 implementation work MUST treat § 1.6 disclosure paragraph + § 22 production launch gate checklist as non-negotiable launch criteria, not "nice to have." The 8 items: provider tokens mandatory (PG-1), proxy rate limits (PG-2), gateway exposes only buyer endpoints (already in v1.1.4 nginx routing), advertised = enforced concurrency (SPEC-001 v1.2.4), model identity labeled as provider-reported (§ 5.5), one terminal accounting outcome per request (D-CROSS-1 + cancel-usage), Tier 1 plaintext-to-provider disclosure (§ 1.6), Tier 2 claims blocked until Tier 2 enforcement is live (§ 1.6 + § 19 audit category). Each catches a different class. Per-spec catches single-spec quality; cross-spec catches inter-spec drift; regression catches narrow fix-side mistakes; external catches expectation drift. **Action item:** make external audit a normative pre-launch gate for any major surface (SPEC-006 v1.0 lock pre-BUILD_PHASE5, then re-audit before SPEC-008 Tier 2 lock). Budget ~$2-5K + a few weeks for the external audit cycle each time. Commit c94da11, tag v1.2.4 released cleanly. (2) **SPEC-006 v0.5 estimation-to-actuals swap.** Narrow follow-up patch: § 7.2 prefer-actuals normative with `ceil(bytes_emitted_so_far / 4)` fallback for pre-v1.2.4 binaries (M4 + M1 if they don't upgrade); § 17.5 refund matrix splits client-disconnect into v1.2.4+ row (exact, from usage field) and pre-v1.2.4 row (estimated, ±5 token tolerance); AC-37 expanded with Branch A (actuals) + Branch B (fallback). 38 insertions across SPEC-006; dependency line updated to SPEC-001 v1.2.3. SPEC-002/003 untouched. (3) **Self-canary v1.2.4 on augustass-macbook-air** via install.sh upgrade-in-place. Surfaced the FIRST install.sh upgrade bugs: install.sh defaulted to port 8080 even though existing install was on 18080 (no config-port detection); `ensure_port_free` refused to proceed because the current macprovider-cli service held the port (own-service-as-foreign-holder). Manual launchctl bootout + tarball swap got the v1.2.4 binary running locally; pool registration verified within 10s; production round-trip via coord returned `V124-PROD-OK` in 1.73s. (4) **M4 partner upgrade: 6 distinct install.sh failure modes in one session.** What was supposed to be a 30-second `curl get.streamvc.live/install.sh | bash` turned into ~90 minutes of partner-debugging via Signal. The bugs (in order they surfaced): (a) port-detection failure (same as self-canary); (b) own-service-port collision (same); (c) M4's old install wasn't under standard LaunchAgent domain so `launchctl bootout gui/$UID ... 2>&1` returned "input/output error"; (d) **install.sh proceeded to model download + service start, but the binary crashed silently with launchd exit code 255 + empty err log**; (e) manual launch directly via `~/macprovider/macprovider-cli` worked cleanly, exposing that the issue was the launchd plist invoking the binary via `~/.local/bin/macprovider-cli` (the symlink path); (f) Swift's Bundle resolution on M4's macOS didn't dereference the symlink, so it searched for `.bundle` directories in `~/.local/bin/` (empty) instead of `~/macprovider/` (where they actually are) → "MLX error: Failed to load the default metallib." This is **Entry 20 Bug A pattern resurfaced for the upgrade-in-place case** — fresh installs worked, upgrades broke. M4's filesystem also revealed Python venv leftovers in `~/macprovider/` (May 26 from some unrelated project) that install.sh silently overlaid the binary + bundles onto. After diagnosis: sed-patch M4's plist to invoke the real binary path; service came up clean; M4 registered as `air5` (M4 self-chose at install.sh's interactive prompt) in WS-tunneled mode (transitioned from legacy HTTP-forwarding via dead cloudflared tunnel). Cloudflared killed on M4's side; old `m4-anon` config entry orphaned but harmless. (5) **Coord config + restart in parallel with M4 debugging:** SSH'd to Pearl, edited `/opt/macprovider/coordinator.yaml` to drop M4's `endpoint_url` (force WS-tunneled mode), mirrored change in local `dist/coordinator.yaml`, restarted coord. My Mac (v1.2.4) reconnected in <5s per SPEC-001 v1.2.2's reconnect lifecycle fix. M4 came up as v1.2.4 / ws_tunneled / ready shortly after. (6) **BUILD_PHASE5_PROMPT.md drafted** while M4 was debugging in parallel: 501-line single prompt with 5-phase checkpoint structure (Phase A scaffolding + storage interfaces, B auth + identity, C quota + streaming + buyer surface, D status + feedback + capacity + kill switches, E integration + deployment + docs). Each phase prints a handback summary and stops for operator review. Hard constraints encoded: no upstream spec edits, no design relitigation, stateless handlers, sub-ms p95 auth check, backward-compat for pre-v1.2.4 providers via estimation fallback, no premium positioning, no Tier-3 deprecation. (7) **SPEC-003 v0.7 install.sh hardening cycle.** Six findings filed from M4's debugging + self-canary, all closed in one coordinated FIX session: F-603-V7-1 read existing config to detect port; F-603-V7-2 `ensure_port_free` stops own service via launchctl bootout before erroring (foreign holders still exit 6); F-603-V7-4 plist invokes real binary path (`~/macprovider/macprovider-cli`) so Swift Bundle resolution finds `.bundle` directories adjacent — symlink at `~/.local/bin/` remains for shell PATH; F-603-V7-5 cold-cache deadline 20 min vs 5 min warm (detects HF cache directory presence); F-603-V7-6 self-test timeout message diagnostic-rich (tells user how to verify binary alive vs actually crashed, where to find logs, when to give up); F-603-V7-7 warns on non-empty INSTALL_DIR with non-macprovider entries (doesn't block). install.sh +135 lines, SPEC-003 +97 lines. **Hardware-verified scenario 2 on augustass-macbook-air:** ran the LOCAL install.sh (with v0.7 patches) against existing v1.2.4 install. F-603-V7-1 logged "Detected existing config port: 18080"; F-603-V7-2 logged "Existing macprovider-cli holding port 18080; stopping it for upgrade-in-place. Port 18080 freed; proceeding with upgrade"; service rebootstrapped on PID 60656; v1.2.4 reinstalled cleanly in 23s. Pool reconnect to ready. **Plist now explicitly verified to use the real binary path** (`/Users/augstar/macprovider/macprovider-cli`), not the symlink. The discipline pattern continues to outperform shortcutting. (3) **Hardware-verification gates work even when the executing session feels confident.** Stream 1 deferred v1.2.3 tagging at the gate (Day 3); v1.2.4 cycle's executing session deferred tagging until the hardware cancel test produced `chunks_sent=26, completion_tokens=26` exact-match numbers; v0.7's executing session deferred scenario 1+2 since they'd require destructive live state changes. | 2026-05-28 | **First stranger-shaped `curl-pipe-bash` install surfaces 4 bugs in install.sh (A–D); all shipped same-day.** With the GitHub repo flipped public + `get.streamvc.live` → raw.githubusercontent.com redirect live + v1.2.1 signed release published, I ran the literal command a stranger would run: `curl -fsSL https://get.streamvc.live/install.sh \| MACPROVIDER_PORT=18080 bash`. Four distinct bugs surfaced across two retest cycles. **Bug A (the obvious one):** the install put the binary at `~/.local/bin/macprovider-cli` but copied the `mlx-swift_Cmlx.bundle` + `swift-nio_NIOPosix.bundle` Metal/transport bundles into `~/macprovider/` — mlx-swift loads `.bundle` resources adjacent to the executing binary, so on first run Metal kernel JIT failed with "Library not found". Fix: install bundles + binary together in `~/macprovider/` and make `~/.local/bin/macprovider-cli` a symlink (PATH discoverability preserved, bundles found). **Bug B (the user-experience one):** `MACPROVIDER_PORT=18080 curl ... \| bash` sets the env on `curl`, not on `bash` — port stayed default 8080, collided with the previous v1.2.1 install still listening, second copy crashed silently. Fix: added `ensure_port_free()` that runs `lsof -nP -iTCP:$PORT -sTCP:LISTEN -t` after quarantine-clear, prints the holding process + correct invocation form (`curl ... \| MACPROVIDER_PORT=18080 bash`), exits non-zero. **Bug C (the cosmetic one):** when the install script redirects /dev/tty for confirm prompts inside a pipe, bash leaked `read: 1: Bad file descriptor` noise every iteration. Fix: `read_line` now opens /dev/tty as fd 4 with `exec 4</dev/tty` only after `[ -c /dev/tty ]`, falls back to plain `read` from stdin if no controlling tty exists. **Bug D (the deep one, surfaced only after A+B+C shipped as v1.2.2):** the self-test `wait_for_local_model` did `grep -Fq "$model"` against the response — but Swift's JSONEncoder emits forward-slashes as the legal-but-cosmetic `\/` escape, so `"id":"mlx-community\/Llama-..."` never matched the unescaped `mlx-community/Llama-...` we searched for. The binary was actually up and serving correctly within 12s of port-bind; the install script just never recognized it and printed "Local self-test failed" while everything was green. The previous "extend deadline to 300s + progress logging" fix made the failure mode louder (waiting 5 minutes instead of 1) but not correct. Real fix: normalize the response with `sed 's\|\\\/\|/\|g'` before the grep, tolerating both encodings. | **Pattern: the audit rounds caught what's wrong with the design; only the stranger-shaped test catches what's wrong with the surface.** All four bugs are in glue code that no audit reviewer would flag because each line is locally correct: install.sh copies a tarball, ensures a port is free, reads a prompt, parses JSON. The failure mode in every case was an interaction with the user's actual shell environment (curl-pipe-bash semantics, /dev/tty in a pipe, JSON-escape conventions, mlx-swift bundle-loading rules). Worth name-checking in future SPEC-003+ audit categories: **"every shell-script path that touches a real OS resource (tty, fd, port, FS layout, JSON over loopback) needs an integration test that actually exercises it, not a code review."** Decision-side lesson: the cost of shipping v1.2.1 → v1.2.2 → install.sh-only patch (no release bump) was small precisely because the install.sh distribution channel is decoupled from the binary release tarball — the get.streamvc.live → main-branch redirect means a 1-line install.sh fix lands in seconds without re-running the macos-15 build action. That's worth preserving as an explicit architecture property in SPEC-003. | **State at Entry-20 commit: install.sh patched and pushed to main (commits d1093de + 7aae075). `curl -fsSL https://get.streamvc.live/install.sh \| MACPROVIDER_PORT=18080 MACPROVIDER_NO_PROMPT=1 bash` now goes green end-to-end in <30s on a warm cache: port bound 12s, model registered immediately, coordinator pool/check reports `tier: provisional, state: ready`. v1.2.2 binary tarball stays current (binary unchanged); install.sh from main carries the four fixes. The full distribution stack is now actually-strangers-can-install — repo public, redirect live, signed release published, install script tolerates the wire format we actually emit. **Open follow-ups:** (a) consider patching the Swift JSON encoder to stop emitting needless `\/` escapes (cosmetic, would unbreak other downstream tooling that may have the same assumption); (b) Bug A's symptom was reported as "Local self-test failed" — the same generic message as Bug D — which delayed root-cause attribution by one whole retest cycle; the next install.sh revision should print the failing grep's actual input bytes on failure so the operator sees the wire format directly; (c) Bug D should be a SPEC-003 spec-side normative requirement that "owned_by:macprovider" presence in /v1/models response is the readiness signal, with an explicit example showing both `\/` and `/` are acceptable in the id field (decouples client tolerance from server encoder).** |

Fix 2: eliminated gratuitous `tx.Commit()` in the `ErrQuotaExceeded` rejection path of `store.ReserveQuota` — that commit could fail and escape the 429 branch; replaced with `tx.Rollback()` (read-only path, semantically identical). Nginx gap closed: `limit_conn buyer_conn 20` + `limit_conn_status 429` added to `/v1/` location. **Phase C re-run (gateway sha `5de08803`, fresh quota, 100c/5min/invalid-model):** 320,858 requests at 1,069 req/s; avg 96ms / p99 255ms (vs 194ms/633ms pre-fix — faster because quota exhaustion is no longer a bottleneck); 277,152 status=429 (concurrency cap ✅), 10,847 status=404 (coord pass-through ✅), **0 `quota_reservation_failed` status=500** ✅; 38 status=0 (context-cancel early-return — new expected behavior: handler exits silently when buyer disconnects mid-reservation), 12 status=500 at run-termination second only (`concurrency_reservation_failed` from context.Canceled when wrk closed all 100 connections simultaneously — pre-existing, not a quota issue, no quota impact); **quota delta = 0** (used=1,395 before AND after, reserved=0 throughout) ✅; zero SQLite contention errors ✅; RSS growth 15.6MB over 5 min; both services alive ✅. **Phase C: PASS.** **Phase D (50-account SQLite load): PASS.** 50 test accounts pre-created directly in gateway.db (required brief authorized gateway stop to release WAL write lock — modernc.org/sqlite embedded in the Go gateway holds the lock continuously; external Python C libsqlite3 cannot acquire it while the gateway is live; this is a test-methodology artifact, not a gateway bug). Prerequisite fix required: `inferenceResponseEnd` in the mockprovider was missing the `Usage` field added by the Phase 7 warmup gate (`completion_tokens > 0` check); one-line struct fix + `mockUsage` constant added to all `sendInferenceEnd` call sites; mock passed warmup gate on attempt 1 after rebuild. Three sticky behaviors verified from coordinator routing_decision journal: (1) First request of each conversation: `sticky_miss_not_found` → deterministic routing → **sticky pin written** (conv-A → mock-qwen-1, conv-C → air5). (2) Subsequent same-conv requests: no `sticky_miss_not_found` emitted — pin found on every lookup ✅. (3) When pinned provider is outside ε=0.05 cohort of best provider (air5 metric=1.12 vs mock metric=9; threshold=8.55): coordinator emits `sticky_outside_epsilon` and correctly overrides the pin — **correct SPEC-004 Pillar A behavior** (sticky doesn't trap buyers on a slow provider when a much faster one is available). `sticky_hit` (swap-to-front case) not triggered because mock's artificial TPS=9 is 8× air5's real TPS=1.12 — outside any reasonable ε cohort. To observe `sticky_hit` requires two same-speed providers. Pillar A is behaviorally verified end-to-end. Also: mockprovider `Usage` fix committed to repo (fixes a latent bug where `tools/mockprovider` was out of sync with Phase 7 warmup gate requirements). **Phase F (coordinator failover under load): PASS with confound note.** 50-RPS load (50c, 90s, invalid-model path). Coordinator restarted at T+20s: stopped 23:59:39 → listening again 23:59:40 — **restart duration 1 second** (target: ≤5s). Gateway did not crash. Both services active post-restart; P2 monitor shows clean state (pool: air5+air8gb ready, coord_up: true, gw_status: up). Confound: test window straddled UTC midnight (daily quota reset at 00:00); pre-restart error rates were inflated by quota_reservation_failed (not coordinator unavailability) and dropped post-restart due to quota reset rather than purely coordinator recovery. Re-run recommended with a fresh-quota dedicated account. Also confirmed: `coordinator.yaml` has `model_classes` populated (mlx-fast / mlx-accurate from Entry 35) and Pillar B is live; `/internal/routing` endpoint doesn't surface model_classes but they are active (Entry 35 live-verified). | **Phase C now PASS; all three code/nginx fixes shipped.** Remaining open items before "production-shaped" final verdict: (1) Phase A 48h burn-in — verdict due 00:03 UTC 2026-06-02; (2) Phase F re-run with non-midnight timing and a fresh-quota dedicated account to get clean coordinator-recovery error-rate isolation. The 12 end-of-run `concurrency_reservation_failed` 500s (context.Canceled at teardown) are a known pre-existing rough edge — no quota impact, can be addressed in a follow-up fix (same pattern as the `quota_reservation_failed` fix). **Updated scorecard: A=running, B=5/6 pass, C=PASS, D=PASS, E=PASS, F=PASS (confound noted).** System is approaching production-shaped; the only blocking gate remaining is Phase A verdict. | All three fixes shipped in gateway sha `5de08803` + nginx reload. Follow-up (not blocking): `concurrency_reservation_failed` at connection teardown also needs context-cancel guard (same pattern applied to `quota_reservation_failed`). Phase A burn-in CSV: `/var/lib/macprovider/burnin.csv` on Pearl; check `tail -1` at 00:03 UTC 2026-06-02 for 48h RSS/FD growth verdict. SPEC-006 §17.7 (quota refund matrix): update to enumerate the context-cancel/timeout refund path as a required invariant — the fix was code-only, no spec update was made. |

Gateway now exits silently on buyer disconnect at the concurrency gate; no 500 written to a dead connection. **(2) SPEC-006 §17.7 context-cancel refund invariant:** SPEC-006 was bumped to v0.8.3 (2026-05-31) in the same session. §17.7 matrix rows 2575–2576 now explicitly enumerate both the mid-quota-reservation and concurrency-gate context-cancel paths: "Gateway MUST exit silently without writing a 500 to the dead connection." Spec and code are in sync. **(3) Task #11 — configurable `maxChatRequestBodyBytes`:** `phase4-coordinator/internal/config/config.go:94` already has `MaxChatRequestBodyBytes int64 \`yaml:"max_chat_request_body_bytes"\`` wired into `buyer/server.go:220-221`, with validation (must be > 0 and ≤ 128 MiB). `coordinator.yaml` on Pearl (`dist/coordinator.yaml`) already ships `max_chat_request_body_bytes: 1048576` with comment "raise for buyers with large tools arrays." The item was implemented as part of the SPEC-004 Pillar B hardening but not noted as closed. | **Zero remaining pre-beta code items.** All three items carried forward from Entries 37–40 are confirmed closed in HEAD. Process lesson: when a carried-forward item is fixed, the fixing commit message should explicitly name the entry it closes so cross-session tracking is unambiguous. | **The system is fully ready for beta launch with no outstanding code, spec, or config debt.** The only pre-public-paid items remaining are operational gates (token re-issuance for `require_provider_tokens` flip, provisional-tier token gating) — none blocking for developer beta. |

| 2026-06-02 | **Entry 40 — Phase F coordinator failover re-run: PASS with one operational finding.** Clean re-run (no midnight confound): fresh test account (`acct_8a46c98637eacba70a4fb98d592c6196`, quota=0 pre/post), 50c/90s load on `/v1/chat/completions` with invalid model, coordinator restarted at T+20s. **Coordinator restart: 33ms** (target <5s ✅). Graceful drain: coordinator sent `provider drain` to air8gb before shutdown; new PID started in the same second (23:15:29Z). **Gateway did not crash** ✅ — active throughout, healthz=ok post-test. **Provider recovery: 15s** — air8gb reconnected at 23:15:42Z (hello_ack), passed warmup gate at 23:15:44Z, first heartbeat at 23:16:12Z. **Quota integrity: used=0 reserved=0 post-test** ✅ — all pending requests were context-canceled when wrk closed connections, refunds applied correctly. **Operational finding — `coordinator_request_seconds: 300` causes buyer hangs:** During the 15s provider-reconnect window, the gateway holds buyer connections open for up to 300s rather than failing fast. All 50 wrk connections hung silently; when wrk closed at T+90s the gateway logged all 413 requests simultaneously (23:16:39Z) with status=0 (context-cancel). The 409 wrk socket timeouts were wrk's 2s default firing on connections the gateway was holding. Gateway journal shows wall_ms of 428ms–13,466ms — the 15s window is the dominant delay (coordinator up in 33ms, but pool empty until air8gb passed warmup at T+15s). Root cause confirmed: gateway.yaml `timeouts.coordinator_request_seconds: 300` is 20× the provider warmup window. Fix: reduce to 15–20s (matching observed warmup duration) so gateway can return 503 fast instead of hanging buyers. **Full pre-public gate scorecard: A=PASS (RSS flat at 28.8MB, FDs flat, 0 errors/75320 req), B=PASS (6/6 fuzz probes, slow-loris fixed), C=PASS (quota/concurrency clean), D=PASS (50-account SQLite p99=56ms), E=PASS (sticky affinity + ε-override verified), F=PASS.** | **All six pre-public stress gates are now cleared.** The `coordinator_request_seconds=300` finding is non-blocking for developer beta (coordinator restarts are operator-triggered, infrequent events) but must be fixed before public paid traffic. | Reduce `coordinator_request_seconds` to 15–20s before public launch (config-only change, no code required). Remaining pre-code items: `concurrency_reservation_failed` context-cancel guard, SPEC-006 §17.7 spec update, Task #11 (configurable `maxChatRequestBodyBytes`). **Beta launch gate is open.** |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 42 — Billing no longer trusts provider-reported completion tokens as the sole payout input.** The audit remediation for T1.1 changed coordinator billing to preserve the server-observed completion estimate in `request_log.estimated_completion_tokens`, compute completion credits from `min(provider_reported_completion_tokens, server_observed_estimate)`, and make reconciliation/recovery use the preserved estimate instead of restoring inflated provider usage. The observed estimate is derived from emitted bytes using `tier2.output_bytes_per_token_ceiling`, so output caps and byte observation now bound money movement. Regression coverage proves a provider returning tiny output while reporting `completion_tokens=10000000` is billed at the byte-derived ceiling in hot-path billing, remains clamped through reconcile, and is recreated clamped by recovery. | **Trust boundary moved to the coordinator.** Provider usage remains recorded for audit, but payout is bounded by coordinator-observed output. This closes the architecture/security finding where an untrusted provider could self-report arbitrary completion tokens for real-USDC payout. | Continue T1 audit remediation with the same rule: any buyer/provider-controlled value that affects money, idempotency, or availability needs coordinator-owned evidence or explicit quarantine. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 43 — Buyer `X-Request-ID` is correlation only, never billing identity.** The T1.2 remediation changed the coordinator to generate a fresh request-log/billing ID for every buyer chat request, regardless of a valid client-supplied `X-Request-ID`. The gateway also now generates fresh coordinator-upstream IDs instead of forwarding the public request ID. Regression tests cover repeated buyer requests carrying one fixed UUID and prove they create distinct coordinator IDs; failover attempts within a single coordinator request still share one coordinator-owned ID so retry rows remain grouped correctly. | **The free-inference idempotency primitive is removed at ingress.** Buyers may still receive/use a public correlation ID, but they cannot choose the identifier that drives billing attempt grouping or coordinator request-log rows. | Any future idempotency API must be explicit, body-hash-bound, and separate from payout attempt derivation; do not re-use public correlation headers for money authority. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 44 — Gateway streaming scanner failures settle as failed streams, not `ok`.** The T1.3 remediation added an explicit `scanner.Err()` branch after SSE relay. Non-cancel read/scanner failures now emit an SSE error frame, log the read failure, and settle quota usage with `outcome=stream_truncated` instead of falling through to the clean-EOF `ok` branch. Clean EOF remains `outcome=ok`. Regression coverage includes an oversized SSE line that exceeds the scanner cap and a clean EOF control case. | **A truncated stream is no longer represented as successful inference.** The buyer still sees a committed 200 SSE transport once headers have gone out, but settlement and operator evidence now distinguish upstream stream failure from successful completion. | Any future streaming relay rewrite must preserve post-loop read-error classification before success settlement. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 45 — Raw HTTP stream cancellation is buyer-side, not a provider breaker fault.** The T1.4 remediation added the missing buyer-context check in the coordinator raw-HTTP streaming path before provider fault classification. If the buyer cancels after streamed work is observed, the provider receives normal credit for work done and no `breaker_qualifying` fault is recorded. Regression coverage drives a real raw-HTTP SSE provider, cancels the buyer after the first usage-bearing chunk, and verifies a paid non-breaker billing row while the provider remains `ready`. | **Provider fault accounting now matches the actual fault boundary.** Buyer disconnects no longer punish raw-HTTP providers or zero their payout, aligning the raw-HTTP path with the existing guarded WS streaming paths. | Any future stream-forwarding path must check buyer context before classifying provider read errors as breaker-qualifying. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 46 — Self-update trust roots are pinned in the binary, not inherited from process environment.** The T1.5 remediation removed the `MACPROVIDER_CHECKSUM_PUBLIC_KEY_PEM` runtime override from `SelfUpdate`, replaced the temporary-file `openssl` verification path with CryptoKit verification against the embedded P-256 public key, and removed the implicit `MACPROVIDER_RELEASES_API_URL` fallback. Explicit release API overrides remain available only as a CLI/test seam and are rejected unless they use `https://api.github.com`. Regression coverage sets both former environment variables and proves they do not affect the resolved release URL or signing key; untrusted explicit metadata endpoints fail before fetch. | **The self-update supply-chain root of trust is no longer operator-environment mutable.** A poisoned LaunchAgent, shell profile, or wrapper can no longer redirect both metadata and signature trust to a self-consistent malicious release through environment alone. | Release-signing key rotation must ship as pinned-key code through an already signed release. Do not reintroduce env-selected signing keys or unchecked metadata endpoints in release builds. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 47 — Provider WebSocket resource use is bounded immediately after upgrade.** The T1.6 remediation added coordinator config for provider WS handshake timeout, write timeout, max frame bytes, and max unauthenticated upgraded connections. After `UpgradeHTTP`, unauthenticated connections now reserve from a bounded semaphore, get a handshake read deadline before the first auth read, and are released only after registration or rejection. All provider reads go through a `wsutil.Reader` with `MaxFrameSize`; post-auth reads refresh the liveness deadline from the heartbeat miss threshold; direct and queued provider writes set write deadlines. Regression coverage proves a silent pre-auth upgraded client is dropped by the handshake deadline and an oversized initial frame is rejected. | **Unauthenticated provider sockets can no longer hold coordinator FDs forever or force unbounded frame buffering.** HTTP server timeouts are no longer the only availability boundary; the hijacked WebSocket path now carries its own limits. | Any new provider WS read/write path must use the bounded reader and deadline wrappers, not raw `wsutil.ReadClientData` or deadline-free writes. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 48 — Public pool-check rate limiting is keyed on trusted socket source and bounded.** The T1.7 remediation changed `/v1/pool/check` rate limiting from a never-evicting `sync.Map` keyed on buyer-controlled `X-Forwarded-For` to a mutex-protected TTL map keyed on the parsed host from `RemoteAddr`, with a default 4,096-entry cap and one-minute TTL. Regression coverage proves rotating spoofed `X-Forwarded-For` values no longer bypass the one-request-per-second limit for the same remote source, and a tiny-cap limiter evicts old unique keys under churn. | **The public pool-inspection endpoint no longer has a spoofable bypass or unbounded-memory keyspace.** The endpoint remains unauthenticated for compatibility, but its local abuse controls now depend on coordinator-observed connection metadata. | Do not trust forwarding headers for public rate limits unless a configured trusted-proxy allowlist is added and tested; keep public limiter state TTL- and size-bounded. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 49 — Gateway explorer buyer totals are aggregate-first, not join-product sums.** The T1.8 remediation rewrote `ExplorerListBuyers` to aggregate usage, quota reservations, concurrency reservations, and feedback in per-account CTEs before joining to accounts. Identity and API-key filters now use `EXISTS`, so multiple identities or keys filter accounts without multiplying usage/quota rows. Regression coverage seeds two usage rows, two active quota reservations, two identities, and two API keys, and verifies the buyer list reports the true totals: 24 used tokens and 70 reserved tokens. | **Operator-visible buyer accounting is no longer inflated by LEFT JOIN fan-out.** Dashboard totals now match underlying storage facts instead of the N-by-M shape of related rows. | Any future explorer aggregate that spans more than one one-to-many table must pre-aggregate each side or use separate aggregate subqueries before joining. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 50 — Coordinator availability claims are scoped to implemented behavior.** The T1.9 remediation removed the gateway's dormant `coordinators[]` failover config and runtime branch, so gateway configuration no longer validates or advertises a multi-coordinator capability it does not execute. Provider liveness now has three concrete controls: the Swift provider sends WebSocket ping frames before heartbeat frames, the coordinator enables TCP keepalive on upgraded provider sockets, and the provider binary starts a local `caffeinate` no-sleep assertion while a coordinator session is accepted. The deferred coordinator HA blast radius and tracking work are captured in `docs/notes/FOLLOWUP_COORDINATOR_HA_2026_06_03.md`. Regression coverage proves the provider reconnect loop re-enters connect after coordinator drain and that a provider session sends WebSocket ping before heartbeat. | **Availability semantics are now honest: one coordinator, bounded liveness checks, clean provider reconnect, but no claimed multi-coordinator failover.** Full coordinator state externalization remains out of scope for this audit fix and is tracked separately instead of implied by config. | Do not reintroduce `coordinators[]` or failover copy until gateway retries are backed by durable coordinator state or an explicit active-standby ownership design. Keep provider ping/pong, TCP keepalive, and no-sleep assertions as required controls for the Mac-provider leg. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 51 — Operator bearer checks no longer use direct string equality, and the public gateway has body/idle timeouts.** The Tier 2 remediation replaced direct `"Bearer "+key` comparisons in coordinator operator/admin handlers and gateway operator handlers with parsed bearer-token comparison over SHA-256 digests using `hmac.Equal`. Gateway's public HTTP server now sets `ReadTimeout=30s` and `IdleTimeout=120s` in addition to the existing `ReadHeaderTimeout=10s`, closing the slow-body/idle edge gap without changing upstream coordinator stream timeouts. Regression coverage locks the coordinator bearer helper, the gateway operator helper, and the gateway HTTP server timeout constructor. | **Operator-token timing no longer varies on raw secret string comparison, and gateway clients cannot hold slow request bodies or idle keepalives indefinitely.** | Keep operator/admin auth checks on the shared bearer helper or an equivalent digest-based constant-time comparator; do not add direct `Authorization == "Bearer "+key` checks in production paths. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 52 — Raw coordinator request logs now have bounded retention and SQLite PRAGMAs are connection-open policy.** The Tier 2 remediation added `storage.request_log_retention_days` with a 90-day default and a daily coordinator pruner for `request_log`, so buyer IPs and raw request attempts are not retained indefinitely after ledger/reconciliation tables have durable derived accounting. The prune query uses SQLite time comparison rather than lexicographic RFC3339 string ordering. Coordinator and gateway SQLite opens now apply `busy_timeout`, `foreign_keys`, `journal_mode(WAL)`, and `synchronous(NORMAL)` through modernc `_pragma` DSN parameters so recreated pooled connections inherit the same settings. The mock provider heartbeat stop channel is now closed through `sync.Once`, removing the deterministic double-close panic path. | **PII-bearing request logs are lifecycle-bounded, SQLite connection policy survives pool connection recreation, and the test mock provider can shut down idempotently.** | Keep long-lived accounting in ledger/settlement tables, not raw `request_log`; any retention change must remain at least as long as the nightly reconciliation lookback. |

| 2026-06-03 | **Entry 53 — Remaining Tier 2 web/auth/toolchain drift is closed or regression-locked.** Demo-token IP binding now normalizes IPv6 clients with `netip` and binds to a proper masked `/64` prefix instead of constructing malformed `net.IP(ip[:8]).String() + "::/64"` identifiers; same-prefix IPv6 privacy addresses validate, different prefixes fail. Gateway-served HTML no longer allows `script-src 'unsafe-inline'`; the account page uses a per-response CSP nonce and docs use `script-src 'none'`. The frontdoor console no longer uses `innerHTML` in its status/dashboard rendering path, and a static contract test blocks reintroduction; the coordinator operator explorer was also moved to DOM-node rendering with the same guard. Coordinator dispatch regression coverage now proves validated OpenAI-compatible fields (`seed`, `presence_penalty`, `frequency_penalty`, `response_format`) survive model-class alias rewriting. Both Go modules now declare `go 1.26`, matching the patched Go toolchain used for verification. | **The residual Tier 2 findings are reduced to explicit controls and tests rather than hidden assumptions.** Demo auth identifiers are canonical, browser script policy is nonce-bound, console status data is rendered through text nodes, coordinator dispatch preserves validated buyer parameters, and CI/runtime should no longer build against a stale vulnerable Go line by default. | Keep frontdoor status/dashboard rendering on DOM construction with `textContent`; do not relax gateway HTML CSP back to inline scripts. Any future module/toolchain downgrade needs an explicit vulnerability review. |

| 2026-06-06 | **Entry 54 — SPEC-010 v1.5 LOCKED (Provider Model Catalog) after 6-round audit cycle + scope-split decision.** Triggered by the arm64golf canary run (2026-06-05): operator could not change the served MLX model on a running provider without binary restart + WS reconnect + multi-minute cold load + red dashboard. **First three rounds (v0.1, v0.2, v0.3 as one wide-scope spec, ~1400 lines) failed to converge:** round-1 found 2 CRITICAL/9 MAJOR/3 MINOR, round-2 (a restructure pulling warm-swap into one spec) produced 0 CRITICAL/12 MAJOR/1 MINOR, round-3 (contract-tightening) introduced 1 NEW CRITICAL (SPEC-008 §5.7 hash-block collision) + 12 MAJOR. **Root cause:** the "additive on 4 locked specs" framing was structurally optimistic — capability advertisement + warm-swap mechanism + demand-pull cold-wake + buyer `/v1/models` aggregation + operator `/v1/status.state` together touch SPEC-001/SPEC-002/SPEC-004/SPEC-005/SPEC-008 in interlocking ways, and each fix pass expanded the contract surface faster than the prior round closed it. **Scope-split decision (2026-06-06):** work split into three specs — SPEC-010 (capability advertisement only, ~1300 lines after audits), SPEC-011 (operator-pushed warm swap, ~1700 lines, separate audit cycle still in flight), SPEC-012 (deferred; requires SPEC-008 v0.4 normative edit to §5.7 hash block when pain #3 buyer-visibility is prioritized). Wide-scope v0.3 draft preserved at `specs/SPEC-012-source.md`; its 3-round audit history at `specs/SPEC-012-source-audit-history.md`. **Narrow SPEC-010 v1.0 → v1.5 audit trajectory:** 0/3/1 → 0/3/2 → 0/5/0 → 0/2/5 → 0/0/3 → 0/0/0 (LOCK CONFIRMED). Each round Codex GPT-5; convergence followed once the scope matched what an additive contract can actually be. The repeated "code-grounding" finding pattern in rounds 2-4 (spec text didn't match actual parser line numbers, struct fields, or coordinator return paths) taught a methodology lesson: every wire claim and code citation must be line-anchored to specific source before audit, not after. **What v1.5 ships:** two optional fields on the v2 `auth_request` initial-stage frame (`supported_models[]`, `publishes_supported_models`); a coordinator `Provider` struct extension; opt-in `/v1/status` echo; `seenModels` index expansion; a 5-clause proof-stage parser/retention contract (R-3.1.10) with auth-attempt-scoped `defer releaseRetention(authAttemptID)` cleanup keyed on the coordinator-generated `auth_attempt_id` (server.go:354); a normative `parseAuthInitial` field table that is the interim source-of-truth for the v2 `auth_request` flow (no locked spec normatively documents it today; SPEC-001 v1.2.5 / SPEC-002 v1.3.5 candidate annotations per §6.1/§6.2 must add it); presence-gated retention so L-1 byte-identical default is literal (zero SPEC-010 internal state, not just zero observable state). **23 ACs** covering wire correctness, R-3.1.10 retention lifecycle, byte-identical default, validation order with exact `bad_request` reason strings, deterministic harness join for bounded-state assertions. **Locked-spec dependencies:** v1.5 cites SPEC-001 v1.2.4, SPEC-002 v1.3.4, SPEC-004 v0.3.1, SPEC-008 v0.3, SPEC-006 v0.8.1. v1.5 adds NO new coordinator state machine state, NO new audit-log event types, NO SPEC-005 billing interaction, NO SPEC-008 §5.7 hash-block change. | **arm64golf operator pain #3 (buyer-picker visibility) and #4 (HF ID discovery) are explicitly deferred to SPEC-012; SPEC-010 alone delivers the capability primitive that SPEC-011 (in audit) and SPEC-012 (deferred) build on.** The narrow-scope split is now a generalizable rule: when a spec touches >3 locked specs in interlocking ways, the "additive contract" framing usually means the audit cycle won't converge — split first. | Append SPEC-010 v1.5 §3.1 / §3.6 contract citations to the SPEC-001 v1.2.5 BUILD prompt (binary CLI + auth_request fields + R-3.6.3 local pre-flight) and the SPEC-002 v1.3.5 BUILD prompt (coordinator `Provider` struct extension, opt-in `/v1/status` echo, R-3.1.10 retention machinery, auth-attempt lifecycle normative addition). The SPEC-001 v1.2.5 / SPEC-002 v1.3.5 BUILD prompts MUST explicitly add a normative v2 `auth_request` section (locked SPEC-001 §6.5 / SPEC-002 §7.1 currently document the legacy `hello` handshake, not v2). Do NOT proceed with SPEC-012 until SPEC-008 v0.4 is on the table — the §5.7 hash-block normative edit is the unavoidable cross-spec dependency that took SPEC-010 v0.3 round 3 from 0 CRITICAL to 1 CRITICAL. SPEC-011 v0.4 audit is in flight; lock per its own trajectory when verdict lands. |

| 2026-06-06 | **Entry 55 — SPEC-011 v0.5 LOCKED (Operator-Pushed Warm Swap) after 4-round audit cycle; SPEC-001 v1.3 / SPEC-002 v1.3.5 BUILD prompts now fully unblocked.** Companion to Entry 54: the second of the three arm64golf-fix specs from the scope-split decision. SPEC-011 closes arm64golf operator pain #1 (multi-minute restart loop to change served model) and pain #2 (red-dashboard / WS reconnect on swap). **What v0.5 ships:** a new `macprovider-cli models switch <id>` operator-pushed warm-swap path on the running provider binary, gated by an opt-in `--enable-warm-swap` flag (L-1 byte-identical default preserved literally — zero SPEC-011 surface activates without the flag); macOS-native Unix-domain control socket at `$TMPDIR/macprovider-cli/ctl.sock` (NOT Linux `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR`) with deterministic detection precedence (ENOENT → fresh start, ECONNREFUSED → stale-socket reclaim, handshake timeout → abort); a 4-state in-process runtime state machine (ready → loading → draining → failed) with atomic factory-pointer swap and old-model retirement only after the new model passes a verification probe; opt-in heartbeat extension carrying `model_hash` (raw lowercase hex, matching `hexString` in `ModelRuntime.swift:294-325`) and `loading: bool` — gated on `--enable-warm-swap` so L-1 byte-identical heartbeat is byte-preserved when the flag is absent; a typed `switch_ack` WS frame per R-3.1.5 with explicit REQUIRED `type` field; an `operator_model_swap` audit event with full payload schema (operator identity, old/new model IDs, old/new hashes, transition timestamps, outcome); a concurrent-switch policy that returns exit code 3 with a cooldown reason; a WS-drop policy that preserves hello-based reconnect semantics (no v2 `auth_request` interaction on swap). **26 ACs** including the rewritten AC-26 (3 structural assertions + 4-location allowlist replacing the impossible-to-satisfy literal-substring grep contract from v0.4). **4-round audit trajectory:** v0.2 round 1: 2 CRITICAL / 5 MAJOR / 3 MINOR → v0.3 round 2: 0 / 2 / 4 → v0.4 round 3: 0 / 0 / 2 (LOCK-READY pending polish) → **v0.5 round 4: 0 / 0 / 0 → LOCK CONFIRMED.** All R3V findings PASS; Codex literally ran the AC-26 grep and verified all 16 `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` matches fall in AC-26's 4 allowed locations (change-log, R-3.1.5 "Why not" rationale, R-3.9.2 prohibition body, AC-26 self-reference). **Convergence happened faster than SPEC-010 (4 rounds vs 6)** because the scope-split decision was already made before SPEC-011's first round, so the narrow operator-pushed-warm-swap contract never carried the cross-spec churn that crashed wide-scope SPEC-010 v0.1-v0.3. The macOS platform-path mismatch (Linux `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` → macOS `$TMPDIR`) was caught as a round-1 CRITICAL and fixed in v0.3 — a recurring methodology lesson: spec text written from generic-Unix instincts must be cross-checked against the actual target platform's filesystem conventions (here, the spec is implemented on Darwin, not Linux). **Locked-spec dependencies:** SPEC-011 v0.5 cites SPEC-001 v1.2.4, SPEC-002 v1.3.4, SPEC-010 v1.5. SPEC-011 v0.5 adds NO new WS auth flow (warm swap is post-auth lifecycle on an already-connected hello session), NO SPEC-005 billing interaction, NO SPEC-008 §5.7 hash-block change (warm-swap heartbeat carries the new model's hash through the existing field, with hash-clearing REPLACEMENT semantics specified for the `ApplyHeartbeat` path in `phase4-coordinator/internal/pool/provider.go:411-432`). | **Both narrow arm64golf-fix specs (SPEC-010 v1.5 capability + SPEC-011 v0.5 warm-swap mechanism) are now LOCKED; the only remaining piece of the original wide-scope SPEC-010 v0.3 is SPEC-012 (buyer-visibility), which stays deferred per Entry 54 until SPEC-008 v0.4 normative §5.7 hash-block edit is on the table.** The combined effect: an operator running `macprovider-cli` with `--enable-warm-swap` can change served model with a single CLI call, the new model warms in-process while the old continues serving, the swap is atomic at a verified-ready instant, the heartbeat carries the new hash on the next tick, and there is zero WS reconnect / zero dashboard red / zero buyer-visible disruption (buyer-side picker visibility remains pain #3, deferred to SPEC-012). The 4-round convergence under a pre-committed scope split validates Entry 54's generalizable rule: scope split first, audit later. | **Immediate next step:** draft SPEC-001 v1.3 BUILD prompt citing SPEC-010 v1.5 (CLI flags + v2 `auth_request` field section + R-3.6.3 local pre-flight) and SPEC-011 v0.5 (`--enable-warm-swap` opt-in gate + macOS control-socket path + 4-state runtime machine + heartbeat extension). Draft SPEC-002 v1.3.5 BUILD prompt citing SPEC-010 v1.5 (`Provider` struct extension + opt-in `/v1/status` echo + R-3.1.10 retention machinery + auth-attempt lifecycle) and SPEC-011 v0.5 (heartbeat hash-clearing REPLACEMENT in `ApplyHeartbeat` + `operator_model_swap` audit event payload schema). Both BUILD prompts MUST add the normative v2 `auth_request` section to SPEC-001 §6.5 / SPEC-002 §7.1 (currently only the legacy `hello` handshake is locked-spec normative — see Entry 54 followups). SPEC-012 remains deferred, paired with future SPEC-008 v0.4 normative edit. The `backup-main-pre-merge-20260604` branch and Entry 54's scope-split rule remain in force; SPEC-011's faster convergence is empirical validation that the rule works, not an invitation to widen future specs back to interlocking-additive shape. |
