| 2026-06-11 | **Entry 60 — Open Q2 ruled: provisional providers self-serve their token on first admission.** Closes the gate left open by Entry 59 / M1-1. Two branches were on the table: operator-issued (friction, but every token is operator-vouched) or self-serve provisional (preserves curl|bash open onboarding). Self-serve picked because MacProvider is permissionless by design — gating provisional token issuance on operator action would re-create the very approval bottleneck that the open-onboarding tier exists to remove, while still leaving XSEC-1 (provider impersonation) open until the flag flips. **Concrete contract:** when a stranger connects with no `auth.provider_token`, the coordinator (1) admits them as today through SPEC-002's existing provisional path, (2) mints a fresh token via `auth.Store.IssueToken(provider_id, hostname)` keyed to the `provider_id` the stranger declared in `hello` / `auth_request` initial, (3) returns the token in both the v1 `hello_ack` and v2 `auth_response` frames under a new optional field `assigned_provider_token`, exactly once per connect. The phase3-binary persists the returned token to `macprovider.yaml` (atomic-rename, 0600) before any reconnect; next connection carries it as `Authorization: Bearer`. `install.sh` is untouched — the bootstrap stays a single shell pipe. **Pre-flight finding that drove dual-path:** `phase3-binary/Sources/macprovider-cli/CoordinatorClient.swift:349` shows the binary's default first frame is v1 `hello`, not v2 `auth_request` — v2 is tier-2 gated. So strangers running curl|bash today land on the v1 `hello → hello_ack` path. Mirroring `assigned_provider_token` onto both ack frames is the only way the actual target population (provisional strangers) receives it. App-layer envelope on v2 still helps the opt-in v2 cohort; v1 stays TLS-only, which is the wire-security baseline today. **Trust semantics:** the provisional token is NOT a trust signal — any stranger can mint one — but it IS an identity-binding signal. After first admission, no second machine can claim the same `provider_id` without that token. This is exactly the XSEC-1 fix at provisional tier. Pinned-tier tokens remain operator-issued via `coordinator-cli issue-token` (no change to M1-1's existing path). **Multi-mint by design:** the coordinator mints a fresh row in `provider_tokens` on every tokenless provisional admission (no in-flight dedup) so a failed persist on the binary side always self-heals on next connect. Operators MAY prune unused tokens via existing admin tooling — `last_used_at` makes pruning safe. | **The flag-flip gate is now liftable end-to-end.** Sequencing before the flip: ship coordinator + new binary release → install.sh's pinned tag bumps to a binary that parses `assigned_provider_token` → existing provisional providers reconnect and self-mint → after a settling period (24-48h), flip `require_provider_tokens=true`. Reconnect storm at flip = zero: tokenless providers connect → coordinator mints + returns → binary persists + reconnects with token. Old v1.2.x binaries that can't parse `assigned_provider_token` will fail at flip-time (compatibility cutoff) — same blast radius as the original M1-1 plan, no worse, and Entry 59 already framed the flip as the cutoff. | **Implementation lands as branch `feat/m1-1-self-serve-provisional-tokens` against `origin/main`** carrying the SPEC-003 amendment to v0.8 (normative `assigned_provider_token` field on both `hello_ack` and `auth_response` initial-stage; coordinator MUST mint on first tokenless provisional admission; binary MUST persist atomically with 0600 perms), the coordinator-side mint-on-admit code path (`phase4-coordinator/internal/ws/server.go` v1 + v2 ack-write sites + `internal/ws/messages.go` field add), the phase3-binary persist-and-reload path (`CoordinatorClient.swift` ack handler + `Config.swift` writer with atomic rename + 0600 perms), and regression tests on both modules. After merge, the full flag-flip choreography becomes safe even with provisional strangers in the pool. **Production gating remains operator-driven:** the new binary needs to be cut as a release tag and `install.sh`'s GitHub Releases API call needs to resolve to it before any tokenless provider can self-mint; the existing v1.3.0 binary cohort (including the live `air5` provider) will need to reconnect on the new binary version before the flag flip is safe. |

| 2026-06-11 | **Entry 62 — PR #44 focused security re-audit on commit 4b1c527: 0 CRITICAL, 1 MAJOR, 3 MINOR, 2 NIT; all fixed in fix-pass 2.** Codex security-reviewer focused pass on the audit-fix commit (the v0.8.1 hardening from Entry 61) caught two predicted issues plus one cleanup I'd missed. **MAJOR-1 (predicted): TOCTOU race in v0.8.1's TOFU implementation.** The Entry 61 fix used `HasActiveTokenForProvider` (SELECT) then `IssueToken` (INSERT) as separate operations; two concurrent tokenless connects for the same `provider_id` could both pass the SELECT before either INSERT committed, both mint valid bearers, defeating the entire point of the v0.8.1 TOFU rewrite. SQLite WAL serializes the writes but not retroactively the reads. v0.8.2 fix: partial unique index `idx_provider_tokens_one_active_per_provider ON provider_tokens(provider_id) WHERE revoked_at IS NULL` enforces the invariant at the DB layer. `IssueToken` returns `auth.ErrActiveTokenAlreadyExists` on constraint violation; `resolveProvisionalToken` maps it to TOFU close. The migration step revokes pre-existing duplicate active rows so the index installs cleanly on legacy DBs. Regression test: `TestIssueTokenRefusesConcurrentDuplicateForSameProviderID` races 16 goroutines for the same `provider_id` and asserts exactly one succeeds. **MINOR-1 (predicted): detached-persist brick mode.** v0.8.1 fired persist as `Task.detached` while adopting in-memory immediately; a process crash or SIGKILL between adoption and persist completion stranded the token coordinator-side and TOFU-rejected the binary on next restart. v0.8.2 fix: AWAIT the detached persist before in-memory adoption (`await Task.detached(...).value`). Disk I/O still off the receive loop (Task is detached), but the in-memory token only becomes live after `rename(2)` returns. On persist failure, the in-memory token is NOT adopted; the asymmetry "coordinator has token, binary doesn't" never appears in this design. **MINOR-2: `prune-tokens` operator footgun.** A self-minted token is `last_used_at IS NULL` until the binary reconnects with Bearer (seconds after persist completes). A confused operator running `--apply --older-than 0s` during a settling window would brick the active first-session provider. v0.8.2 fix: refuse cutoffs younger than 24h without `--force`; dry-run lists candidate `provider_id` + `token_prefix` + `created_at` for sanity check. **MINOR-3 (missed in v0.8.1): SPEC-003 still contained pre-TOFU "multi-mint by design" passages in FR-C9.1 + FR-C9.4 prose, contradicting the new TOFU contract.** Spec text drift like this can cause future implementers to reintroduce the credential-capture behavior. v0.8.2 rewrote the contradictory passages. **NIT-1 + NIT-2** (TOFU-DB-error metric gap; stale `auth.provider_token` comment in messages.go:35-38) — NIT-2 fixed; NIT-1 deferred as a metric/observability improvement that doesn't change security posture. **SPEC-003 bumped v0.8.1 → v0.8.2** with new normative DB-uniqueness requirement in FR-C9.1, AWAIT-persist contract in FR-C9.3, and prune-cutoff floor in operator runbook prose. | **Methodology compounds: the re-audit caught what the first three auditors didn't see, because the first three reviewed v0.8 code and the audit-fix introduced ~580 LOC of NEW code paths that those three audits did NOT cover.** The TOCTOU race lived in code that ONLY existed after the v0.8.1 fix landed. The detached-persist brick mode lived in the v0.8.1 fix's response to architect MAJOR-2 (non-blocking persist). Both findings demonstrate that "fixing audit findings" is itself security-sensitive new code that warrants its own audit. The audit-fix-of-the-audit-fix pattern (Entry 62 fixing Entry 61's fixes) is the cost of moving fast on security; the alternative is to land each round in a separate PR and audit each PR independently, which is slower but provides cleaner per-PR audit history. For PR #44 the choice was speed; for future security-sensitive work the trade should be made deliberately. | **Coordinator changes (Go):** `auth/tokens.go` (+`ensureActiveProviderIDUniqueness` migration, +`ErrActiveTokenAlreadyExists` sentinel, +`isActiveProviderTokenConstraintFailure` helper, IssueToken maps UNIQUE failure → sentinel), `ws/server.go` (resolveProvisionalToken maps sentinel → RejectTOFU, parameter renamed authState to disambiguate from auth package), `ws/messages.go` (HelloAck comment NIT fix), `cmd/coordinator-cli/main.go` (prune-tokens `--force` flag + 24h cutoff floor + verbose dry-run output), `internal/auth/tokens_test.go` (+`TestIssueTokenRefusesConcurrentDuplicateForSameProviderID`, +`TestIssueTokenMigrationRevokesPreexistingDuplicates`). **Binary changes (Swift):** `macprovider-cli/CoordinatorClient.swift` (`adoptAssignedProviderTokenIfPresent` is now async, awaits detached persist before in-memory adoption, fail-safe on persist error). **Spec:** SPEC-003 v0.8.2 change-log + FR-C9.1 DB-uniqueness normative paragraph + FR-C9.3 AWAIT-persist rewrite + FR-C9.5 dependency notes. **Operational follow-up before deploy** (carried from Entry 61): `go1.26.4+` for stdlib advisories; cut new binary release tag; FR-C9.5 settling window. |

| 2026-06-11 | **Entry 61 — PR #44 codex three-auditor pass: 0 CRITICAL, 4 MAJOR, 2 MINOR, 1 QUESTION; all fixed in-branch.** Three independent codex sessions (code-reviewer, security-reviewer, architect, `omc ask codex --agent-prompt <role>`) reviewed `feat/m1-1-self-serve-provisional-tokens` after the v0.8 implementation landed. No CRITICAL findings — XSEC-1 closure intent verified. The four MAJORs were unanimously load-bearing and fixed inside the same branch rather than in a follow-up PR. **(M1) Security: tokenless repeated self-mint allowed credential capture.** Attacker declares a victim's `provider_id` on a tokenless connect, coordinator mints a parallel valid bearer, after flag-flip the attacker authenticates as the victim. Pre-fix multi-mint (FR-C9.4) made this free. Fix: TOFU via new `Store.HasActiveTokenForProvider`; refuse second mint with `CloseInvalidToken / "invalid_token"`. FR-C9.4 rewritten from "multi-mint covers self-heal" to "TOFU; persist-failure recovery requires operator revoke + retry." Trade explicit: closed credential-capture at the cost of automatic self-heal; operator labor in the rare persist-fail case beats credential exfiltration in the common attack case. **(M2) Code: indented `provider_token:` (nested under `auth:`) clobbered to top-level by the persist helper.** Pre-fix used `trimmingCharacters(.whitespaces).hasPrefix(...)`. Fix: bare `line.hasPrefix("provider_token:")` — column-0 only. **(M3) Code: only first duplicate `provider_token:` line replaced; later duplicates survived.** Fix: replace ALL matching top-level lines; collapse to one canonical entry. **(M4) Architect: persist violated FR-C9.3 "MUST NOT block WS receive loop."** Pre-fix called the synchronous persist inline in `acceptCoordinatorSession`. Fix: detached `Task.detached(priority: .utility)` offloads disk I/O off the receive loop; in-memory adoption is immediate so the next reconnect benefits even before flush. FR-C9.3 wording relaxed to SHOULD-not-block with implementation guidance. **MINORs fixed:** (a) JSON log line in `CoordinatorClient.adoptAssignedProviderTokenIfPresent` was hand-built via string interpolation — now uses `JSONSerialization` so embedded quotes/newlines in errors or paths cannot break the envelope; all three auditors flagged this independently. (b) SPEC-003 prose mismatched code on the persist target key (`auth.provider_token` vs flat `provider_token`); SPEC-001 + ConfigLoader are flat, fixed SPEC-003 prose everywhere to match. (c) `TokenValidator` interface widened in v0.8 to include `IssueToken` (validate + issue mixed); architect MINOR-2 — now split into `TokenValidator` + `TokenIssuer`, server holds both fields, production wires the same `*auth.Store` to both, the seam is at the interface layer. (d) Multi-mint had no normative prune story — added `coordinator-cli prune-tokens [--older-than 168h] [--apply]` with dry-run default; `Store.PruneUnusedTokens` cleans `last_used_at IS NULL AND revoked_at IS NULL AND created_at < cutoff`. (e) v2 `auth_response` path had no regression test (only v1 `hello_ack` covered) — added `TestSelfServeProvisionalTokenMintedOnAuthResponseV2` exercising full ECDH handshake. **QUESTION** (dead-code provisional-name synthesis branch) removed. **govulncheck** flagged Go stdlib advisories `GO-2026-5039` + `GO-2026-5037` (fixed in `go1.26.4`); operational follow-up — build env upgrade required before deploy, not a code change. **SPEC-003 bumped v0.8 → v0.8.1** with FR-C9.3 wording relaxation, FR-C9.4 TOFU rewrite, FR-C9.5 explicit supersede of locked SPEC-002 v1.3.5 FR-P12 / PG-1 for tokenless provisional under `require_provider_tokens=true`. | **The methodology lesson generalizes:** three-auditor cross-model review on a security-sensitive PR caught real bugs that single-author + single-reviewer would have shipped. The credential-capture exploit (MAJOR-1) was not obvious from the v0.8 design — multi-mint sounded like a good idea ("self-heal"), but the security lens reframed it as "free credential capture for any declared provider_id." Two independent reviewers (code + architect) also caught the spec/code key drift, and all three flagged the JSON log injection, which matches the `audit-two-model-finding-rate` memory: the Nth reviewer is not redundant. | **Coordinator changes (Go):** `auth/tokens.go` (+`HasActiveTokenForProvider`, +`PruneUnusedTokens`), `ws/server.go` (TokenValidator/TokenIssuer split, `resolveProvisionalToken` with TOFU, dead-code removed, both ack-write sites updated), `cmd/coordinator/main.go` (wire `WithTokenIssuer`), `cmd/coordinator-cli/main.go` (`prune-tokens` subcommand), `ws/provider_token_self_serve_test.go` (+TOFU rejection test, +v2 auth_response mint test). **Binary changes (Swift):** `MacProviderCore/ProviderTokenPersist.swift` (top-level-only match, replace-all-duplicates), `macprovider-cli/CoordinatorClient.swift` (detached persist Task, JSON-encoded structured log via `JSONSerialization`), `Tests/.../ProviderTokenPersistTests.swift` (+nested-key preserve test, +duplicate-line replace-all test, +comment-line preserve test). **Spec:** SPEC-003 v0.8.1 change-log + FR-C9.3/FR-C9.4/FR-C9.5 normative rewrites. **Operational follow-up before deploy:** build coordinator + binary with `go1.26.4+`; cut a new `macprovider-cli` release tag carrying the binary-side persist code so `install.sh` resolves to it; only then begin the FR-C9.5 settling window. |

| 2026-06-12 | **Entry 67 — SPEC-003 v0.8.3: FR-C9.4 unused-token self-heal closes the deploy-gap lockout class.** First end-to-end M0-5/M1-6 production deploy on Pearl (2026-06-12, v1.3.0-24-g87b3a6b → v1.3.1-5-gba04cd4) exposed a concrete cost of v0.8.1's blanket TOFU policy: `air5` connected to the v1.3.0 coordinator under `auth.require_provider_tokens=false`, the coordinator restarted to v1.3.1-5 and minted a self-serve token during admission per FR-C9.1, but `air5`'s old binary did not consume the ack-frame's `assigned_provider_token` (the binary on disk did not understand the v0.8 field). On reconnect the binary sent tokenless and v0.8.2's strict TOFU rejected it indefinitely; the pool dropped to 0 ready providers until operator intervention. **The amendment:** when the active row's `last_used_at IS NULL`, the coordinator MUST revoke that row atomically and mint a fresh token instead of rejecting. When `last_used_at IS NOT NULL`, the strict-reject path is preserved unchanged. **Security analysis:** the codex MAJOR-1 credential-capture vector v0.8.1 closed requires an attacker to authenticate with the harvested bearer at least once — which sets `last_used_at`. A token that has never been used CANNOT have been used by an attacker; revoking-and-reminting an unused row gives the attacker no advantage that the original tokenless connect did not already give them, and gives the legitimate provider a clean self-heal path. **The trade-off** (originally captured in Entry 61, refined here): v0.8.1 chose "close credential capture at the cost of operator labor in the rare persist-failure case." That formulation assumed persist-failure was rare. It is not — every routine coordinator restart while a v0.8-unaware binary is connected reproduces the failure mode. v0.8.3 retains the strict-reject for the genuinely-vulnerable shape (used token + tokenless reconnect — capture-then-reset attack signature) and self-heals the deploy-gap shape (unused token + tokenless reconnect — never-authenticated identity, no live credential to protect). | **Race safety:** the unused-token revoke is implemented as `auth.Store.RevokeUnusedTokenForProvider` inside a `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction over `UPDATE … WHERE provider_id = ? AND revoked_at IS NULL AND last_used_at IS NULL`. The partial unique index `idx_provider_tokens_one_active_per_provider` (v0.8.2) is the second-line defense: even if two concurrent tokenless reconnects both revoke-and-attempt-to-mint, the index traps the duplicate INSERT and the loser follows the existing `ErrActiveTokenAlreadyExists` → reject path. The single-row guarantee from the partial index is preserved. **Operator-facing log lines distinguish the two paths** so alerting infrastructure can keep firing on `fr_c9_4_tofu_reject` (the strict path that warrants human review) without noise from the self-heal `fr_c9_4_self_heal` (which is by-design recovery). | **The recovery runbook narrows but does not disappear.** `coordinator-cli revoke-token` is still the answer for the genuinely-rare used-token-persist-failure case (manual config edit removing a token that had been validating, disk corruption mid-write, etc.) where the operator IS the trust signal distinguishing legitimate-provider-from-attacker. The 2026-06-12 deploy-gap incident on `air5` was operationally handled with a one-off sqlite3 UPDATE before this amendment was implemented; with v0.8.3 the same incident self-heals before the operator notices. SPEC-003 amended in line; FR-C9.4 contract table now lists both branches normatively. |

Pure row existence is NOT operational evidence under the v0.8.3 contract. <br><br> **Code MAJOR-1 + architect MAJOR-3 — stale prose:** "TOFU close path" / "MUST treat this as a TOFU rejection" comments in `tokens.go:22, :220, :239` and `server.go:94-104, :406-432` + Entry 63 vs Entry 66 references throughout SPEC-003 + the test file. | 2026-06-12 | **Entry 76 — PR #69 merge-time composition with PR #78 + fix-pass-5 audit closure: SPEC-003 v0.8.4 reconciles two parallel v0.8.3 drafts of FR-C9.4.** *(Renumbered through 71 → 76 because main claimed 71 twice during fix-pass-5 — M3-1 batched DELETEs and M3-8d tier2 catalog DI both labelled themselves Entry 71.)* <br><br> **fix-pass-5 closures (codex three-auditor pass on the v0.8.4 composition):** <br> – Atomic `auth.Store.ValidateAndMarkTokenUsed` closes the ValidateToken+MarkTokenUsed TOCTOU window (code MAJOR-1). <br> – `pool.AuthMintFailed` enum + fail-closed RejectTOFU on transient IssueToken DB errors closes the empty-AuthState DoS-amplification vector (security MAJOR-1). <br> – Extended `pool.Registry.Register` eviction defense refuses non-Bearer-validated replacement of an existing routable Bearer-validated session (security MAJOR-2 + architect Steelman). <br> – SPEC-003 §FR-C9.4(b) rewrite, doc-comment refresh, eviction-defense test renamed + two new direct registry tests. <br><br> <br><br> PR #69 (started 2026-06-11, six revisions) and PR #78 (merged 2026-06-12) independently labeled themselves "SPEC-003 v0.8.3" while implementing different responses to the same deploy-gap lockout class on the `air5` reconnect failure. PR #78 chose **self-heal-on-NULL** (revoke the unused row, mint fresh, admit routable) — merged to `main` first, became canonical. PR #69 chose **admit-tokenless-with-quarantine-marker** plus an `AuthState` eligibility primitive + pool-registry eviction defense + buyer-side `RoutingEligible()` single-authority refactor. <br><br> **Resolution: not "pick one"; compose them.** The two PRs address orthogonal failure modes within the same conditional surface. PR #78's table answers "what does the WIRE CONTRACT do per `last_used_at` state?"; PR #69's primitives answer "what does the AUTH SUBSYSTEM treat as routing-eligible, billing-eligible, eviction-eligible?". Both are needed: self-heal alone is silently routable under a race-loss-after-self-heal-passed (the `ErrActiveTokenAlreadyExists` branch), and the AuthState/eviction-defense primitives alone do not fix the deploy-gap lockout. <br><br> **The composed v0.8.4 contract** (FR-C9.4 normative table updated): `NULL` last_used_at → self-heal (PR #78); `NOT NULL` last_used_at → strict TOFU reject (PR #78 ≈ v0.8.1); DB error → fail closed (PR #78); IssueToken race-loss after self-heal/no-row passed → admit-bearer-less-quarantined with `AuthBearerlessDuplicate` marking (PR #69). The `TokenIssuer` interface keeps BOTH `HasActiveTokenForProvider` (PR #78 disambiguation step) and `RevokeUnusedTokenForProvider` (PR #78 self-heal step). `pool.AuthState` enum + `RoutingEligible()` single authority + `Register` eviction defense ship as PR #69's contribution. v0.8.4's `RoutingEligible()` scope correction (HashStatus filtering removed; config-aware Tier-2 logic owns it) ships from PR #69 fix-pass-4. <br><br> **Three distinct log events** distinguish the paths for post-incident analysis: `fr_c9_4_self_heal` (deploy-gap recovery), `fr_c9_4_tofu_reject` (credential-capture closure), `fr_c9_4_race_loss_admit_quarantined` (concurrent-attacker quarantine). <br><br> **Test reconciliation:** keep main's `TestSelfServeProvisionalTokenSelfHealsWhenExistingTokenUnused` + `TestSelfServeProvisionalTokenRejectedWhenExistingTokenUsed`. Keep PR #69's `TestSelfServeProvisionalTokenEvictionDefenseProtectsRoutableSession` (with fix-pass-4 positive `AuthBearerValidated` assertion) — but note this test now exercises the strict-reject path under composition (`MarkTokenUsed` fires on the legitimate Bearer connect, so the attacker hits TOFU reject before ever reaching the eviction-defense layer). Replace PR #69's v2 admit-tokenless tests with `TestSelfServeProvisionalTokenSelfHealsOnAuthResponseV2WithRetentionCleanup` — v2 mirror of self-heal that also locks the SPEC-010 retention defer's terminal-path release. Keep all PR #69 buyer regression tests (orthogonal to PR #78's surface): `TestChatCompletionsExcludesAuthBearerlessDuplicate`, `TestChatCompletionsPinnedHeaderRejectsAuthBearerlessDuplicate`, `TestModelsExcludesAuthBearerlessDuplicateFromCapacity`. <br><br> **Pool-registry eviction defense (PR #69) is now defense-in-depth.** Under composition it fires only on the race-loss admit-quarantined path; the strict-reject path closes the connection before any Register call. The defense protects against the theoretical case where a self-mint races a legitimate self-mint (both `AuthSelfMinted`, both routable) — eviction can still occur there per last-writer-wins. Tracking issue #82 captures the architect's followup items (Phase 5 gateway aggregation, `AuthMintFailed` enum, `coordinator-cli pre-flip-audit` CLI, explorer `auth_state`) plus a new entry for the AuthSelfMinted-vs-AuthSelfMinted race protection question. | **The audit-cycle pattern recognized in Entry 70 applies to this composition too — but at a different layer.** <br><br> Entry 70's lesson: "each scope-narrowed fix creates the next bug class." This merge surfaces a complementary lesson: **two independent fixes for the same operational pain can both be correct in their own frame and compose without conflict if they're addressing orthogonal failure modes.** PR #78's frame: "what should the WIRE CONTRACT do?". PR #69's frame: "what should the AUTH SUBSYSTEM treat as routable?". Different lenses, both right, no contradiction. <br><br> **The cost paid by parallel work without rebase:** ~30 minutes of merge-resolution + spec composition + a renumbering. The COST AVOIDED by parallel work: PR #78 shipped its self-heal recovery on Pearl while PR #69 was still in its three-auditor cycle. Operators had self-heal in production for several hours before PR #69 finished its security-fix work. Parallel correctness > serialized speed, in this case. <br><br> **Discipline reaffirmed:** the auditor cycle on PR #69 was the right intervention for PR #69's failure modes; PR #78's audit cycle (lighter, single-reviewer) was right for its narrower scope. The composition at merge time was inexpensive because both PRs converged on the right *primitives*; the integration was about layering them, not picking between them. Future spec changes should consider whether parallel work is composable upfront — the answer for FR-C9.4 here was yes by accident; future explicit "design for composition" would catch this earlier. | **Coordinator changes (Go):** all PR #69 code preserved (buyer/server.go + pool/provider.go + ws/server.go) + main's PR #78 code preserved (auth/tokens.go RevokeUnusedTokenForProvider). <br><br> **`resolveProvisionalToken`** rewritten as the composition: 3-tuple return `(outcome, token, AuthState)` from PR #69 + flow `RevokeUnusedTokenForProvider → HasActiveTokenForProvider → IssueToken → race-loss admit-quarantined` from PR #78 + PR #69 ErrActiveTokenAlreadyExists handling. <br><br> **`TokenIssuer` interface** keeps both `HasActiveTokenForProvider` (PR #78) and `RevokeUnusedTokenForProvider` (PR #78). PR #69's interface narrowing reverted. <br><br> **`pool.Provider.RoutingEligible()` scope correction** preserved from fix-pass-4 — HashStatus filtering removed; the predicate is exclusively credential trust + slot availability. <br><br> **SPEC-003 → v0.8.4** with FR-C9.1 + FR-C9.4 composed normative bodies; v0.8.3 changelog entries from both PRs preserved as historical notes. <br><br> **Tests:** keep PR #78's two v1 tests + PR #69's eviction-defense (with positive lock) + PR #69's three buyer regression tests; replace PR #69's v2 admit-tokenless tests with v2 self-heal mirror. <br><br> **DECISION_CRITERIA:** Entry 67 preserved (PR #78 self-heal, on `main`); Entries 69 + 70 renumbered from 67 + 68 (PR #69 fix-pass-3 + fix-pass-4); Entry 71 added (this composition note). <br><br> **Pearl operational state unchanged** (still on v1.3.0-24 rollback; 2 orphan tokens still in DB awaiting future revoke-before-redeploy). |

| 2026-06-12 | **Entry 66 — v1.3.1 Pearl deploy failed + rolled back; SPEC-003 v0.8.3 replaces hard-TOFU reject with settling-window admit-tokenless.** Operator-driven deploy of the v1.3.1 coordinator (FR-C9 self-serve provisional tokens) and v1.3.1 binary to Pearl unwound through three cascading failures, each surfacing a structural gap that Entries 60-62 didn't anticipate. **(1) Pre-flight: macOS 26.3.1 launchd AMFI tightening.** Operator's own Mac (Augustass-MacBook-Air, macOS 26.3.1) could not load the v1.3.1 binary under `launchctl bootstrap`: AMFI rejects adhoc-signed binaries with "no CMS blob, Unrecoverable CT signature issue, bailing out." Earlier macOS versions accepted adhoc-signed binaries; 26.3.1 does not. Standalone shell execution still works (kernel execve is more permissive than launchd bootstrap), but the launchd service install fails generically with `5: Input/output error`. Discovery captured in memory `macprovider-launchd-amfi-blocker-macos-26`; fix is release-pipeline Developer ID signing + notarization, filed as PR #62 with the workflow steps conditional on operator-supplied Apple Developer secrets. **(2) Mid-deploy: duplicate `limit_req_zone ws_provider_rate` in nginx.** M1-4 (PR #39) added the zone declaration to `phase4-coordinator/dist/nginx-coordinator.streamvc.live.conf:18-19` without checking that `api.streamvc.live` vhost already declared identical zones (nginx requires zone names unique across `http {}` context). `nginx -t` failed during the new TLS site install at deploy step 6b. Hot-patched live conf (sed-comment both lines; references later in the conf continue to resolve against api's shared declaration) and the deploy completed. Source-of-truth fix still TODO as a separate follow-up. **(3) Post-deploy: v0.8.2 hard-TOFU bricked the existing pool.** The deploy script restarted the coordinator at 13:55Z; air5 + air8gb reconnected tokenless within 5 minutes, each self-minted a token (DB rows `372c3372023e` for air5, `d630849e8acb` for air8gb), and the cleartext bearers were returned in their `hello_ack` frames. **But both providers were running v1.2.5 binaries that cannot persist `assigned_provider_token`** — Swift's JSON decoder silently drops unknown keys. The minute either provider disconnected (air8gb did at 22:47:43Z), the next tokenless reconnect hit the v0.8.2 hard-TOFU gate (`HasActiveTokenForProvider` returns true → `CloseInvalidToken / "invalid_token"`) and was permanently bricked. air8gb stayed offline; air5 was racing toward the same fate. Coordinator rolled back to v1.3.0-24 via `coordinator.prev` swap at 22:49Z; both providers recovered on next reconnect (v1.3.0-24 has no TOFU enforcement). The two orphan tokens are harmless under the old binary but MUST be revoked before any future v1.3.1 redeploy. **The structural defect:** v0.8.1's TOFU contract (Entry 61) and v0.8.2's TOCTOU-safe partial unique index (Entry 62) BOTH assume the wire-level reject is acceptable. Neither caught that during the settling window — when `RequireProviderTokens=false` AND old binaries are still in the pool — the wire-level reject IS the brick. Auditors evaluated the security model (correctly: TOFU does close credential capture) but did not stress-test the deployment plan against the "existing tokenless providers cannot persist tokens" constraint. **v0.8.3 fix: admit-tokenless on duplicate during settling.** `resolveProvisionalToken` now maps `ErrActiveTokenAlreadyExists` to `provisionalTokenSkip` (admit without an ack token) instead of `provisionalTokenRejectTOFU` (close). The `HasActiveTokenForProvider` SELECT call is removed (it was a TOCTOU-racy belt for the unique-index suspenders). The `provisionalTokenRejectTOFU` enum value and the v1/v2 ack-write close-on-reject paths are deleted. The credential-capture closure remains: the partial unique index still prevents minting a parallel bearer for someone else's `provider_id`, so whoever races first owns the bearer. Subsequent tokenless connects for the same `provider_id` are admitted bearer-less, surfacing the operator-action need at flag-flip time via `coordinator-cli list-tokens` audit. Regression test `TestSelfServeProvisionalTokenRejectedWhenActiveTokenExists` renamed and rewritten to `TestSelfServeProvisionalTokenAdmitsTokenlessWhenActiveTokenExists` — asserts hello_ack received (not close), `assigned_provider_token == ""`, exactly one active row in DB. **SPEC-003 → v0.8.3** with FR-C9.1 mint-conditional-on-DB-constraint rewrite + FR-C9.4 admit-tokenless contract + explicit operator runbook for flag-flip audit. | **The methodology compounds in the deploy lane too.** Three rounds of auditors caught security issues, then a fourth round of focused security audit caught the audit-fix's own issues, and yet none of the four caught the deploy-lane brick because the failure mode lived in the COMBINATION of (a) v0.8.2 hard-TOFU and (b) old binaries silently dropping the new ack field. The fix is a structural recognition: deployment plans for security-sensitive contract changes need a separate adversarial review against "what existing field consumers in the wild do" — not just "what the spec says they should do." For PR #44 the audit lens was correct (security model) but the deployment lens was missing. Future security-sensitive PRs should include a deployment-rehearsal step before merging into a release tag: build the binary, deploy to a non-prod target with a known-old client population, and verify that the disconnect-reconnect cycle survives. | **Coordinator changes (Go):** `phase4-coordinator/internal/ws/server.go` (`resolveProvisionalToken` rewritten to admit-tokenless on duplicate; pre-INSERT `HasActiveTokenForProvider` SELECT removed; `provisionalTokenRejectTOFU` constant deleted; v1/v2 ack-write close-on-reject branches deleted). **Tests:** `internal/ws/provider_token_self_serve_test.go` rewrite of TOFU test to assert admit-tokenless. **Spec:** SPEC-003 → v0.8.3 with FR-C9.1 + FR-C9.4 normative rewrites. **Pearl state at session close (2026-06-12):** coordinator on `v1.3.0-24-g87b3a6b` (rolled back), pool restored (air5 + air8gb on v1.2.5 binaries); 2 orphan tokens in DB. **Operator follow-ups before next deploy attempt:** (1) merge PR #62 (Developer ID signing + notarization, conditional on Apple Developer secrets); (2) operator-side: enroll in Apple Developer Program ($99/yr), generate Developer ID cert, populate the 5 new GitHub secrets per `phase3-binary/dist/release-signing-runbook.md`; (3) merge PR (this commit) for v0.8.3 admit-tokenless; (4) cut v1.3.2 release tag (will produce a signed + notarized + stapled binary); (5) revoke the two orphan tokens (`coordinator-cli revoke-token --token-prefix 372c3372023e` for air5; `--token-prefix d630849e8acb` for air8gb); (6) fix nginx duplicate-zone source-of-truth in `phase4-coordinator/dist/nginx-coordinator.streamvc.live.conf:18-19`; (7) redeploy coordinator + verify both providers reconnect with bearer-less sessions during settling; (8) coordinate provider upgrades to v1.3.2 binaries (now signed, will install under launchd cleanly); (9) flag-flip audit + revoke per FR-C9.4 v0.8.3 runbook. |

(1) `connectAndRunLegacy` (line 341) contained an `if wsTunneledMode { InferenceRelay(...) } else { inferenceRelay = nil }` block (lines 343-357); the `true`-branch is provably unreachable because `connectAndRun()` (lines 288-294) only dispatches to `connectAndRunLegacy()` when `wsTunneledMode == false` — the single callsite verified by `grep -n connectAndRunLegacy` returning one hit at line 292, inside the `else` branch of `if wsTunneledMode`. The dead 12-line block was replaced by the unconditional `inferenceRelay = nil` with comment `// endpoint_url legacy mode — no relay needed.` (2) `connectAndRunTier2` (line 314) wrapped `receiveAuthChallenge` in a `do { ... } catch { throw error }` rethrow (lines 318-320) that added no handling; collapsed to a direct `try await` assignment. `swift build` clean; `swift test --parallel` 172 tests passed. | **Dead code imposes a disproportionate maintenance cost because readers must trace all the conditions proving it is dead before trusting that adjacent live code is correct; deleting it now means future readers of `connectAndRunLegacy` are not slowed by a large `if wsTunneledMode` branch that can never execute.** | No follow-up needed; the deletion is a pure simplification with zero behavior change confirmed by both static analysis (single-callsite guarantee) and the full test suite. |
| 2026-06-12 | **Entry 70 — M3-8c / CODE-6: `fmt.Sscanf`/`Sprintf` JSON serializer for `capacity_tier` replaced with `encoding/json` in `phase5-gateway/internal/storage/sqlite/store.go`.** Introduced a package-private `capacityTierDTO` struct (`json:"tier"` + `json:"signals"`) matching the KillSwitchState pattern already used by the sibling `GetKillSwitch`/`SetKillSwitch` functions in the same file. `GetCapacityTier` now scans the DB row into a `[]byte` and calls `json.Unmarshal`; `SetCapacityTier` calls `json.Marshal(capacityTierDTO{...})`. The on-disk JSON shape `{"tier":N,"signals":"..."}` is preserved byte-for-byte for signals that contain no JSON-special characters, ensuring existing rows decode cleanly. A new `TestCapacityTierRoundTripSpecialChars` test covers round-trips through backslashes, double quotes, control characters (`\n`, `\t`), and Unicode (`™`); the test comment states "Added because the old Sscanf %q format would have failed on these inputs." `go build ./...`, `go test ./internal/storage/sqlite/... -count=2 -race`, and `go vet ./...` all clean. | **The hand-rolled `fmt.Sscanf`/`Sprintf` JSON serializer at `store.go:767,778` was the sibling inconsistency identified in audit finding CODE-6: "Hand-rolled `fmt.Sscanf`/`Sprintf` JSON serializer for capacity tier where the sibling kill-switch correctly uses `encoding/json`."** The risk is forward-compatibility: if any future code path writes the row via `json.Marshal` (which produces `\uXXXX` escapes and may add fields), `fmt.Sscanf` with a hardcoded format string would fail; using `json.Unmarshal` handles all valid JSON including alternative escape forms. The test documents the class of inputs that the old path mishandled. | The encoded shape must remain `{"tier":N,"signals":"..."}` (no field reorder, no added fields) so the existing 7-day-retention reaper and any admin tooling reading `runtime_config.value` raw does not need a migration. `json.Marshal` on `capacityTierDTO` produces exactly this shape because Go's `encoding/json` sorts struct fields by their JSON tag name alphabetically and `signals` > `tier` alphabetically — wait, `s` < `t`, so `signals` comes before `tier` — however the struct field order is `Tier` then `Signals` and `encoding/json` uses declaration order for structs (not alphabetical), so the output is `{"tier":N,"signals":"..."}` matching the old format exactly. Verified by running `go test -v -run TestCapacityTierRoundTripSpecialChars` with a raw-SQL read of the stored value to confirm field order. |
| 2026-06-12 | **Entry 71 — M3-1 / PERF-3: batched retention DELETEs (Option A — `julianday()` retained).** Each of the three pruners (`requestlog.PruneBefore` for `request_log` + `request_idempotency_keys`; `audit.PruneBefore` for `audit_log`) now loops a 5,000-row capped DELETE until a short batch returns, keeping the SQLite write lock short while billing's 6s hot path shares the handle. The `julianday(ts_utc) < julianday(?)` predicate is preserved (Option A): every INSERT in these packages writes `time.RFC3339Nano`, which strips trailing zeros in the fractional seconds, so `"…00Z"` and `"…00.000000001Z"` mix variable widths and `.` (0x2E) sorts before `Z` (0x5A) — direct lexicographic comparison would be subtly wrong. Normalizing every `RFC3339Nano` write to a fixed-width format is a >50-LOC change touching billing money-path tables (`ledger_request_credits`, `ledger_operator_credits`, settlement/recovery/endpoints query sites) and was deferred. EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN confirms the new DELETE shape (`WHERE rowid IN (SELECT rowid FROM t WHERE julianday(…) < julianday(?) LIMIT ?)`) references `idx_request_log_ts_utc` / `idx_audit_log_ts_utc`. Tests added: 12,000-row cross-batch correctness, concurrent-insert no-SQLITE_BUSY, EXPLAIN-plan index pin. `go test ./internal/requestlog/... ./internal/audit/... -count=2 -race` green; `go vet ./...` clean. | **A single un-LIMIT-ed DELETE on a backlog can hold the write lock for many seconds, blowing past SQLite's `busy_timeout=5000` while billing's 6s hot path waits for it; batching caps each lock-hold to one batch's worth of work without changing prune semantics. Option A keeps the change inside the two stores, off the billing money path.** | Follow-up: normalize every `RFC3339Nano` write in `requestlog/audit/billing` to a fixed-width format (e.g. `Format("2006-01-02T15:04:05.000000000Z07:00")`) and switch every `WHERE ts_utc < ?` / `WHERE julianday(ts_utc) < julianday(?)` site to sargable direct comparison. Today billing already relies on direct lexicographic `<` on these columns (e.g. `settlement.go`, `endpoints.go`) which is a latent variable-width hazard worth closing in the same pass. |

| 2026-06-22 | **Entry 80 — Open Q3 ruling: target tier-2 posture for the 2-person beta remains "defer Part C entirely"; the five enforcement flags stay at their `false` defaults until the team has a buyer-side reason to flip any individual one.** The five flags are `Tier2Config.RequireHashVerified`, `.RequireEncryptedLeg`, `.RequireAttestation`, `.BehavioralSafetyEnabled`, `.EncodingValidationEnabled` in `phase4-coordinator/internal/config/config.go:142-159`; all default to Go zero-value `false`. The audit's M1-6 Part C task was to add `g(coord, "<flag>")` lines to `phase4-coordinator/dist/check-deploy-config.sh` so the deploy gate refuses to start the coordinator if production drops a flag that was supposed to stay on. The operator-instructed answer recorded in `audits/2026-06-10/MILESTONE_1_HANDOFF.md:55-65` during the M1 autopilot was "Defer Part C entirely" — i.e. don't assert any of the five yet. That decision was not previously captured in DECISION_CRITERIA; this entry is the dated record. **What the flags actually do (so the next reader can revisit without re-deriving):** `RequireHashVerified` rejects routing to any provider whose reported `model_hash` is absent or doesn't match the signed catalog at `Tier2Config.CatalogPath` — turning this on prevents weight-substitution attacks but breaks any model that isn't in the catalog yet (the catalog signing script is in `scripts/sign-catalog.go`); `RequireEncryptedLeg` mandates AEAD on the coordinator→provider segment with a periodic rekey (`EncryptedLegRekeyAfterRequests` / `EncryptedLegRekeyAfterSeconds`) — pure defence-in-depth, no buyer-visible behaviour change; `RequireAttestation` demands a valid Apple App Attest receipt from the provider at admission with a freshness bound (`AttestationMaxAgeS`) — gates by hardware/OS integrity, blocks any non-attestable provider (mock attestation is gated separately by `AllowMockAttestation`); `BehavioralSafetyEnabled` enforces `OutputSizeCapBytes` / `OutputBytesPerTokenCeiling` / `ResponseTimeAnomalyFactor` thresholds on every response — catches a misbehaving provider streaming garbage at the wire instead of post-hoc; `EncodingValidationEnabled` checks UTF-8 + JSON-object well-formedness on streaming chunks — catches malformed SSE before it reaches the buyer. | **The 2-person beta cannot afford the friction of a flag that fail-closes on a legitimate scenario the team hasn't yet exercised end-to-end.** The five flags each have a buyer-visible failure mode (catalog misses, attestation outages on macOS minor upgrades, false-positive size caps when a long-form completion is genuinely large, encoding rejections on a model that emits trailing whitespace) and the cost of debugging a "why is my request 503-ing" against the right one of five is meaningfully higher than the marginal security improvement from any individual flag in a 2-provider closed beta. The deferral does NOT mean tier-2 is off — `Tier2Config.ObserveEnabled` and the catalog hash check still run in observation mode; the `model_hash_verified` event logs that hit Pearl journald during air5's connect cycles (`reason:"hash_match"`) are proof the observation half is live. **Flipping any flag is a config change on Pearl `coordinator.yaml`, not a code change** — so this deferral has zero engineering cost to reverse later. The MORE actionable closure for M1-6 Part C is to ship the deploy-gate lines for whichever flag the team WILL turn on next, so deploy assertion drift is impossible by construction. | **Operator follow-ups, in order:** (1) **Revisit trigger — when any of three things change:** (a) the provider pool stops being "2-3 Macs the team can text" (next-tier providers start joining whose hardware/OS hygiene we cannot personally vouch for — `BehavioralSafetyEnabled` + `EncodingValidationEnabled` become high-leverage); (b) the catalog signing pipeline is ergonomic enough that adding a new MLX model takes <10 minutes operator time (`RequireHashVerified` becomes feasible); (c) a buyer with a real privacy posture lands and asks for attested-only routing (`RequireAttestation` becomes a sales-required flag for that customer). Any of those is a reason to revisit; before then the deferral stands. (2) **When ANY flag is turned on,** ship M1-6 Part C in the same PR — add the corresponding `g(coord, "<flag>")` line(s) to `phase4-coordinator/dist/check-deploy-config.sh` so the deploy gate enforces that production keeps the flag on. The flags + the gate ship together or the gate becomes a paper rule. (3) **Q3 is hereby logged as resolved** in DECISION_CRITERIA terms — the deferral is the ruling, this entry is the record. `audits/2026-06-10/REMAINING_WORK.md` "operator-pending item 2" can be updated to reference Entry 80 in lieu of the freestanding deferral note. |

| 2026-06-18 | **Entry 78 — SPEC-013 cli autotune landed via a disciplined 21-round cross-model audit-loop pattern (11 BUILD steps, codex audit between each step, fix-pass until 0 CRITICAL/MAJOR, codex re-audit, lock).** The implementation surface (`phase3-binary/Sources/macprovider-cli/AutotuneCommand.swift`, `AutotuneDB.swift`, `Stage1Iterator.swift`, `Stage2HillClimb.swift`, `RecommendationEmitter.swift`, `RFC8785JCS.swift`, `ConfigApplier.swift`, `AutotuneRuntimeSupport.swift`, `CandidateProviderRunner.swift`, `ProviderConflictDetector.swift`, `ProviderDrainer.swift`, `ProviderPreWarmer.swift`, `HuggingFaceCacheChecker.swift`, `PortProbe.swift`) was built one step at a time. After each codex BUILD commit landed on `feat/cli-autotune-impl`, I authored a step-specific `AUDIT_SPEC_013_IMPL_STEP_N_PROMPT.md`, fired it at codex via `omc ask codex`, applied targeted fix-passes for every finding (1 CRITICAL in Step 6, Step 7, Step 10 caught the LOCKED-file additive-only violations; multiple MAJORs caught contract gaps; MINORs caught test-strength + documentation gaps), then ran a `R<n+1>_PROMPT.md` round to close out. The full audit history lives at `specs/SPEC-013-impl-audit.md` (21 rounds, ~80 KB of step-by-step verdicts). Final state: 393 unit + integration tests passing, 7 skipped (2 baseline + 5 integration/v2-expansion placeholders), 0 failures, NO locked-file regressions across 11 steps. | **The audit-loop discipline earns its cost on money-path code and on architecture work that introduces new abstractions.** Without it, SPEC-013 would have shipped at least: (a) Step 7's E.1 CRITICAL (wrong all-infeasible reason surfaced for AC-17 operator order — a silent recommendation correctness bug), (b) Step 8's D.1 MAJOR (multiple `kept=true` rows breaking the FR-G.1 single-winner invariant), (c) Step 9's G.1 (TOCTOU between fileExists + rename in ConfigApplier backup — operator-visible data-loss surface), (d) Step 10's M.1 CRITICAL (non-additive change to LOCKED Stage1Iterator behavior — would have silently changed Step 7's fallback semantics under operator override). Each of those would have been a real bug shipped to operators or a v2 incompatibility. The cross-model angle matters: codex caught the LOCKED-file violations Claude (the author) missed; Claude caught the AC-17 alternates contract violation that codex's Step 11 BUILD test got wrong. **The 21-round overhead was ~3 wall-clock hours of audit time across one work day** — cheap relative to the money-path correctness it locked in. The pattern composes on top of `/code-review ultra` (which can still run later) — it is NOT redundant; the audit loop runs DURING construction and catches issues before the diff stabilizes for a cross-cutting review. | **Keep doing this for any money-path or architecture-introducing work**: (1) Write SPEC first, audit-loop the SPEC until 0 CRITICAL/MAJOR before opening the SPEC PR (the existing rule per `feedback-spec-audit-loop-before-pr` memory). (2) Author a master BUILD prompt from the locked SPEC, then audit-loop EACH step's implementation in a separate codex session before pushing the impl branch (this entry's pattern, now memorized as `feedback-build-audit-loop`). (3) When a finding catches a LOCKED-file regression, the fix-pass MUST restore the LOCKED semantics; pass new behavior through an additive seam (defaulted init parameter, optional field, etc.) — never reinterpret a LOCKED line. (4) Don't skip ahead to "push and PR open" until the final BUILD step's R2 verdict says READY TO PROCEED TO POST-BUILD. (5) The pattern is NOT for trivial work — a one-line bug fix doesn't need an 11-step BUILD or 21 audit rounds. The break-even is when the change touches >3 files OR introduces a new abstraction OR is money-path correctness. (6) Document deliberate v1 deviations as v0.4 candidates in implementation-notes.html with a concrete fix path (see SPEC-013 AC-17 alternates as a worked example). |
| 2026-06-11 | **Entry 77 — Open Q4 ruling: gateway event-table append-only-forever is a deliberate compliance posture; M2-4 Part C will ship as archive-rotate to cold storage (NOT trigger amendment).** *(Originally drafted as Entry 63 on 2026-06-11; renumbered to 77 on merge because main had claimed 63–76 via parallel-session PRs that landed between draft and merge.)* The 8 gateway event tables (`usage_events`, `feedback_events`, `audit_events`, `api_key_events`, `demo_usage_events`, `capacity_signal_events`, `signup_events`, `demo_session_events`) plus `concurrency_reservations` keep their `RAISE(ABORT)` BEFORE-DELETE triggers in `phase5-gateway/internal/storage/sqlite/migrate.go:184-251` — those are not amended. Disk pressure on Pearl is solved instead by a periodic rotation job that ships the live `gateway.db` file (or rolled-over portion of it) to cold storage (S3-class) on a size or age threshold; the new file starts fresh and the old one becomes a forensic / compliance artifact. Implementation deferred and tracked as a follow-up GitHub issue; the design sketch lives at `audits/2026-06-10/Q4_ARCHIVE_ROTATE_DESIGN.md`. | **The audit's PERF-1 finding is now formally resolved at the policy level — "events are append-only forever" is reaffirmed as the system's tamper-evidence claim, and the disk-pressure problem is handled out-of-band rather than by relaxing the append-only invariant.** This is the safer of the two branches the audit posed: trigger-amendment would have introduced a 'tamper-evidence horizon' that is hard to reason about under audit / compliance review and would require explicit per-table retention-policy decisions for each event class. Archive-rotate, by contrast, preserves the existing invariant ("nothing is ever deleted") and trades only ongoing cold-storage cost (~$0.01/GB/month at current usage). | **Operator follow-ups, in order:** (1) Implementation work tracked in a separate GitHub issue (filed alongside this entry); not blocking any current M2 deploy. The trigger is when `gateway.db` approaches Pearl's `/var/lib` headroom — until then the design doc stands and no code change ships. (2) When implementation lands, OPS.md §3 (gateway restart) gets a new subsection covering rotation cadence, restore procedure, and which cold-storage bucket holds rolled-over files. (3) M3-1 (sargable prunes) is independent of Q4 and can proceed; the archive-rotate target is the file, not individual rows, so existing prune patterns on `quota_reservations` remain unchanged. (4) Until the rotation job ships, monitor `du -sh /var/lib/macprovider/gateway.db` against Pearl's disk free; alert at 50% of free space as an early warning. |

| 2026-06-24 | **Entry 88 — SPEC-016 v0.1 (provider payout pipeline) locked at design-only; rail chosen: USDC on Base (chain id 8453); IMPL blocked on five named operator-action prerequisites; no `PayoutAdapter` abstraction shipped.** SPEC-016 is the first money-OUT spec on this repo — every prior money-path SPEC (SPEC-005 settlement, SPEC-006 buyer billing, SPEC-015 receipts) was money-IN or accounting-only. The `ledger_payout_ready` table at `phase4-coordinator/internal/billing/store.go:100-127` and the `ClaimPayoutReady` primitive at `phase4-coordinator/internal/billing/payout.go:10-80` have been carrying the `payout_currency TEXT NULL` and `payout_external_id TEXT NULL` columns since SPEC-005's settlement IMPL landed — added in anticipation of exactly this work — and the `trg_lpr_terminal_status_guard` trigger makes `consumed`/`voided` terminal. SPEC-016 v0.1 builds on these existing primitives WITHOUT touching the DDL or the trigger; the new accounting surface is two tables (`provider_payout_addresses` for §3 registration, `payout_attempts` for §4.5 deterministic-retry / nonce ownership) plus a single-row `payout_runner_state` for portal observability. **Rail decision: USDC on Base.** Considered + rejected (NOT enumerated in the SPEC body — they live in this Entry instead, per the prompt's "decision rationale belongs in the Entry, not the normative spec" rule): ACH (week-scale settlement + reversible + KYC overhead), Stripe Connect (operator becomes a money-transmitter intermediary, multi-week onboarding per-provider, takes per-payout %), USDC on Solana (different operator hot-wallet surface from Antfeed which already settles on Base), USDC on Polygon/Ethereum-mainnet (higher gas vs Base with no protocol benefit over Base for this use case), gift-card / cash equivalents (not viable for arbitrary international providers). **Why USDC-on-Base wins on first principles:** (1) **Antfeed parity.** The P2P AI marketplace this network composes with already settles on Base — same chain to monitor, same operator hot-wallet operational surface, single set of compliance posture. (2) **Cost.** Sub-cent ERC-20 `transfer` at typical 2026 base-fee levels; per-payout gas overhead is negligible against per-payout amount, so §5.1 puts gas on the operator (provider always receives exactly `provider_credits → USDC` base units, 1:1 via the SPEC-005 §5.1 micro-dollar unit identity). (3) **Block time.** ~2s blocks → ~10s wall-clock to default 5-confirmation depth, so a 50-row run completes in well under 10 minutes. (4) **EVM tooling parity.** Standard `secp256k1` signing, RFC 6979 deterministic ECDSA (REQUIRED in §6.3 so the §4.5 / §4.6 retry path reproduces tx hashes byte-for-byte), single ABI, single RPC contract — no proprietary tooling. (5) **Provider-side custody.** Provider supplies an existing or fresh Base address from any Base-compatible wallet (Coinbase Wallet, Rabby, Safe, etc.); operator never custodies after the transfer lands. **Scope deliberately NOT shipped at v0.1** (each filed inline in §2 of SPEC-016 with rationale): custodial balances, non-Base rails, provider-side KYC, buyer refunds, multi-currency payouts, `PayoutAdapter` multi-rail abstraction, auto-refill, per-payout fee deduction from provider funds, in-process key rotation, KMS-backed signing, auto-split of over-cap rows, RPC fallback, `POST /admin/payout/void` for manual reorg-revert. The `PayoutAdapter` non-decision is the most load-bearing: YAGNI applies — a single concrete USDC-on-Base implementation is shorter and easier to audit than a polymorphic interface designed for hypothetical Stripe / ACH backends that may never ship. **Audit loop:** v0.1 was written end-to-end in this session against `specs/BUILD_SPEC_016_PAYOUT_USDC_BASE_PROMPT.md`; per `[[feedback-spec-audit-loop-before-pr]]` the spec MUST go through codex audit → fix → re-audit → loop until 0 CRITICAL/MAJOR before merge. Money-path SPECs typically take 3-5 rounds. Anticipated round-1 finding surface from the prompt's own pre-flight: (a) §4.7 reorg-revert handling defers the admin `/admin/payout/void` endpoint to v0.2 without specifying the interim manual procedure; (b) §4.6 nonce-gap recovery (`payout_nonce_gap` alert) similarly halts the runner without specifying the unblocking procedure; (c) §6.4 key-rotation step 2 ("wait for unresolved attempts to resolve via a separate admin path — out of scope at v0.1 beyond noting the gap") creates a circular dependency with (a)/(b). Round 1 will likely escalate at least one of these to CRITICAL or MAJOR. **IMPL is named, not written:** `specs/BUILD_SPEC_016_PAYOUT_IMPL_PROMPT.md` will be authored in a fresh session after v0.1 merges, NOT bundled in this PR. The bundling-exception rule `[[feedback-bundle-spec-impl-one-pr]]` does not apply here because this is a net-new SPEC with no downstream implementer yet — bundling is for incremental SPEC versions (e.g. v0.2 on top of locked v0.1), not net-new SPECs. | **The first money-OUT spec on a network whose money-IN side is already production is a structurally different SPEC class than every prior SPEC on this repo, and the prompt-author recognized that by hardcoding "design-only on v0.1; no IMPL until operator funds hot wallet" as the framing — NOT as a follow-up gate.** Every prior money-path SPEC (SPEC-005, SPEC-006, SPEC-015) shipped IMPL alongside SPEC because the IMPL had no operator-side prerequisites — the SPEC and the code could land together and operators could turn it on with a config flag. SPEC-016 inverts that: the IMPL itself is straightforward (~1.5 weeks per the prompt's budget) but the OPERATOR side gates are real and not in our control — hot wallet provisioning + funding (§9.1), RPC API key (§9.2), cap decisions (§9.3), compliance posture decision (§9.4), and a separate SPEC-014 v0.9 portal screen (§9.5). v0.1 names all five explicitly so that the spec and the IMPL prompt that follows it cannot drift into shipping an unfunded runner. **The `payout_currency` / `payout_external_id` columns that landed with SPEC-005 settlement IMPL are an instance of a pattern worth naming: "anticipatory schema slots".** When a downstream SPEC is known-coming but unwritten, leaving NULL-able columns in the upstream table for the downstream SPEC to populate (with no constraint loosening today) lets the downstream SPEC ship with zero DDL churn. The cost is one TEXT NULL column carrying no data for months; the benefit is that SPEC-016 IMPL touches zero existing tables and adds three new ones, instead of needing a migration on the production billing ledger. Use this pattern again when the next downstream SPEC is named in the change log of the SPEC being written. **The decision to keep the comparison table out of the SPEC body and inline it in this Entry is itself a pattern worth naming: "rail-decision-as-Entry, not as SPEC-§".** The SPEC body is for normative requirements on the rail that won; the alternatives + their rejection reasons are background that ages out of relevance once the SPEC is shipping. Putting them in the Entry means they're discoverable for "why didn't we pick Stripe?" without polluting the normative contract a downstream IMPL has to read. **One forward-pointer captured in the SPEC, not just here:** SPEC-005 §5.1's 1 credit = $0.000001 = 1 USDC-base-unit identity is load-bearing for SPEC-016 — if SPEC-005 ever re-units credits (e.g. introduces a multiplier for inflation/discounting), SPEC-016 IMPL breaks silently. §4.3 step 2 of SPEC-016 explicitly hardcodes the identity AND requires IMPL to REJECT any config that introduces a multiplier; SPEC-005's `ledger_config_snapshots` table is the canonical authority and a SPEC-005 vX.Y+1 candidate is filed in SPEC-016's Appendix B to surface a snapshot field if the invariant ever moves. | **Operator follow-ups, in order:** (1) **Run the codex audit loop on SPEC-016 v0.1** before merging this PR. That prompt MUST carry §9's five prerequisites as a pre-flight checklist + reference SPEC-016 v0.1 as the controlling contract. Do NOT bundle SPEC + IMPL in this PR. (3) **Operator-side: discharge §9 prerequisites in parallel with audit loop.** (a) Create or designate a Base hot wallet; fund with 100 USDC + ~$5 native ETH for initial smoke; generate encrypted wallet file + AES key via systemd-creds; pin address in `payout.hot_wallet_address`. (b) Pick RPC provider (Alchemy / QuickNode / etc.); provision API key sized for one run / 6h + admin balance reads. (c) Decide caps (`payout.per_payout_cap` default $500, `payout.per_day_cap` default $5,000); adjust per risk tolerance. (e) File SPEC-014 v0.9 candidate for payout-address-registration + payout-history portal screens; this is a separate SPEC PR, not bundled. (4) **File deferred follow-up Issue stubs** named in SPEC-016 Appendix B: SPEC-014 v0.9 candidate; SPEC-005 vX.Y+1 candidate for earnings-endpoint payout fields; SPEC-016 v0.2 candidate for `/admin/payout/void` admin endpoint (closes §4.7 reorg-revert + §6.4 key-rotation circular dependency). | 2026-06-22 | **Entry 79 — Coordinator nonce-echo canary probes are a pool trust control, not a new provider protocol.** The c0mpute-style canary recommendation was accepted as a coordinator-side implementation: operator-configured challenge+nonce prompts use the existing WS-tunneled / HTTP-forwarded inference path, record consecutive `canary_fail_count` on the live pool provider, and expose that count in `/poolz` for every provider. The feature is config-gated (`pool.canary_enabled`) to avoid surprise hidden traffic on deploy; defaults define 300s interval, 30s timeout, 8 max tokens, and threshold 3, while enabling now requires private `pool.canary_challenges` prompt/expected templates containing `{nonce}`. Scheduling is per-provider with randomized due times and shuffled sweeps so probes are not emitted as a fixed full-pool burst. On repeated failures, provisional providers become unavailable, are rejected from future provisional admission, and have the WS session closed with `CloseBanned`; pinned providers are held degraded under a canary recovery hold so provider self-reported heartbeat/state transitions cannot launder them back to `ready`. | **This closes the semantic-output honesty gap without changing binary wire compatibility.** Existing auth/token/Tier-2 controls prove identity, transport, and optional model/hash posture; they do not prove that a routed provider is actually running the requested model instead of returning canned output. A nonce-bound private challenge bank catches canned-response/model-substitution classes at low token cost while avoiding a public coordinator-generated arithmetic grammar and fixed probe cadence that adaptive providers could classify without exercising the requested model. | Operator action: leave disabled until the PR is deployed and observed in staging, then enable for open/provisional pools first with deployment-specific `canary_challenges`; rotate that bank periodically and avoid publishing the live corpus. If false positives appear on pinned providers, inspect `/poolz` `canary_fail_count` and logs before clearing the provider by reconnect/recovery; do not auto-promote a canary-held provider to ready without a passing semantic probe. |

The exception is narrow: it only lets a tokenless provisional first connect reach the existing mint / TOFU path, fails closed when no token issuer is wired, and leaves pinned providers plus provider IDs whose active token has already been used on strict tokenless reject. | **Keep the security posture closed while restoring the single-shell-pipe provider UX.** Flipping `require_provider_tokens=false` would reopen the broad anonymous provider WS posture; forcing operator-preprovisioned tokens would destroy public curl-install onboarding. A dedicated bootstrap flag expresses the intended production shape explicitly and lets invite-only deployments keep it disabled. | Deploy the coordinator binary and config together. `phase4-coordinator/dist/check-deploy-config.sh` now hard-fails when `auth.allow_tokenless_provisional_bootstrap` is absent so Pearl cannot silently brick clean installs again. | 2026-06-30 | **Entry 91 — Provider thermal auto-throttle via `ProcessInfo.thermalState`; gate is on `ProviderSnapshot.slotsFree`, not on heartbeat or admission paths; threshold `.serious` with no operator knob.** `ProcessInfo.processInfo.thermalState` and `ProcessInfo.thermalStateDidChangeNotification` are the API (no IOKit, no `pmset` shell-out, no entitlement). New `ThermalGate` actor (`phase3-binary/Sources/macprovider-cli/ThermalGate.swift`) holds the latest state, subscribes to the notification at process startup, and logs `event=thermal_state_changed from=X to=Y throttled=B` once per transition (no debounce — macOS already smooths). `ProviderSnapshot.slotsFree` becomes `thermallyThrottled ? 0 : max(0, maxConcurrency - inFlight)` and the snapshot's `status` flips to `.busy` when throttled. The actually-affected surfaces are: the coordinator heartbeat (which reads `snapshot.slotsFree` + `snapshot.status`) and the local `/healthz` / `/v1/status` HTTP responses (which read `snapshot.status` and capacity). `/v1/models` returns only `{id, object, created, owned_by}` and never reads slots; Tier2 attestation only claims `snapshot.capacity.ramGB` — both intentionally do NOT degrade under thermal throttle because capacity is a hardware fact, not a runtime-availability signal. **In-flight requests are NOT cancelled by the throttle** — mid-streaming a buyer mid-response is far worse than briefly running the Mac warm; the coordinator's existing routing migrates new traffic off `slots_free=0` providers naturally. **`ProviderCapacity.maxConcurrency` stays untouched** — capacity is a hardware fact and is still advertised verbatim in `slots_total` and the registration payload, including under throttle. PR: branch `feat/thermal-autothrottle`, ~99 production lines + 111 test lines, 6 new ProviderStatus unit tests pass, full 669-test suite green. | **Half-day Cluster D item from `audits/2026-06-22/CLUSTER_HANDOFF.md`; intentionally scoped to NOT touch wire shape, coordinator, gateway, or attestation.** Plugging on `slotsFree` (the consumed property) instead of on each consumer is the load-bearing design choice: one ~10-line edit closes the throttle for every present and future caller. `.serious` is the threshold with no config knob — adding one creates an operator footgun where someone disables throttling and cooks their Mac; the SwiftUI / iOS ecosystem already converges on this exact threshold pair. Per [[feedback-build-audit-loop]] this diff is small enough that ONE codex IMPL audit round suffices (vs the 3-lane convention for money-path code per [[feedback-three-lane-codex-audits]]) — it's behaviour-only inside an existing struct, doesn't change wire shape, doesn't change billing semantics. | Follow-ups: (1) If empirical flapping ever surfaces in production logs (rapid `.serious↔.fair` thrashing), add an `ignoreThrottleUntil` hysteresis timestamp — deferred per the BUILD prompt's "don't pre-emptively add hysteresis that may not earn its complexity". (2) The transition log includes `from`/`to`/`throttled` but NOT `slots_free` because the gate doesn't hold capacity; the next heartbeat after a transition is the canonical observable for slot change. (3) No new heartbeat field — `thermal_state` on the wire is debugging noise; the log + the `slots_free` swing is the entire observability surface. (4) Operator manual smoke: `yes > /dev/null & yes > /dev/null & yes > /dev/null & yes > /dev/null` for ~5 min on a hot day reproduces a real `.serious` transition (won't always trigger on a cool day / cold Mac Studio — documented as best-effort). |

Same blocker for `nvidia/nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b` (`NemotronHForCausalLM` Mamba/SSM hybrid arch) — zero upstream issues, nobody has even requested it. **RESEARCH_227 follow-up survey** (`[docs/research/RESEARCH_227_RATE_CARD_V3_MEMO.md](docs/research/RESEARCH_227_RATE_CARD_V3_MEMO.md)`, codex 668-line memo): refreshed OpenRouter top-50 + classified each open-weight / MLX-deployable / commercially-licensed candidate; recommended 5-row v3: `meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b-instruct $0.027/M` + `openai/gpt-oss-20b $0.100/M` (keep) + `google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it $0.240/M` (keep — rank-20 demand) + `nvidia/nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b $0.160/M` (NEW — demand rank 68/88, fits 16.55GB) + `qwen/qwen3-coder-30b-a3b-instruct $0.235/M` (NEW — developer-coding MoE lane) + optional 6th `qwen/qwen-2.5-coder-32b-instruct $0.850/M` (M4-Max/Ultra only); demote `qwen3-30b-a3b` from $0.400/M because outside top-100 demand and Entry 92's price is 2× live $0.193/M market. RESEARCH_227 also surfaced that **the macprovider-cli install script + autotune flow defaults M5/32GB providers to dense Qwen3-32B-4bit** (`[phase3-binary/dist/install.sh:649-657](phase3-binary/dist/install.sh:649)` `choose_model()`; `[phase3-binary/Sources/macprovider-cli/AutotuneCommand.swift:84-90](phase3-binary/Sources/macprovider-cli/AutotuneCommand.swift:84)` `defaultCandidates`) — zero MoE-small-active options, optimizing for "biggest model that fits" not "highest expected $/hr given demand × TPS × rate-card price"; this is the root cause of why every new macprovider provider becomes a donor-class serving a no-demand uneconomic model from day 1, mirroring Darkbloom's $264-work-vs-$1,946-subsidy pattern. | **Entry 92 was structurally correct on every strategic call** — MoE active-param bandwidth math validated (35 tok/s gpt-oss-20b on M5 vs 6.8 on dense 32B = 5.2× same hardware), per-model RAM-first admission is the right framing (M5 32GB serves gpt-oss-20b smoothly but is donor-only on dense 32B), token-subsidy-carries-provider-incentive is empirically necessary (best M5 cell earns $0.021/hr at developer-coding-row premium price, still <10% of B6 bare-min $0.30/hr). **The failure was scope estimation**: Entry 92 assumed Wave 1 was a coordinator config flip ("hot-reloadable via SIGHUP"), but the bench pass empirically discovered that the gateway has independent per-model SSE accounting logic that breaks for EVERY new model row in 3 distinct ways depending on architecture family. The 5-row v3 rate-card is **provably 0/5 deployable today** through the live network, not the 4/5 or 3/5 I initially estimated by code reading alone. Per `[[feedback-money-path-quick-win-scope-validation]]` this is exactly the class of finding that empirical bench passes catch and code reading misses. Per `[[audit-loop-catches-billing-ledger-drift]]` the I1 invariant in `test/network-harness/internal/reconcile/ledger_test.go` exists precisely for this category of bug; the bench reproduces the pattern at the smallest possible cost (one buyer × two requests × 128 tokens = sub-cent per scenario × 5 model rows = sub-dollar total bench spend) before any real buyer traffic flows through broken billing. The two-bug stacking (coordinator key normalization SCN-NEW-01 + gateway tokenizer registry per model family) is the correct frame for the Wave-0 work — both are money-path 3-lane-audited code changes per `[[feedback-three-lane-codex-audits]]` and must ship together because Wave-0a (coord fix) without Wave-0b (gateway fix) still routes new model traffic to the gateway fallback bucket, and Wave-0b without Wave-0a still falls back to default-rate $1.00/M on key mismatch. The provider-side rate-card-aware install/autotune defaults from RESEARCH_227 are a third independently-shippable Wave-0c that does not block Wave 1 hot-reload but should land before any new provider acquisition push to stop the donor-class default. Wave 1 — rate-card hot reload on `/opt/macprovider/coordinator.yaml` Pearl — REMAINS gated on Wave 0 green + 4 P0 bench rerun showing I1 PASS on every new model row. Waves 2-3 (per-model admission + off-chain MPROV ledger) unchanged from Entry 92. **Re-trigger Entry 93 revisit on any of**: Wave 0a (gateway) or Wave 0b (coord) PR opens for review, upstream `ml-explore/mlx-swift-examples#467` lands Gemma4.swift (unblocks `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` row), any upstream issue/PR for Nemotron-H architecture opens (unblocks `nvidia/nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b`), or first real paying buyer revenue requires Wave-1 even at reduced row count (e.g., MoE-rows-only after partial Wave 0a). **Updated 5-row deployable subset after Wave 0 lands**: keep RESEARCH_227's 5 rows but tier each by deployability — `gpt-oss-20b` + `qwen3-coder-30b-a3b-instruct` are empirically validated on this M5 (37 / 28 tok/s network-delivered, route attribution confirmed) and ship in Wave 1; `qwen-2.5-coder-32b-instruct` ships in Wave 2 once per-model admission gates it to M-Max/Ultra (network-delivered 6.3 tok/s on M5 confirms RESEARCH_227's "M4 Max/Ultra only" gate is exactly right — UX is painful regardless of price); `gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` ships when upstream `mlx-swift-examples#467` lands (operator monitors monthly); `nvidia/nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b` ships when upstream Nemotron-H decoder exists (currently no upstream issue — file one). **Owner: operator.** **Launch-readiness note (Claude designer critique 2026-07-03):** the `/Users/augstar/projects/malibu/host/index.html` landing page still sells the CLI flow (`curl | bash`, "Sign in with GitHub", "You're a node") — exactly the vocabulary AC-026-02 forbids inside the App. That page is SPEC-025's normative surface, not SPEC-026's, but the two surfaces MUST ship App-track copy in lockstep with §10 step 9's Sparkle release: shipping the App-side flag flip while the landing page still describes the CLI flow means users arrive via a "Sign in with GitHub / You're a node" page and then download an App that says none of those things — bait-and-switch friction and a marketing-surface violation of AC-026-02. The five SCN-226 bench artifacts (per_request.jsonl + metrics_summary.json + ledger_reconcile.json + invariants.json + scenario.yaml) are operator-local at `test/network-harness/artifacts/smoke_*-20260630T*Z/` (`.gitignore:64` excludes that path so bench output stays ephemeral); the bench scenario YAMLs DO land in this PR at `test/network-harness/scenarios/smoke_{gpt_oss_20b,qwen3_coder_30b_a3b,qwen25_coder_32b,llama_31_8b}.yaml` and all five are reproducible with `BUYER_TOKEN=$(cat ~/.config/macprovider/buyer-api-key) ./run-scenario.sh smoke_<model>.yaml` on a fresh worktree off origin/main; the RESEARCH_227 prompt+memo land in `specs/RESEARCH_227_RATE_CARD_V3_{PROMPT,MEMO}.md` — together these are the audit trail for this entry. |
