| 2026-07-02 | **Entry 98 — SPEC-024 v0.1 design draft records prefix-cache billing as one wire field, one ledger column, one rate-card field, one formula split, and one buyer-visible usage field.** `specs/SPEC-024-prefix-cache-billing.md` defines provider-reported `cached_prompt_tokens` for SPEC-004 sticky-hit conversations, `ledger_request_credits.cached_prompt_tokens`, `prompt_cache_hit_rate_per_mtok`, and flat buyer-visible `usage.cached_prompt_tokens`. The operator discounted-rate guidance is documented as target 25% of `prompt_credits_per_mtok`; pre-SPEC-024 configs omit the new key and load with an effective cache-hit rate equal to the normal prompt rate until explicitly configured. The rollout invariant is algebraically proven: when `cached_prompt_tokens IS NULL` or `0`, `COALESCE(cached_prompt_tokens, 0)=0`, so the new numerator reduces byte-identically to SPEC-005 v0.4. The per-attempt decision is locked: only `attempt_n=0` can receive cache-hit billing; retry / re-attempt rows price all prompt tokens at the normal prompt rate. | **This closes the design-side half of Cluster C #5 without widening the money-path surface.** Routing-side sticky affinity already pins conversation turns to the same provider; SPEC-024 supplies the economic reward only when the provider reports actual KV-cache reuse. SPEC-005 coordinator billing is the sole v0.1 authority for credit-denominated buyer billing; SPEC-006 daily quota remains token-count based and does not refund quota tokens for cache hits. Over-reporting is not a provider fraud vector because it reduces payable credits; under-reporting is the buyer-risk direction and is addressed in v0.1 by explicit sticky-hit `cached_prompt_tokens=0` observability, with coordinator cross-checking deferred to v0.2. Invalid cache reports use existing SPEC-005 quarantine machinery (`quarantined=1`, deterministic `quarantine_reason`, valid `usage_source`, zero payable credits), and buyers see only `effective_cached_prompt_tokens = COALESCE(ledger_cached_prompt_tokens, 0)`. | Next step after v0.1 audit lock is `BUILD_SPEC_024_PREFIX_CACHE_BILLING_IMPL_PROMPT.md`; do not bundle it with the design PR. File separate follow-ups for SPEC-002 / SPEC-005 / SPEC-006 / SPEC-007 addenda rather than editing those locked specs in this branch. |
| 2026-07-02 | **Entry 99 — PR #317 DeepSec run becomes the standard subscription-only audit procedure for money-path, auth, network-security, and performance-sensitive PRs.** The reference run audited PR #317 at exact SHA `b0f1c60a1971c2705e4c713b964634e8a4af4944`: Codex discovery scanned 19 files and produced 8 findings; Claude revalidation confirmed all 8 as true positives, 0 false positives, 0 fixed, 0 uncertain. The reusable operator process is now documented in `docs/operations/deepsec-security-audit-runbook.md`, including exact-SHA scratch worktree setup, file-manifest rules, subscription-only wrapper, Codex discovery command, Claude revalidation command, export commands, and merge gates. | **DeepSec is now an explicit security gate, not an ad hoc experiment.** Any future PR touching billing, buyer quota/settlement, auth, request logs/idempotency, gateway routing/auth, provider admission, local loopback API, Pearl VPS/API abuse controls, or rate-limit/performance surfaces should produce a DeepSec artifact set before merge. Claude is used to revalidate Codex findings, not to rerun discovery. Unresolved `CRITICAL`, `HIGH`, `HIGH_BUG`, and `MEDIUM` true positives block money-path/network-security merges by default; `BUG` findings block when they affect billing, settlement, quota, auth, or abuse control. | The runbook locks the key auth lesson from PR #317: stripping shell env is not enough because user-level Claude settings can inject `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`; Claude SDK subprocesses must be forced through project-only settings via `CLAUDE_CODE_EXECUTABLE` shim / `--setting-sources project`, while Codex runs with API/gateway envs stripped and local `~/.codex/auth.json` present. Re-trigger this entry if DeepSec changes Claude SDK invocation, if the wrapper stops proving subscription-only behavior, or if a future audit needs a third verifier model. |
| 2026-07-03 | **Entry 100 — KV-cache hit detection implementation lands the provider-side half of SPEC-024 without widening billing authority.** The coordinator now forwards optional `conversation_key` only for SPEC-004 sticky-hit WS-tunneled routes, sourced from `X-MacProvider-Internal-Conv`; clear WS frames carry it as relay metadata, while tier-2 encrypted frames carry it only inside the AEAD-authenticated `inference_request_plaintext` envelope and ignore forged top-level keys. The provider parses that frame field, keeps an actor-isolated bounded LRU conversation cache keyed by conversation key, reuses MLX KV cache layers only when model ID, KV-bit mode, trimmability, and a 32-token minimum LCP pass, trims stale suffix tokens before suffix-only generation, and reports `cached_prompt_tokens` while preserving `prompt_tokens` as the original full prompt length. Defaults are intentionally conservative: 8 conversations, 200k cached tokens, 15 minute TTL, configurable via `MACPROVIDER_CONV_CACHE_MAX_CONVERSATIONS`, `MACPROVIDER_CONV_CACHE_MAX_TOKENS`, and `MACPROVIDER_CONV_CACHE_TTL_MINUTES`. | **The cache is an optimization signal, not a new trust root.** Cache updates commit only after successful generation and structured validation; partial failures abort without storing a trimmed or speculative state. Misses on model swap, KV-bit swap, short/diverged prefix, non-trimmable cache layers, exact no-new-token prompts, TTL expiry, and cap eviction all produce zero cached tokens. The coordinator still decides discount eligibility through SPEC-024/SPEC-005 billing validation; this implementation only enables honest provider reporting on sticky-hit attempts. Metrics are deliberately limited to structured `event=conv_cache` stderr logs for the first implementation pass rather than adding a metrics backend. | Verification on the implementation branch: `swift test --filter ConversationCacheTests` passed 6 tests; `swift test --filter 'HTTPServerReceiptTests|InferenceRelayTests|Tier2ProviderSessionTests|ChatCompletionRequestTests|ConversationCacheTests'` passed 63 tests; `go test ./internal/ws ./internal/buyer -count=1` passed both changed coordinator packages; second security-auditor pass reported 0 C/H findings remain. Re-trigger this entry if SPEC-024 adds coordinator cross-checking, if MLX cache APIs change trimmability/offset semantics, if cache sizing causes memory pressure on provider Macs, or if metrics need promotion from structured logs into production telemetry. |
| 2026-07-03 | **Entry 101 — SPEC-023 v0.2 amendment ratifies the v1.7.9 soft-signal-gate shipped semantics, rotates the autotune-static keypair v2 → v3, and re-signs the live catalog with M-Base-realistic `min_sustained_tps` advisory values.** The v0.2 change log documents that `min_sustained_tps` / `max_4k_ttft_ms` / `swap_detected` are advisory (emit `tps_below_gate` / `ttft_above_gate` / `swap_observed_under_load` warnings but do not veto eligibility); the only remaining runtime hard eligibility gate is `thermal_throttle_detected == true`, and the real financial gate is `expected_net_usd_per_hour >= $0.0050/hr`. §3.1, §5, §7.1, AC-12, AC-13, AC-22 amended inline with `[amended v0.2]` callouts so the v0.1 language is preserved for history but the shipped semantics are unambiguous. New keyID `streamvc-autotune-static-v3`; new base64 pubkey `1qzXegR2OEu0TaQNWjUkN4PamQAHdpvBcYW/pJ4h6oE=` baked in [`AutotuneRecommend.swift`](phase3-binary/Sources/macprovider-cli/AutotuneRecommend.swift); v3 **public** key committed at [`phase3-binary/dist/static/keys/autotune-static-v3.public.base64`](phase3-binary/dist/static/keys/autotune-static-v3.public.base64); v3 **private** key held off-repo by the operator at `~/.config/macprovider/keys/autotune-static-v3.private.base64` (`chmod 0600`), overridable via `$AUTOTUNE_STATIC_V3_PRIVATE_KEY_PATH`. Live catalog `min_sustained_tps` cuts: qwen3-coder-30b-a3b 25→20 (M5 cold 23.4 tok/s headroom), openai/gpt-oss-20b 30→15 (M5 cold 16.7 tok/s), meta-llama/llama-3.1-8b 20→15 (M-Base-lite), qwen2.5-coder-32b 25→20; qwen3-32b unchanged at 15. Baked catalog mirrors those 4 M-Base cuts; intentionally keeps `runtime_status="listed"`/`"blocked"` diagnostic rows and a conservative M-Max floor on qwen3-32b that live doesn't have. New re-sign script at [`scripts/resign-autotune-static.sh`](scripts/resign-autotune-static.sh) reads private key from env or default path and refuses to run if key mode is wider than `0600`. The v2 → v3 rotation is opportunistic (a clean cut-over point that's easy to audit in git), NOT prompted by any known v2 key compromise. Attacker surface with a hypothetical v3 private key leak: still bounded to malicious catalog delivery, still requires TLS MITM against `coordinator.streamvc.live` OR Pearl VPS root; still cannot bypass `model_sha256` HuggingFace-artifact pinning; still cannot manipulate rate-card earnings (separate TLS-signed coordinator endpoint). Track landing-page copy as a hard prerequisite for step 9, not a soft one. **Phase 5 / network implication**: the M-Base drop-out cliff was the largest known UX obstacle to the 120-provider acquisition cohort ramp; closing it via soft gates (v1.7.9) + calibrated catalog (v1.7.10) unblocks that acquisition push. | 2026-07-03 | **Entry 103 — Provider runtime migrates from the stale `mlx-swift-examples` fork point to upstream `mlx-swift-lm` 3.31.4 so Gemma4 and Nemotron-H are no longer treated as architecture-absent.** The phase3 binary now resolves `mlx-swift-lm` 3.31.4 at `bd4b7434e6bdb588c7ef55706ff8904cb7fd4c57` and its `mlx-swift` dependency 0.31.6 at `0bb916c67f4b9e5c682cbe02a42c701c93ab5021`, with an explicit `swift-transformers` 1.0.0 dependency to satisfy the new tokenizer loader surface. Runtime loading moved to the upstream local-directory `loadContainer(from:using:)` API while preserving the signed coordinator artifact preflight and local HuggingFace snapshot fallback. Because command-line SwiftPM does not build the dependency's Metal shaders, release artifacts now carry `mlx.metallib` beside `macprovider-cli` and the installer preserves that resource next to the real installed binary. Installer rollout changes keep default installs on latest stable releases only, require explicit `MACPROVIDER_VERSION=v1.8.0` opt-in for the canary prerelease, and keep rollback pinned to v1.7.11. | **This corrects Entry 97's stale blocker, but it does not promote new buyer rows by itself.** Gemma/Nemotron remain `runtime_status="blocked"` in diagnostic catalogs until migration validation and rate-card rollout finish; the correction is that the block is no longer "upstream architecture missing." Canary behavior is intentionally conservative: prereleases are skipped by default, explicit pins are strict SemVer tags above the rollback floor, and signature/SHA failures fail closed without falling back to latest stable. The M5 32GB local validation set is the three M5-eligible currently-shipping rows: `Qwen3-Coder-30B-A3B-Instruct-4bit`, `gpt-oss-20b-MXFP4-Q8`, and `Meta-Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct-4bit`. `Qwen3-32B-4bit` and `Qwen2.5-Coder-32B-Instruct-4bit` remain larger-RAM/default-promotion gates, not M5 waivers. | Verification on the implementation branch: `swift test` passed 822 tests with 7 skipped; installer pin regression harness passed with invalid pins, prerelease-before-tag JSON, checksum mismatch, and signature mismatch cases failing closed; provider artifact preflight rejects missing/unsafe Metal resources; local branch-binary smokes passed for the three M5-eligible rows with non-streaming and streaming HTTP 200, non-empty output, terminal streaming usage, and no stop-token leakage. Remaining promotion gates before default v1.8.x stable: larger-RAM §3.6b validation for the two 48GB+ rows; buyer-path smoke through `api.streamvc.live` or equivalent gateway+coordinator staging with route hit, no fallback row, gateway/coordinator token reconciliation, and no token drift; explicit prerelease canary observation window; and PR body evidence for all supply-chain pins and audit status. Phase 7 P1 (provider availability & fitness — SPEC-002 v1.2.0 FR-P11a runtime circuit-breaker; provider admission gated on token production; sleep-tolerant status; P2 observability monitor) then shipped its regression coverage across commits including `717b58b` (SPEC-002 v1.2.0 breaker + P1 build spec), `f85f73a` (remove repeatedly faulting providers from routing), `699a782` (gate provider admission on token production), `fc009e4` (close Phase 7 provider-fitness audit gaps), and `aa016e2` (harden provider recovery audit edges), culminating in Entry 32's audit closure and v1.3.2 deploy. Every one of those tests placed its fault doubles **inline in `phase4-coordinator/internal/buyer/server_test.go`** (see `deadMidInferenceRelay` at `:5516`, wired via `WithRelay` from `:4257` and `:4446`) and `phase4-coordinator/internal/ws/server_test.go` (warm-up / timeout / zero-token cases at `:922`, `:976`, `:1012`), never importing `internal/testfaults/`. The module carried a `//go:build testfaults` tag so it never compiled into production; `go tool nm` confirmed zero symbols in the release binary. Three-lane codex audit (code / security / architect) converged at 0 CRITICAL / 0 HIGH / 0 MEDIUM after one round; the sole LOW was this decision-log conflict, which this entry resolves. `SPEC-004-smart-router.md:916` and `PHASE7_P1_BUILD_SPEC.md:79,:182` updated to point at the inline doubles. Historical build/audit records (PHASE6_ENGINEERING_BUILD_REPORT, PHASE6_ENGINEERING_AUDIT_FIX_CHECK, BUILD_SPEC_004_PROMPT, BUILD_PHASE6_ENGINEERING_PROMPT, AUDIT_PHASE6_ENGINEERING_FIX_CHECK_PROMPT) intentionally left unmodified as point-in-time artifacts. | **Entry 29's "keep the fault helpers" clause is superseded, not reversed.** Entry 29 correctly captured the intent at the time; the implementers who then built Phase 7 P1 chose colocated inline test doubles because the fault scenarios were tightly coupled to specific test setups (`WithRelay` closure captures, per-test provider fixtures) that didn't factor cleanly into a shared package. Inline doubles ship with the tests that use them and don't require a build-tag gate to stay out of production. If a future fault-injection scenario needs to be shared across multiple `_test.go` files (or across `internal/buyer/` and `internal/ws/`), promoting the shared piece into `internal/testutil/` (or resurrecting a slim `internal/testfaults/` targeted at that scenario) remains a live option — but scaffolding it ahead of a concrete second consumer proved to be premature abstraction. | **Effective on merge of `fix/deadcode-cleanup`.** Verification before push: `go build ./...` clean and `go test ./...` PASS on both `phase4-coordinator` and `phase5-gateway` with the module removed; three-lane codex audit converged 0/0/0 (code lane referenced by [`specs/AUDIT_DEADCODE_TESTFAULTS_PROMPT.md`](specs/AUDIT_DEADCODE_TESTFAULTS_PROMPT.md)); no importer of `internal/testfaults` exists in tree. Re-trigger this entry if a future feature slice adds a second consumer that would want a shared fault package, if a Phase 7 P3 / Phase 8 build spec is drafted that assumes `internal/testfaults/` exists, or if the inline-doubles pattern in `server_test.go` becomes unwieldy enough that the module's build-tag isolation is worth reinstating. |
| 2026-07-04 | **Entry 105 — Dashboard hardware stats ship through the existing SPEC-017 rollup pipeline with cached, verified, asynchronous enrichment only.** The implementation adds `specs/BUILD_SPEC_STATS_HARDWARE_CACHE_PROMPT.md`, `provider_hardware_profiles`, `chip_hardware_profiles`, `phase4-coordinator/internal/stats/hardware` cache, `poolsnapshot.NewWithHardware`, and coordinator rollup wiring from `statsPools.Rollup`. Public `/v1/stats/overview` keeps the existing response shape and continues reading precomputed `stats_overview_current` through `stats_reader`; there are no stats-handler DB joins against hardware tables. App-track `/register` persists provider-reported hardware identity only after rate-limit, nonce, identity, and token-mint success. A manual `007_hardware_profiles.down.sql` rollback artifact is checked by migration tests. `.deepsec-local/` is now ignored so raw local security artifacts stay out of commits. Re-trigger this entry if any buyer routing, token streaming, provider selection, heartbeat, auth, billing, or public stats handler path imports/calls `internal/stats/hardware`; if hardware enrichment becomes synchronous or unbounded; if `provider_onboarding` gains `verified` authority; if capacity uses unverified provider-reported fields; if Docker-backed integration grants can skip in CI; or if `/v1/stats/overview` stops reading precomputed rollup rows. |

The fix adds `cmd/stats-inventory-sync`, a oneshot operator binary that reads `/etc/macprovider-stats/stats-hardware-inventory.yaml`, validates chip/provider references before opening Postgres using the same lowercase, space-collapsed chip key shape as provider onboarding, rejects non-`operator` sources, and upserts trusted chip constants plus provider verification bindings inside one transaction using a dedicated `STATS_INVENTORY_DSN`. The YAML is authoritative for trusted inventory: removed providers are unverified and removed chip constants are deleted in the same transaction. Systemd service/timer and sample role grants are shipped under `phase4-coordinator/dist/`; the timer is opt-in through `ConditionPathExists`, runs as a distinct `macprovider-stats` Unix user from `/opt/macprovider-stats`, and performs no coordinator RPCs. | **The coordinator remains a reader of precomputed, cached facts.** No buyer routing, token streaming, provider selection, heartbeat, auth, billing, or public stats handler path is modified. The sidecar writes only `chip_hardware_profiles` and `provider_hardware_profiles`; the existing stats rollup/cache decides when those rows become public stats. Hardware-attested remains separate and must stay zero until real attestation succeeds. | **Effective on merge of `feat/stats-hardware-inventory`.** Re-trigger this entry if the sidecar gains coordinator HTTP calls, if it runs more frequently than needed for static inventory, if it accepts provider-submitted capacity values as trusted input, if `stats_inventory_writer` receives grants beyond the two inventory tables, if the sidecar stops running under a separate Unix identity from the coordinator, or if code starts guessing Apple chip capacity constants instead of requiring operator config. |
| 2026-07-05 | **Entry 107 — Malibu.app persists CLI-emitted autotune serve config, not app-synthesized provenance.** The `Malibu-v1.8.2.pkg` first-run flow ran `macprovider-cli autotune --recommend --json`, displayed the recommendation, then started `serve` with a config that still contained only `provider_id` and `link_state`. `serve` correctly refused coordinator join because verified artifact/catalog provenance was missing. The audited fix extends the CLI recommendation JSON with a `serve_config` object built from the same selected benchmark plus signed candidate-catalog row that `--apply` uses for `ConfigApplier`, then Malibu.app parses, validates shape, and upserts only those recommendation-owned fields through `ProviderConfig` before download/start. | **Trust stays with the CLI recommendation pipeline.** Malibu.app may persist only the typed `serve_config` payload emitted by the bundled CLI; it must not derive `model_artifact_path`, `model_artifact_sha256`, or `model_catalog_*` provenance from display JSON, local cache paths, defaults, or `onboarding.json`. App-side validation is syntactic shape validation to prevent obvious exit-2 loops before spawn; semantic artifact hash verification, catalog freshness, rate-card/model agreement, and signed catalog admission remain owned by `macprovider-cli serve`. | Re-trigger this entry if `serve` adds required coordinator-join fields, if `ConfigApplier` changes its owned recommendation key list, if the app starts computing artifact/catalog provenance itself, if `onboarding.json` begins storing recommendation payloads, if `--recommend --apply` becomes reliable enough to replace App-side persistence, or if a shared Foundation schema becomes available and should replace the mirrored app/CLI field list. |
| 2026-07-05 | **Entry 108 — Trusted hardware identity promotion roots require a separate operator credential.** The stats inventory sidecar now supports an optional `trusted_hardware` YAML section so operator-verified autotune evidence can be promoted without editing coordinator code or adding runtime coordinator work. That section writes `hardware_verification_trust`, but it is gated by `STATS_TRUST_INVENTORY_DSN` and a distinct `stats_trust_inventory_writer` role; the ordinary `stats_inventory_writer` remains write-limited to chip/provider inventory tables and has only read-only access to trust/job tables so it can demote stale CLI-promoted profiles from exact verified evidence. Trust identity hashes must be lowercase 64-hex SHA-256/HMAC digests, not raw serial-like identifiers. Omitted `trusted_hardware` leaves trust roots untouched; present non-empty sections reconcile the table authoritatively; present empty maps are rejected to avoid accidental global trust wipes. Conflict upserts preserve `trusted_at` when trust-defining fields are unchanged. | **Trust-root writes are operationally distinct from capacity inventory writes.** This keeps Entry 106's low-privilege inventory role intact while still giving operators a durable path to seed all providers after autotune evidence lands. The coordinator, public stats handler, buyer routing, provider selection, billing, and websocket paths remain unaffected; the sidecar performs bounded Postgres writes outside hot paths. | **Effective on merge of `codex/trusted-inventory-release-cli-2`.** Re-trigger this entry if `stats_inventory_writer` gains trust/job write privileges, if trust hashes accept non-64-hex values, if an empty YAML trust section can delete all trust roots, if provider-reported hardware is auto-promoted without operator trust, or if trust promotion moves into coordinator request/heartbeat paths. |
| 2026-07-05 | **Entry 109 — Public hardware capacity may fall back to live heartbeat summaries, while attestation remains separate.** Entry 106's sidecar is still the trusted inventory path, but the Malibu dashboard must not require an operator to SSH into Pearl and hand-enter capacity before non-sensitive hardware totals become non-zero. The phase3 binary now samples local chip/CPU information once per process, derives display bandwidth/power/GPU capacity from Apple Silicon family, and publishes that optional `hardware_summary` on normal provider heartbeats. The coordinator stores the bounded heartbeat summary on the live pool session, and the SPEC-017 pool snapshot uses it only when the verified hardware cache has no row for that provider. | **This is a display-capacity fallback, not trusted attestation.** Verified `chip_hardware_profiles` still override heartbeat summaries. `nodes_hardware_attested` continues to depend only on `pool.AttestationStatusAttested`, so a provider-reported hardware summary cannot claim attestation. Auth handshakes and identity-signature transcripts are unchanged; the new metadata rides heartbeat only. | Re-trigger this entry if heartbeat hardware starts influencing routing, billing, provider selection, auth, token streaming, payout, or attestation status; if public stats prefers provider-reported capacity over verified inventory; if invalid optional hardware fields can drop an otherwise valid provider session; or if the binary probes slow external hardware commands on every heartbeat. |
| 2026-07-06 | **Entry 110 - SPEC-023 recommendation-owned provider slots scale by Apple Silicon class and RAM.** The phase3 `autotune --recommend` / `--apply` serve-config payload now derives `max_concurrency_override` from the detected hardware summary instead of hardcoding every provider to one slot: M-base and M-Pro stay at 1, low-RAM M-Max/M-Ultra stay at 1, M-Max with at least 48GB RAM emits 2, M-Ultra with at least 96GB emits 3, and M-Ultra with at least 128GB emits 4. The existing config and serve path already honors that value through `--max-batch`, so the coordinator continues to learn total provider slots through the existing heartbeat `slots_total` field. | **This resolves the A1a retry ceiling on physically capable providers without changing coordinator routing or heartbeat shape.** The decision is deliberately chip-name plus RAM gated rather than bandwidth-tier gated because M-Pro can derive tier B but must remain a single-slot provider for safety. The recommendation pipeline remains the source of truth; Malibu.app and operators should persist the emitted serve config rather than synthesizing slot policy locally. | **Effective on merge of `fix/373-a1b-multislot`.** Re-trigger this entry if provider slot policy starts depending on provider-reported bandwidth tier alone, if coordinator routing starts overriding `slots_total`, if `serve` stops honoring `max_concurrency_override`, if Malibu.app synthesizes `max_batch` outside the CLI recommendation payload, or if live M-Max/M-Ultra sweeps show memory pressure requiring lower ceilings. |
| 2026-07-06 | **Entry 111 - Nemotron moves from blocked diagnostic row to paid-yield candidate after runtime validation; coordinator pricing starts at Qwen3-Coder parity.** Issue 411 promotes `nvidia/nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b` in SPEC-023's baked and signed-static recommendation surfaces and the Pearl coordinator rate-card YAML. Demand rank is recorded as `68` from RESEARCH_227 with conservative demand weight `0.30`, `recommendable=true`, and `min_provider_target=20`. | **This is a rollout-state correction, not a new runtime bet.** Entry 103 superseded the stale "upstream architecture absent" blocker; the remaining blocker was coordinator/catalog rollout. The coordinator YAML stores the normalized billing key `nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b`, while the catalog/demand surfaces keep the public model key `nvidia/nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b`; `RateFor` normalization tests cover the raw, namespaced, and MLX artifact aliases so buyer ledger rows do not fall back to `default`. | **Effective on merge of `fix/411-nemotron-rate-card` and Pearl deploy.** The local v3 static signing private key was unavailable, so SPEC-023 rotates the static-feed keypair to v4 in this branch: `streamvc-autotune-static-v4`, public key `zTKDIdMmKKkO1Cgf5OdTzMOytVqW7U8SGsJ9XrzAltU=`, private key held off-repo at `~/.config/macprovider/keys/autotune-static-v4.private.base64`. | 2026-07-06 | **Entry 112 - Coordinator slot queues count as routing wait, not provider billable latency.** The coordinator may hold a buyer request briefly when an otherwise eligible ready provider reports `slots_free=0`, but that wait happens before provider dispatch and before any settlement-capable work is produced. | 2026-07-06 | **Entry 113 - SPEC-023 starter tier keeps positive-net M4-32 providers on the serving path without lowering the paid threshold.** Issue 412 adds a third recommendation outcome between paid and donor: `recommendation_tier = "starter"` when no eligible row clears `$0.0050/hr` but at least one eligible row earns `> $0/hr` net. The CLI still refuses non-positive economics as a recommendation. Donor remains the non-positive or no-fit path and keeps default-No local donor-mode semantics. | **The threshold is preserved, not hidden.** The product constraint was "all providers have to have a model on start" for the beta cohort, while buyer prices cannot be raised and provider subsidies are undesirable. The harness now distinguishes `eligible_row_count` (paid) from `eligible_earning_row_count` (paid or starter), and only the M4-32 row uses `at_least_one_earning_row`; higher tiers still gate on paid rows, smaller structural tiers remain future work. | **Effective on merge of `fix/412`.** Re-trigger this entry if the paid threshold changes, if starter rows become non-serving or non-registered, if donor mode begins serving buyer traffic, if `recommendation_tier` disappears from JSON or cached state, if M4-32 current pricing becomes paid-tier and should move back to `at_least_one_paid_row`, or if marketing renames "starter" and the transcript/warning vocabulary must change coherently. |

| 2026-07-06 | **Entry 114 — Rate-card v4 pivot — drop hourly-net projection, adopt per-token payout recommendation semantics. Fix nemotron to RESEARCH_227 broad-fleet rate.** The SPEC-023 autotune recommender currently projects provider earnings as `expected_net_usd_per_hour = gross_usd_per_hour × provider_share × assumed_utilization − electricity_usd_per_hour`, then gates recommendations at `$0.0050/hr` (`AutotuneRecommendEngine.paidThreshold`). PR #419 added a "starter tier" for positive-but-below-threshold rows. That stack layers four speculative assumptions: 25% utilization, $0.15/kWh electricity, chip-tier watt heuristics, and an aspirational paid floor. The sku-econ network-harness lane shipped in PR #419 surfaced the structural incompatibility on 2026-07-06: at `default` rate-card fallback pricing ($1.00/M completion) three hardware tiers appeared to pass the paid threshold, but that pricing is a billing catch-all, not production pricing; at intended production pricing (nemotron at qwen3-coder parity $0.235/M) no tier clears $0.005/hr; at RESEARCH_227-methodology-correct nemotron ($0.160/M) earnings fall further. **This entry locks the pivot:** replace hourly-net projection with per-token payout recommendation semantics (two tiers only — recommended when a catalog row passes §5 gates + rate-card + demand-rank.recommendable; donor when none fit). Scoring becomes `demand_weight × completion_rate_per_mtok × measured_sustained_tps` with no utilization guess, electricity subtraction, or paid threshold. RESEARCH_222/224/226/227 market-pegged pricing methodology is preserved; no subsidy. **Nemotron correction in this PR:** revert Entry 111's qwen3-coder parity ($0.235/M completion) to RESEARCH_227 broad-fleet lane ($0.160/M — 20% undercut vs $0.200/M OpenRouter cheapest DeepInfra/Novita); prompt credits $0.080/M (50% of completion), cache-hit $0.020/M (25% of prompt). PR #419's starter-tier engine change is superseded by follow-up PR 2; do not delete starter code in this PR. | **Per-token payout aligns provider UX with buyer and coordinator billing units.** Hourly projection was structurally incompatible with market-pegged rates at realistic utilization. Nemotron was mis-laned at developer-coding parity; RESEARCH_227 Table A ranks it broad-fleet at $0.160/M. This PR is documentation lock + coordinator config only; engine/harness/SPEC-023 changes land in PRs 2–3. | **Effective on merge of `fix/rate-card-v4-pr1` and Pearl deploy.** Verify: `curl -s https://coordinator.streamvc.live/v1/rate-card | jq '.rows["nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b"].completion_rate_per_mtok'` returns `160000`. Deploy via `ALLOW_CONFIG_DRIFT=1 FORCE_RESTART=1 bash phase4-coordinator/dist/deploy-pearl-vps.sh` with live gateway/catalog paths. Re-trigger this entry if PR 2–4 fail to land, if live nemotron ledger rows still show 235000 completion rate after deploy, or if sku-econ harness re-run post-PR-3 does not pass under the new two-tier vocabulary. **Owner: operator.** |

| 2026-07-06 | **Entry 117 — SPEC-023 v0.5 payout-first scoring: rank eligible rows by provider economics, demote TPS/demand to tiebreakers, suspend diversification pool for beta.** v0.4 scored `demand_weight × completion_rate_per_mtok × sustained_tps`, which optimized buyer throughput over what providers earn per token. Beta supply growth needs install recommendations that maximize provider payout first: `(a)` §5 gates (can run), `(b)` payout per completion token (`completion_rate_per_mtok × provider_share`), `(c)` buyer QoS last (`sustained_tps`, then `demand_weight`, then model key). | 2026-07-06 | **Entry 118 — Terminal `stream_output_exceeded` SSE errors do not bill completion prefixes.** Issue #444 surfaced the residual path after #436: a buyer can receive HTTP 200 and streamed bytes, then the gateway or coordinator terminates with `stream_output_exceeded`; the old fallback charged the last byte-estimated completion prefix, often the full `max_tokens` cap, even though the buyer-visible stream is terminal-error output rather than a verified completion. | **Terminal error is not a billable completion prefix.** Legacy gateway streams with no coordinator finality declaration settle prompt-only; SPEC-022-declared gateway streams are held for reconciler finality instead of being locally finalized; coordinator-local output-cap termination records byte-estimated settlement evidence with zero billable input/output and zero provider credits, even if provider usage appeared before the cap breach. Provider-reported clean usage and ordinary unverified streaming fallback behavior stay unchanged. | **Effective on merge of `fix/444-stream-output-overbill`.** Re-trigger if the network harness sees positive completion billing on buyer-visible `stream_output_exceeded`, if SPEC-022 defines a non-zero verified-prefix accounting rule, or if SDK compatibility requires distinguishing terminal-error prefix accounting by client parser. |

| 2026-07-06 | **Entry 119 — KV-cache reuse telemetry is provider-local request-completion observability, not a buyer API or billing contract change.** Issue #376 adds a structured `kv_cache_request_completed` stderr event on successful provider completions in both local HTTP and coordinator relay paths. The event carries `request_id`, `provider_id`, `model_id`, `stream`, `finish_reason`, `prompt_tokens`, `cached_prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, `kv_cache_reuse_ratio`, and `kv_cache_bytes_reused`. The ratio is derived as `cached_prompt_tokens / prompt_tokens` after the existing `CompletionResult` clamp. Because the current MLX cache layer API used by this binary exposes offsets/trimmability but not stable cache memory size, `kv_cache_bytes_reused` is explicitly the UTF-8 byte length of the decoded cached prompt prefix represented by the reused tokens. Speculative-decoding branches report zero KV-cache reuse in this telemetry version because they do not reuse the conversation-cache layer. | **This preserves the SPEC-024 trust boundary.** The provider already reports `cached_prompt_tokens` in OpenAI-style usage; the new metrics only make per-request production backfill measurable for B1 idle-prewarm analysis and leave buyer response shape, coordinator settlement, payout, and prefix-cache discount authority unchanged. Emitting only after a successful non-cancelled `CompletionResult` avoids counting aborted, buyer-cancelled, or failed generations as reuse wins. | **Effective on merge of `fix/376-kv-cache-reuse-telemetry`.** Backfill 24h of M5 production logs after deploy and answer whether reuse stays above 40% with B1 idle-prewarm active. Re-trigger if B1 prewarm evicts cache entries enough to drive reuse below 40%, if MLX exposes a true KV-cache byte-size API that should replace decoded-prefix bytes, if buyer usage fields are changed to include these telemetry names, if speculative decoding starts sharing conversation-cache state, or if coordinator billing starts trusting provider-local telemetry directly. **Owner: operator.** |

Adopt Keryx **OPoI liveness shape** via existing pool canaries (v0), explicit WS (v1, future), tier multiplier (v1.1, future, money-path PR required). OPoI v0 = enable `canary_enabled: true` in coordinator with 2-entry challenge bank; every challenge embeds `{nonce}` (4-byte random hex, upper-case) in both prompt and expected; server validates at `buildCanaryBodyFromRandom:2216-2217`. Config landed in `feat/opoi-v0-canaries`: `coordinator.yaml.example` (enabled), `dist/coordinator.yaml.example` (disabled, challenge bank commented for reference), new `coordinator.opoi-v0-staging.yaml` staging overlay. Formal OPoI v1 (explicit `opoi_challenge` WS frames) and v1.1 (rate-card tier multiplier) are future work; v1.1 is money-path and requires PR + audit loop. | **Effective on merge of `feat/opoi-v0-canaries`.** Canary test suite: `go test ./internal/ws/ ./internal/config/ ./internal/pool/ -run Canary` — 10 tests PASS. Re-trigger this entry if v1 explicit WS ships, if Pearl staging validation reveals MLX false-fail rates requiring template adjustment, or if tier multiplier (v1.1) PR is opened. **Owner: operator (Pearl staging deploy + log monitoring).** |

| 2026-07-08 | **Entry 121 — Live Qwen3 Coder capacity/config: admitted model keys are the coordinator probe contract, and drift uses served-window samples.** Pearl live validation showed three mismatches after publishing the Qwen3 Coder candidate feed: the static feed TTFT gate was too tight for verified local hardware evidence, the Tier2 catalog compared against a stale snapshot artifact hash rather than the runtime manifest hash, and coordinator-owned warmup/canary probes used the catalog MLX model ID while the provider OpenAI endpoint serves the admitted rate-card key. This entry locks the fix: Qwen3 Coder `max_4k_ttft_ms=3500` in the signed feed and baked fallback; Tier2 Qwen3 Coder live catalog row uses runtime artifact-manifest hash `5a0de156...`; warmup/canary probes prefer `MaxAdmittedModelKey` and fall back to `ModelID`; telemetry drift evaluates rolling served-window TPS only after at least two served requests, and suppresses artifact drift when Tier2 hash status is verified. | **Probe model strings are provider-facing, not catalog-facing.** The signed catalog still owns admission/rate-card identity; once admitted, coordinator probes must speak the served key. Single-request TPS windows, especially warmup/canary probes, are not statistically meaningful drift evidence. Verified Tier2 runtime hash is authoritative for live routing even if older hardware evidence stored a different artifact hash namespace. | **Effective on Pearl deploy `bc04aba5d096f3a71ba08600212e892e8bc1dea000ed86a7bb4910fde622d903` and static feed SHA `c1e9b5fb8ce5ef1db805136055a6c9bfb02c0f91ad65d9564a1681b9256915dd`.** Re-trigger if provider endpoints start accepting catalog IDs directly, if heartbeat telemetry gains token-count windows for better TPS sampling, if Tier2 catalog hash scope changes again, or if canary/drift logs show any C/H/M false positives after the final restart. |

| 2026-07-09 | **Entry 122 — Non-terminal streaming usage frames are not buyer settlement authority.** Live SPEC-029 sustained-throughput harness on PR #514's deployed gateway produced three HTTP 200 streams that emitted a usage-only SSE frame, then stalled until the buyer timed out; gateway settled each as `client_disconnect` with 102 completion tokens even though the harness received zero completion tokens and no `[DONE]`. This entry locks the fix: `client_disconnect` and gateway-authored stream idle timeout settlement use gateway-observed content bytes, not provider-reported usage from a non-terminal stream, and the gateway emits a terminal `provider_timeout` SSE + `[DONE]` after `timeouts.streaming_idle_ms` (default 10s) of post-first-byte upstream silence. | **A usage frame without terminal stream closure is evidence, not authority.** Clean streams that reach `[DONE]` may still trust plausible provider usage; truncated upstream failure paths keep their existing settlement semantics. Buyer-disconnect and idle-timeout paths are different because the buyer did not receive a complete stream and cannot reconcile usage against delivered content. | **Effective on merge of `fix/stream-timeout-overbill` plus Pearl gateway deploy.** Re-trigger if I3/I4 fail again on live harness artifacts, if OpenAI-compatible clients need a different terminal timeout code, if SPEC-022 defines verified prefix billing for non-terminal streams, or if the read-idle timeout causes false positives on legitimate providers. |

| 2026-07-09 | **Entry 123 — Legacy pre-stream 502 provider errors are audit-only, not prompt-billable.** Live SPEC-029 sustained-throughput harness after PR #518 exposed two streaming requests that never delivered model output and returned HTTP 502 `provider_error` before any stream body. Gateway usage rows recorded `prompt_tokens=42`, `completion_tokens=0`, `outcome=upstream_error`, which satisfied audit joinability but still charged buyers for a provider-side pre-stream failure. This entry locks the fix: only coordinator responses with HTTP 502, OpenAI error code `provider_error` / `provider_failed` / `provider_disconnected`, and no settlement-finality headers get a zero-token `upstream_error` audit row plus reservation refund while preserving the sanitized buyer-facing 502 `upstream_provider_error`. | **No-output provider failures stay auditable without becoming billable.** The policy is intentionally narrower than "all upstream 5xx": observe-mode settlement headers, SPEC-022 finality headers, non-provider 502 codes, 504 provider timeouts, null-usage selected-provider errors, validation/idempotency/no-provider paths, and clean stream completions keep their existing semantics. | **Effective on merge of `fix/prestream-502-prompt-charge` plus Pearl gateway deploy.** Re-trigger if live I2 loses a usage/audit row for 5xx, if I3 shows prompt or completion charge on pre-stream 502 provider-error requests, if observe-mode 502 provider errors stop following legacy settlement, or if SPEC-022 defines explicit non-billable finality for this case. |

| 2026-07-10 | **Entry 124 — Canary completeness budget raised to prevent false provider degradation.** Issue #528 showed the 16-token canary cap intermittently truncating the nonce challenge when the model emitted a short preamble, producing `canary_fail_reason: incomplete` and transiently removing the sole 30B provider from routing. The fix is a 32-token baseline in the coordinator default, canary examples, and the Pearl staging overlay; the 30B model-class example remains the deterministic `CANARY-{nonce}` challenge. | **Canary correctness must not depend on a model emitting zero preamble tokens.** The nonce gate and incomplete-response classification remain unchanged; only the bounded completion budget and deployment configuration change. | **Effective on merge of `fix/528-canary-incomplete` plus Pearl overlay restart.** Re-trigger if live logs show `canary_fail_reason: incomplete`, if a new model-class challenge needs more than 32 output tokens, or if the Pearl overlay drifts from the repository baseline. |

| 2026-07-10 | **Entry 125 — Pearl exact-match canaries return to staging, and operator rejection gains an authenticated recovery path.** Production Qwen3 Coder repeatedly transformed an alphanumeric nonce despite deterministic sampling and stricter prompts. The provisional canary trip copied that false positive into durable admission rejection, but the coordinator exposed only reject—not unreject—so the provider could never reconnect to receive the already-built self-update. The hotfix adds idempotent `DELETE /admin/reject/{provider_id}` to clear admission and canary sanction state, updates Pearl's advertised provider version to `1.8.26`, and disables the unvalidated production canary overlay. | **An unvalidated challenge bank must not be allowed to create an operator-only deadlock on the sole provider.** Exact matching remains strict; recovery is explicit, operator-authenticated, provider-scoped, and durable. Warmup plus buyer-path inference remain the production liveness gates while canary templates are revalidated in staging. | **Effective on hotfix deploy to Pearl and recovery of provider `mac`.** Re-trigger if a cleared rejection reappears after restart, if Pearl advertises a provider version older than the release, or before canaries are re-enabled without per-model staging evidence and a bounded false-positive rate. **Owner: operator.** |

| 2026-07-10 | **Entry 126 — The external buyer canary is both the deploy gate and the dead-man paging authority.** An attempt is healthy only when the gateway is reachable, at least one model is probed, every model is serviceable, every configured TTFT/TPS sample succeeds, all cache probes succeed, TTFT p95 is at most 7000 ms, decode TPS p50 is at least 15, cache reuse is at least 0.1, and none of those signals is missing. The production systemd unit requires a heartbeat URL, treats heartbeat delivery failure as a failed run, and may retry one complete strict attempt after bounded backoff to ride through the mandatory AEAD session handoff; a partial attempt itself never becomes healthy. | **HTTP 200 and one produced token are not sufficient rollout evidence.** The 2026-07-10 incident showed that internal exact-match provider canaries can false-sanction a model, while the existing external probe could still exit zero during severe performance or missing-signal regressions and could run forever without paging. Keep internal model-integrity challenges in staging; reuse the buyer canary for scheduled monitoring and signed-updater rollback decisions. | **Effective on merge of `fix/524-canary-deploy-gate` and Pearl heartbeat configuration.** Re-trigger if per-model SLOs need class-specific thresholds, if a scheduled run overlaps a controlled rollout without maintenance suppression, or if heartbeat delivery becomes a material source of false rollback. **Owner: operator.** |

Docs-only; no money-path code touched; both spec-index CI gates green. | 2026-07-10 | **Entry 128 — Installer admission is a recoverable transaction, while a bootstrapped provider identity is durable security state.** A failed install restores the previous binary, resources, autotune recommendation, launchd/watchdog state, and exact foreground invocation, but it must retain any newly persisted `mp-*` provider ID and bearer because the coordinator may already have confirmed that identity. Foreground replay records exact argv, working directory, and environment; replacement begins only after the failed new process is proven dead through bounded TERM/KILL handling; successful commit atomically retires the recovery bundle before best-effort deletion. | **Coordinator admission must enforce the same durability boundary.** A provider with a durable bootstrap identity cannot downgrade to v1 and must prove the original receipt key over v2 even when the optional App-track identity-policy store is disabled. Verified hardware evidence is checked again after the v2 challenge before any durable admission record, token confirmation, or registration, and startup smoke exercises the migration-017 hardware tuple grants used by that gate. Rowless legacy `mp-*` principals retain v1 compatibility. | **Effective only after merge and deployment of `fix/installer-hardware-evidence`; no production activation is implied by this entry.** Re-trigger if bootstrap credentials move out of config/Keychain, rollback gains another process supervisor or freshness-bearing artifact, v1 is removed globally, hardware evidence becomes versioned/CAS-bound, or recovery data cleanup changes its atomic-retirement boundary. **Owner: operator.** |

| 2026-07-10 | **Entry 129 — Invalid installer bootstrap bearers recover only through the original retained receipt identity.** A provider that receives an explicit coordinator `invalid_token` rejection may open a bearerless credential-bootstrap handshake only when its provider ID has the strict installer `mp-*` shape, the corresponding Keychain signing key is available, and the CLI is not running as a Malibu-managed child. The coordinator retains an unconfirmed receipt-key binding for a configurable seven-day window after token expiry and may replace an expired or otherwise unused bootstrap token only for that exact key during that window; a confirmed or operator-revoked bootstrap token, a different key, any historical ordinary operator identity (including a revoked or pruned credential), or a network failure leaves local credential state unchanged and fails closed. Both plain and GitHub-OAuth ordinary issuance reject an existing bootstrap binding transactionally. Operators enumerate retained bindings with `coordinator-cli list-bootstrap-identities` and tombstone abandoned provider IDs with `coordinator-cli revoke-bootstrap-identity`, which also revokes any active bootstrap bearer. | **Token TTL bounds a live lease; identity retention bounds recovery and unauthenticated storage separately.** Live unconfirmed identities remain capped, while the global hourly mint limit multiplied by the finite retention window bounds never-admitted custody growth without letting expired rows permanently exhaust onboarding. After retention, a never-admitted random 128-bit provider ID is collected; confirmed identities and operator tombstones remain durable. Pruning invalidates stale ordinary installer credentials but retains their ownership tombstones, so routine maintenance cannot silently convert an operator-managed principal into a bootstrap-claimable one. Admission also evaluates hardware evidence before token minting, and V1 records provisional capacity only after its fallible hardware gate, so rejected attempts cannot orphan credentials or exhaust pending capacity. | **Effective only after merge and deployment of `fix/installer-hardware-evidence`; no token rotation, release, or production activation is performed by this decision record.** Re-trigger if the coordinator changes invalid-token close semantics, bootstrap identity storage moves out of Keychain, the recovery-retention or rate limits change, Malibu adopts this CLI recovery path, or recovery becomes available to non-installer principals. **Owner: operator.** |

| 2026-07-11 | **Entry 130 — Pearl backend releases are signed pairs and roll back as one serving transaction.** Coordinator and gateway Linux amd64 binaries now travel through the protected reviewed-main release build and signer with signed component metadata. A root-owned Pearl updater pins the repository and release key, rejects unsigned, tampered, revoked, downgraded, or incompatible releases, verifies the exact #524 buyer-canary authority, pauses its external dead-man heartbeat, quiesces all backend and database writers, drains traffic, snapshots binaries/configuration/SQLite state, and only then performs atomic replacement. | **Backend rollout success means verified buyer serving, not merely active systemd units.** Coordinator starts before gateway; provider warmup, local and public semantic health, and the external buyer canary gate success. A fsynced phase journal, transaction-bound systemd permits, ordered rollback, boot reconciliation, and an independent Gmail failure channel preserve recoverability across process death and reboot. The updater never enables staging-only model-integrity canaries or clears provider sanctions; #538 recovery remains operator-only. | **Disabled by default.** Enable only after a manual plan, successful apply, simulated failed-rollout rollback, and interrupted-transaction reconciliation drill. Re-trigger on release-key rotation, component-version divergence, a new database writer or path, Better Stack API changes, #524 authority changes, or any buyer-canary false positive. **Owner: operator.** |

| 2026-07-11 | **Entry 131 — Release candidates prove the complete unsigned build before an immutable production tag is created.** The manual release workflow may admit an absent tag only in its unprivileged, secret-free build job when the explicit `candidate` boolean is true. That job still requires the dispatch ref, `GITHUB_SHA`, checked-out `HEAD`, and freshly fetched `origin/main` to identify one exact reviewed commit; if the tag already exists, it must target that commit. The protected signing and publication job never accepts tag absence and repeats the exact-tag gate before restoring captured inputs, creating the numeric draft, and changing it to public. v1.8.30 also pins `mlx-swift` 0.31.4 at `dc43e62d7055353c7f99fa071a4e71d29dfddc44`, the last reviewed pin compatible with the release runner's Swift 6.1 toolchain. | **Tags are production identities, not build probes.** A candidate dispatch first compiles and uploads all unsigned inputs from reviewed `main`; only after that build succeeds may the operator create annotated tag `v1.8.30` at the exact captured commit and approve the protected signer. This preserves immutable-tag and same-artifact guarantees while moving dependency, compiler, packaging, and cohesion failures ahead of tagging. The version, Malibu build 30, release-build ledger, and coordinator advertisement advance as one reviewed unit; this PR does not itself tag, publish, install, or activate production. Performance evidence collected with mlx-swift 0.31.6 is not silently carried across the compatibility pin and must be revalidated before making new throughput claims. | **Effective only after merge, an annotated `v1.8.30` tag at the resulting fresh `origin/main` tip, and a successful protected manual Release workflow.** Re-trigger on tag-target drift, workflow/appcast mismatch, release-runner toolchain changes, dependency pin changes, configuration or ledger drift, published-binary version divergence, or a throughput regression against the 0.31.6 baseline. Record actual merge SHA, tag, and workflow evidence only after they exist. **Owner: operator.** |
| 2026-07-11 | **Entry 132 — The coordinator canary is a liveness / instruction-following / freshness probe, not a model-identity or anti-downgrade proof (SPEC-031, #564).** The nonce echo travels in the clear inside an ordinary fingerprintable request, so any cheaper or substituted model — or a canary-aware string handler — can return it without running the admitted weights. SPEC-031 reconstructs the previously-unspecced canary → degrade/ban → recovery subsystem (behind three 2026-07-09/10 incidents) and explicitly defers all weight-integrity claims to SPEC-008 (attestation) and the item-9 proof-of-weights/OPoI baseline. It pins: the six-constant failure taxonomy; observe-mandatory latency gates (the non-streaming metric measured 125ms–7000ms for one healthy provider); sole-provider protection (a canary-only signal never empties a model's pool); Sybil-safe correlated-fault containment (a correlation epoch stages results, discards them on a correlated majority + alerts, and creates no persistent provider-triggerable containment state — all durable containment is an authenticated operator action); the buyer 503+`retryable:true` contract; crash-consistent fail-closed sanction persistence; degrade-cause composition with the FR-P11a breaker; and a no-restart config-reload requirement. | **A probe's security claim must match what it actually proves.** Calling an overt nonce-echo an "anti-downgrade identity proof" would let a downgraded sole provider escape sanction while a healthy provider is falsely sanctioned for prompt-following variation — the exact failure the three incidents exercised. §14 is an honest conformance table (Implemented / Tightens / Partial / Gap) against the shipped Go source; the subsystem is **disabled in production** and the §16 re-enable bar (FR-CAN22/23 sole-provider + correlated-fault, FR-CAN15 sanction-load-decoupled-from-`canary_enabled`, FR-CAN18 crash-consistent persistence, FR-CAN14 degrade-cause composition, FR-CAN26 no-restart tuning, and `observe`-until-FR-CAN8) is the recommended pre-re-enable work. The correlated-fault design converged through 9 codex three-lane rounds (quorum → known-good-control → fingerprint-suspend → ephemeral-discard) precisely because any persistent automatic response a provider majority can trigger is weaponizable. | **Effective on merge of `spec/031-canary-degrade-sanctions` (#564, squash c4a98ae).** Docs-only + two comment-only `internal/ws/canary_probe.go` edits; no runtime behavior change. Re-trigger when the §16 re-enable Gaps are implemented (each is a code change that flips a §14 row and needs its own audit + this log), when item 9's proof-of-weights baseline lands (it owns the weight-integrity claim SPEC-031 declines), or if a future version re-introduces automatic challenge-bank rollback (only behind an authenticated, single-flight, generation-fenced, deterministic-only control). **Owner: operator.** |

| 2026-07-11 | **Entry 133 — Signed catalogs and provider v1.8.31/build 31 advance under shared release-transaction authorities.** Candidate and demand bytes, sidecars, manifest, generated baked resources, model artifact identities, coordinator and gateway binaries, and their advertised provider version form one complete release payload. The signed Pearl updater is the activation authority for the binary pair plus catalog `current`/`previous` pointers; every Pearl writer serializes through one shared mutation lock. On each Mac, installer, manual update, coordinator autoupdate, Malibu, watchdog, and recovery paths likewise share one mutation-ownership boundary. Provider config remains the sole mutable runtime authority, and binary, resources, config, plist, recommendation state, and service state remain one rollback transaction. | **A valid signature on stale bytes is not fleet consistency, process health is not buyer-serving capacity, and two individually correct update engines are unsafe when they can mutate the same graph concurrently.** The July 6–10 incident proved that independently advancing baked/static/coordinator identities, raw whole-feed evidence hashes, plist overrides, partial release payloads, and unrelated locks can remove providers despite each local check passing. Upgrade success therefore requires the coordinator-authoritative exact-provider verdict `buyer_serving:true` with `catalog_admission_mode:current|previous`; a busy provider may satisfy that serving-capacity verdict even though it has no free slot and is not immediately routing eligible. | **Effective with `ops/runbooks/catalog-release-provider-upgrade.md` and SPEC-020 v0.1.6.** First activate the signed backend-pair-plus-catalog coordinator in explicit compatibility mode so legacy providers remain capacity. Then upgrade v1.8.31 provider cohorts; catalog-aware providers commit only on authoritative current-or-previous buyer serving. After telemetry proves zero legacy bridge sessions, enable enforcement and restart so every session re-admits strictly. There is no legacy-coordinator success exception. Do not advertise v1.8.31 until its version, build 31 ledger, complete payload, rollback graph, and current-or-previous buyer-serving canary agree. Re-trigger on same-version/different-bytes publication, coordinator/provider release mismatch, concurrent mutation, bridge deadline breach, update rollback, or unexplained model concentration. **Owner: operator.** |

| 2026-07-11 | **Entry 134 — The autotune hello-gate is a hardware-evidence admission control; "OPoI"/`proof_of_weights` is observability-only and must never gate routing, tiering, sanctions, or payout (SPEC-032, #566).** SPEC-032 reconstructs two previously-unspecced coordinator trust signals. **Part A — the autotune hello-gate (LIVE in prod: `require_autotune_hello_gate:true`, `pool_size:1`):** a hardware-evidence admission gate that refuses a provider unless it presents fresh, verified benchmark evidence proving it can serve the tier it claims (this is the gate that closed the intended second provider in the 2026-07-10 transient-degrade incident). It pins the evidence requirement, the capacity ceiling, the five-reason close taxonomy (evidence-absent / no-passing-benchmark / policy-unverifiable), and the pool-redundancy policy. v0.1 ships **no automatic probationary admission** — the gate stays strict; redundancy is a below-two operator alert + a hot-reloadable gate, and auto-probation is deferred behind a full documented constraint set. **Part B/C — OPoI / proof-of-weights / telemetry-drift (specced, disabled):** the check is the identical plaintext-nonce echo SPEC-031 already showed is **not** a weight proof; labeled non-binding / observability-only, with a real weight-integrity proof (statistical/attested) deferred to **FR-PW3**, and telemetry-drift pinned at an alert-only ceiling scoped to the `pow.Evaluator` so SPEC-008's authoritative `hash_mismatch`/`hash_invalid` routing exclusion is preserved. | **A trust signal's authority must match what it actually proves, and a hardware-admission gate must keep enforcing after admission.** The shipped gate does **NOT** enforce its capacity ceiling after the hello handshake — a provider can pass on a small model and heartbeat-switch to a large or uncatalogued one, bypassing the gate entirely (**FR-HG7**, a live prod bypass and the priority remediation). The gate evidence is bearer-authenticated + trust-bound, not "signed" (only the *catalog* is signed) — the earlier "signed benchmark" framing was an over-claim. A stale runbook still directed an OPoI-pass-streak **payout multiplier**, a direct FR-PW2 money-path violation; that runbook is banner-marked superseded and the multiplier voided. §14 is an honest Implemented/Tightens/Partial/Gap table against the Go source; OPoI/drift stay observability-only until an FR-PW3 test exists. | **Effective on merge of `spec/032-proof-of-weights-hello-gate` (#566, squash c1909cb).** Docs-only; the only non-spec edits are comment/doc-only (a `canary_probe.go`/`server.go` OPoI comment, a staging-YAML comment, two superseded runbooks, a runbook item-10 `v1`→`v2` anchor fix); no coordinator behavior change. Re-trigger when **FR-HG7** ceiling enforcement is wired into routing (a code change that flips a §14 row and needs its own audit + this log), when a real **FR-PW3** weight-binding proof is designed, or if any OPoI/drift signal is ever proposed to gate routing, tiering, sanctions, or payout. **Owner: operator.** |

| 2026-07-12 | **Entry 135 — "Attested" is a verified session-bound attestation token, NOT proof of trusted hardware; the shipped default is self-signed Secure-Enclave key-custody, and the buyer-visible `hardware_attestation` field currently overstates it (SPEC-008 v0.4, #567).** SPEC-008 v0.3 documented only the aspirational Apple-MDA path and omitted the entire shipped, default-enabled self-signed Secure-Enclave format (`macprovider-se-p256-v1`). v0.4 documents it byte-for-byte (unpadded-base64url JSON envelope, DER/ES256 body signature, raw 64-byte x‖y key with IsOnCurve, Go `encoding/json` canonicalization NOT JCS, the fixed 10-field `attestation-binding/v1` session-binding signature required for **both** SE and MDA, the SE liveness re-challenge, the path-dependent MDA parsing + acceptance grammar, and the effective 1024-byte WS object cap that shadows the Pillar-C 16/20-KiB caps). It scopes trust honestly: `self_signed` proves key-custody + session binding only — not Secure-Enclave custody, Apple-Silicon, or device identity (`hardware_family` is an unauthenticated self-assertion); the foundational definitions (§1.1/§3/§7/§9) were broadened off "hardware attestation". | **A trust-disclosure field's name must match what it proves, and a reconciliation's scope must be honest about what it did not touch.** The gateway maps the tier-blind `attested` aggregate straight to the buyer-visible `hardware_attestation`, so a pool of self-signed **software-key** providers is disclosed as `hardware_attestation:"all"` — a hardware claim the SE path cannot back. v0.4 documents this as a known overstatement (buyers/consumers MUST NOT read it as hardware proof) and `require_attestation` admits `self_signed`; the **forward code fix** is to gate the field on a genuine `hardware` tier or rename it. The 14-round three-lane audit (security + architect returned 'reconciliation complete'; code-fidelity byte-faithful) caught the dual-format binding-signature requirement and the 1024-byte cap that a lighter review would have shipped wrong. | **Effective on merge of `spec/008-v0.4-attestation-reconcile` (#567, squash db6289e).** Docs-only; no code change (the Go is the live cross-repo interop contract the Swift CLI byte-matches). Re-trigger when the `hardware_attestation` field is gated on a real hardware tier / renamed (a coordinator+gateway code change that needs its own audit + this log), and for the **separate SPEC-008 Pillar-B/D reconciliation** carrying the attribution-verified pre-existing drifts (encrypted-leg optionality/reload, Pillar-D UTF-8/output-cap, buyer error-template wording) surfaced but not touched here. Bundled the SPEC-031 FR-CAN23 correction (`attested` proves neither device identity nor independent ownership). **Owner: operator.** |
| 2026-07-12 | **Entry 136 — The provider IS allowed to see the derived, opaque, account-scoped `conversation_key`; option (a) ratifies the shipped prefix-cache design under an explicit privacy disclosure (SPEC-024 v0.2, #568).** Provider-local prefix caching needs a stable per-conversation key at the provider, so the shipped coordinator sends the derived `conv:` key to the provider (cleartext relay top-level field; inside the Tier-2 `inference_request_plaintext` encrypted envelope). This tensioned the prior SPEC-004 FR-SR-2 / SPEC-006 / SPEC-008 invariant that the provider cannot see `conv:`. **Operator decision (2026-07-12): option (a) — ratify the shipped design**, amending SPEC-004 v0.3.2 (FR-SR-2 carve-out), SPEC-006 v0.9.8 (survivability invariant (b) narrowed), SPEC-008 v0.4.1 (§2.2 invariant (b) narrowed; §6.6/§10.6 envelope). The disclosed trade-off: the provider learns a **stable opaque per-conversation identifier** (the cross-turn correlation sticky affinity already grants — and, since the key is forwarded on misses/re-routes, potentially across >1 provider) but **cannot** recover the raw buyer tag or `account_id` (one-way HMAC), and captured network frames carry no raw values (Pillar B survivability intact). | **When shipped behavior contradicts a spec invariant, decide explicitly — ratify or fix — and if ratifying, document every consequence honestly rather than paper over it.** The 7-round three-lane audit caught two things a lighter review ships wrong: (1) the encrypted-leg byte contract (§6.6/§10.6 claimed the ciphertext decrypts to the bare v1 `body`; the shipped Tier-2 encrypts a JSON `inference_request_plaintext` envelope that also carries `conversation_key` — a conforming reimplementation would have broken interop); (2) an overstated buyer-deletion control — the first draft claimed `DELETE /v1/sticky` severs provider-cache reuse, but the derived key is deterministic and post-delete normal selection can re-populate the same provider, so the provider-local KV is **not** buyer-purgeable (only bounded by its own TTL/LRU). Corrected to honest residuals. | **Effective on merge of `spec/024-v0.2-cache-isolation` (#568, squash e990f00).** Docs-only; no code change (documents shipped Swift-provider + Go-coordinator + gateway behavior). Carries MUST-close forward items: **FR-CI10a** (buyer disclosure does not yet cover provider receipt/retention + cross-provider linkability), **FR-CI11a** (slot-queue path can carry a stale `sticky_result=="hit"` → discount after a non-sticky final selection), and **FR-CI13** (sticky read-path compares only key+candidacy, not `account_id`) — all billing-attribution/disclosure items gated by the account-scoped HMAC, not isolation breaches. Re-trigger if any of these forward items is implemented in code (needs its own audit + this log) or if the provider-visibility posture is ever revisited. **Owner: operator.** |
| 2026-07-12 | **Entry 137 — A provider CANNOT self-certify hardware, but `verified` revocation is best-effort with a real `app_register` escape; SPEC-033 documents-and-defers the fix rather than blocking the spec (SPEC-033, #569).** SPEC-033 is the first canonical spec for the shipped `hardware-verifier.v2` verdict engine. The load-bearing security property **holds**: no provider-reachable path sets `provider_hardware_profiles.verified=TRUE` (column grants exclude `verified`, trigger A forces FALSE on insert/chip/memory-change, and the migration-016 trigger re-verifies the full trust join *in the database* on every verifier promotion) — a provider without an operator-curated trust row reaches at best `waiting_trust`. But three properties are **weaker than a naive reading assumes**, now documented honestly: (i) `verified` is **string-consistency + an operator trust anchor, not benchmark authenticity** — no signature/proof-of-possession/artifact verification, so a provider holding a trusted identity can attach it to fabricated benchmarks; (ii) `verified` is **(chip, memory)-anchored, not identity-anchored**; (iii) **revocation is best-effort** — the `app_register` source-flip escape (**R1**): a verified provider re-registers the same chip/memory, flipping `source` away from `cli_hello`, and the demotion job only touches `source='cli_hello'` while downstream (SPEC-032 admission, SPEC-017 cache) gate on the `verified` bit without rechecking source/trust; a **compromised** `provider_onboarding` SQL role can run this at cross-provider scale (it holds column `UPDATE` on `source`). | **Document the shipped security posture precisely — including its gaps — instead of asserting guarantees the code doesn't provide; a reconstruction spec's job is truth, and a real gap becomes a tracked forward item, not a blocker.** The 6-round three-lane audit twice caught the draft **overstating** security (an early "only the verifier writes `verified`" ignored the operator inventory writer; an early "revocation guaranteed by trust removal" ignored R1) and once **overstating a gap** (R2 "cannot reach zero trust" — actually reachable via an expired-placeholder identity). The spec now byte-matches the code and states R1 as **MUST-close**. Per the spec-drift discipline (a NOASSERTION/shipped-reality reconstruction), the spec ships documenting R1; the code fix is deferred. | **Effective on merge of `spec/hardware-verifier-v2` (#569, squash a7225c9).** Docs-only; no code change (the Go + migrations are the source of truth). **Re-trigger (MUST-close R1):** wire a code fix so revocation cannot be escaped — e.g. scope demotion beyond `source='cli_hello'`, or have SPEC-032 admission / SPEC-017 cache re-check current active trust rather than the standing `verified` bit — which flips the §10.4 R1 disclosure and needs its own audit + this log. Also re-trigger if a real benchmark-execution-authenticity proof is designed (would strengthen §10.2). **Owner: operator.** |
| 2026-07-13 | **Entry 138 — Mandatory Pillar-B request/age rotation uses a single-WebSocket, same-assigned-ID, in-band fresh-key handoff instead of close/reconnect (#540; SPEC-008 v0.5; SPEC-001 v1.8).** Once rotation is due, one atomic coordinator gate stops old-epoch encrypted-frame admission while allowing every already-admitted inference and control probe to finish or expire. The coordinator and provider then exchange fresh X25519 keys and prove both candidate directions with sequence-0 AEAD commit/ack frames; only an exact `(provider_id, assigned_id, old_kid)` generation match can atomically install the epoch, and the first post-commit encrypted frame starts at sequence 1. During a healthy handoff the provider remains ready/published, sticky/model/attestation/catalog state remains intact, and a bounded set of waiting buyer dispatches retain their own context instead of receiving rekey-caused `no_provider_available`; overflow receives explicit relay backpressure rather than unbounded coordinator resource retention. | **The outage was lifecycle downtime, not cryptographic necessity.** Closing the sole provider before reconnect + warm-up created an approximately eight-second empty pool and buyer 503s. A dual-WebSocket overlap would require exceptions to duplicate-provider eviction plus separate warm-up/session ownership and would create two simultaneously valid provider sessions. The selected protocol keeps one authenticated transport and adds an explicit dispatch/drain barrier plus bidirectional proof of fresh-key possession. Failures remain fail-closed: malformed/expired/mismatched proof, timeout, stale generation, or unsupported capability closes only after old work drains and requires full authentication; counter exhaustion/AEAD compromise remains immediate hard-close. | **Effective for code/spec review on merge; production rollout remains gated by the SPEC-008 AC-B-7 Pearl forced-rekey canary.** Hermetic Go/Swift coverage must prove request and age thresholds, continuous one-provider dispatch, in-flight completion/cancel, context-aware bounded wait, timeout/failure, overlap/mismatch/replay rejection, sequence-0 proof reservation, encrypted-control epoch fencing, and legacy drain-before-close. Before enabling mandatory rotation in Pearl, force both thresholds under continuous traffic and record zero rekey-correlated 503s plus committed/failed audit events. Re-trigger on multi-request provider concurrency, a new transport, AEAD suite change, persisted session keys, assigned-ID semantics, or any proposal to overlap provider sockets. **Owner: coordinator/provider operator.** |
| 2026-07-14 | **Entry 139 — Issue #585 Option 2 begins with CLI-owned provider-bearer custody and a launchd-admission-gated compatibility transaction.** `macprovider-cli` stores the bearer in its own login-Keychain generic-password service and resolves that item ahead of layered config input. The selected slice uses the login Keychain's default designated-requirement ACL, pins the stable release identifier `live.streamvc.macprovider.cli` and release Team ID, forbids interactive Keychain UI, preserves conflicts, redacts machine-owned autotune backups, retains rollback plaintext until the launchd proof succeeds, and makes routine uninstall fail closed unless launchd returns the exact service-not-found result. | **Effective for the first ordered implementation slice of #585; not authorization to roll out the full umbrella.** Hermetic CLI/App tests and code/security/architecture audits gate merge.
