| 2026-07-14 | **Entry 140 — The gateway must not charge the buyer for a pre-dispatch `route_snapshot_failed`; no provider ran, so it is a refund + verbatim passthrough (runbook item 18).** The coordinator emits `route_snapshot_failed` as a `500` BEFORE any provider is dispatched — a SPEC-022 durable route-snapshot write failure (`internal/buyer/route_snapshot.go`), written via plain `writeError` with no settlement finality headers. The gateway's generic upstream-error path settled the reservation on the prompt estimate (`shouldRefundLegacyPreStreamProvider502` only refunds `502`s), so the buyer was **debited for a request no provider ever saw**. New `coordinatorPreDispatchNoChargeError` (gated on `500` + code `route_snapshot_failed` + absence of finality headers) routes both streaming and non-streaming requests through `passThroughNoProviderCoordinatorError`: refund the reservation, write a 0-token refunded audit row, and pass the coordinator body through verbatim. The refund fires ONLY on a **positively-marked** no-provider failure: the coordinator stamps `X-MacProvider-Settlement-No-Prior-Dispatch` centrally (`noPriorDispatchResponseWriter`) on any terminal response written while no provider has been billably credited, and the gateway refunds only when the marker is present AND its own retry-side `priorProviderDispatch` is false AND no finality header. "Credited" is ledger-exact from two recorder signals: `providerCredited` (set inside `recordRow` the instant a provider-bound billable row persists — `providerAssignedID != "" AND status != 503`) covers all attempts that bill before their write (coordinator-internal failover), and `dispatchedThisAttempt && terminalStatus != 503` covers the current terminal attempt, whose billing row is recorded AFTER its terminal write on the WS paths. Everything else settles on the prompt estimate (pre-item-18 behavior, not worse): coordinator-internal failover after a credited provider, gateway retry of an unmarked earlier response (a provider-dispatched `provider_*` 502 or a `no_provider` 503 from failover exhaustion after a billed attempt), and any legacy/rolled-back coordinator. A cold marked 502/503, a queue-full 503, and an admission failure do not poison the refund. | **"No provider was credited" is a REQUEST-WIDE, LEDGER-EXACT property, and the refund gate must be a POSITIVE signal.** Charging for a zero-service request is a money defect; refunding after a billed dispatch is the opposite — a verified-realizable NEW-to-diff undercharge surfaced across five audit rounds + a verification lane (gateway-retry, coordinator-internal failover, failover-exhaustion-503, and the WS write-before-bill ordering variants). The signal cannot be a request-log ordinal: `attemptN` over-counted non-billed 503 rows (over-charge) and incremented AFTER the terminal WS write (under-charge). It must be driven off the actual billing decision inside `recordRow` (`providerCredited`) plus a per-attempt dispatched flag for the terminal attempt whose row lands post-write (`dispatchedThisAttempt`, combined with the write status since `recordRow` bills iff `status != 503`). A positive marker, stamped centrally at write time, makes a **gateway-first deploy or coordinator rollback** safe: an unmarked response is never refunded. | **Effective on merge of `fix/item18-route-snapshot-no-charge` (money-path PR, authored Augustas11).** Coordinator behavior change (central positive no-prior-dispatch marker, derived from ledger-exact `providerCredited` + per-attempt `dispatchedThisAttempt`) + gateway behavior change (retry-loop marker signal + marker-gated settlement branch) + reconciled comments. **DEPLOY ORDER: coordinator before gateway; roll back in reverse.** SPEC-006 v0.9.10 § 17.7 + SPEC-022 item (A) resolved. Codex 3-lane audit (multi-round, R5 surfaced the request-log-ordinal insufficiency → R6 ledger-exact redesign) converged to 0 CRITICAL / 0 HIGH; R6 all three lanes agreed the HIGH is fixed. **Carried MEDIUM (documented, ships not blocked):** on the coordinator's write-before-bill streaming / WS-tunneled terminal paths the marker keys on the outward wire status, so a non-billable 503 rendered as 502 can be left unmarked and a later `route_snapshot_failed` settled instead of refunded — the SAME outcome as pre-item-18 (route_snapshot_failed was always settled), i.e. pre-existing and not worsened. A fully ledger-exact marker on those paths (canonical billing status across every write-before-bill terminal site, or reordering terminal billing before the write) is a tracked follow-up. Re-trigger if another coordinator pre-dispatch no-charge `5xx` code is introduced, if `observe`-mode billing of dispatched-but-failed attempts changes, or if the coordinator attaches settlement finality to `route_snapshot_failed`. **Owner: operator.** |
| 2026-07-14 | **Entry 141 — Local lifecycle evidence is a versioned CLI-authored contract, and credential repair remains a CLI transaction.** `/v1/status` v1 capability-gates buyer-serving, catalog, credential, observation, service-instance, and lifecycle-transition fields. Every response has a short-lived observation ID; every `serve` process has a stable instance ID/PID/boot-session tuple; every provider-state edge has a CLI-authored transition ID/time/reason. Malibu reads and renders those fields, preserves an unversioned legacy path, and suppresses incompatible or unadvertised fields. When HTTP cannot start, the signed installed CLI exposes the same typed redacted custody condition through `credentials status`; `credentials repair` may restore only missing/corrupt custody from an unchanged owner-only regular config source and exact-readback-verifies the Keychain result. | **Process health, a status sample, a provider principal, and a credential are different identities.** Conflating them lets a stale reader treat a replacement process as the prior lifecycle or lets an app become an accidental second mutation authority. The capability/minimum-reader envelope permits additive evolution; distinct observation/instance/transition IDs make freshness and replacement explicit; all credential output excludes bearer and token-derived values. Repair refuses conflicts and access-state failures because automatic overwrite there can turn a legitimate custody incident into identity takeover. | **Effective for the second ordered implementation slice of #585; still not rollout authorization.** Hermetic CLI/App tests and two independent audits gate the slice. The App-Keychain compatibility bearer remains only because shipped earnings calls still require it. Production stays blocked on signed update/reboot/login/locked-Keychain evidence, physical-Mac lifecycle tests, exact launchd instance supervision and leases, the whole compatibility-set transaction, #584 canary, and soak/exemption exit. Re-trigger when local contract v2 changes minimum-reader semantics, a new lifecycle writer appears, repair gains another source, or legacy identity recovery lands. **Owner: provider lifecycle.** |

| 2026-07-14 | **Entry 142 — CLI admission identity is a generation-CAS authority with bounded rollback and exact-bound one-shot dual-control loss recovery.** Routine rotation stages a new login-Keychain key, has the current key sign the complete next-key-bearing v2 transcript, and commits only the coordinator-authoritative generation. Generation CAS, a seven-day previous-key window, exact candidate convergence, one-shot dual control, and redacted live status make interrupted rotation recoverable while keeping the provider ID and bearer ownership unchanged. Recovery-marked attempts cannot fall through to generic temporary exemption. | **Effective for the #585 admission-identity implementation slice; not production activation.** Hermetic Go/Swift tests and independent code/security/architecture audits gate merge. | 2026-07-15 | **Entry 152 — A stale existing-provider upgrade preserves the installed signed model and acquires its exact artifact before cutover.** The staged CLI now supports an explicit allowlisted artifact-only prefetch that validates the signed catalog, hardware gates, pinned revision, and canonical hash without loading a model or starting a benchmark runtime. A mismatched incumbent snapshot is never deleted or replaced: acquisition uses a hash-qualified isolated path. Prefetch writes a private receipt binding catalog digest/version/policy, row identity, model ID, revision, artifact hash, and path. The installer requires the incumbent config's exact `model_catalog_model_id`; the post-stop benchmark accepts only that receipt and performs existing-bytes-only verification, so it cannot download or repair artifacts after cutover. The durable recovery marker also distinguishes staging from live mutation: a pre-cutover failure restores only a suspended watchdog and never boots out the incumbent. Missing, blocked, ineligible, corrupt, untrusted, drifted, or missing-after-prefetch rows fail closed without an artifact download after cutover. Broad catalog retuning remains an explicit maintenance action rather than an implicit software-update side effect. | **Software replacement must not silently become a multi-model acquisition campaign.** The first v1.8.36 provider handoff treated ordinary catalog staleness as permission to iterate the complete catalog, stopped the healthy Qwen provider, and began a fresh multi-gigabyte Gemma download; Pearl's bounded handoff expired before recommendation completed. Increasing the timeout, wiping caches, accepting unverified artifacts, destructively repairing the incumbent snapshot, or allowing any download after cutover were rejected because they weaken availability or trust without bounding the work. | **Triggered by the first real-Mac v1.8.36 installer run after the backend/canary gate reached `provider_install_ready`; the installer was interrupted, its transaction restored the complete v1.8.30 provider state, and Pearl returned to a clean 2/2 fleet.** Release v1.8.37 carries the corrected compatibility set. Regression coverage proves prefetch never invokes the benchmark runtime, preserves a mismatched incumbent snapshot, requires an explicit signed model allowlist and receipt, rejects catalog drift, never invokes the downloader after receipt binding, constrains installer prefetch and apply to the same catalog ID, and proves a pre-cutover failure never boots out the provider. Re-trigger if catalog identity fields, artifact caching, recommendation freshness, installer cutover order, recovery markers, or model-change UX changes. **Owner: provider lifecycle/release operations.** |

| 2026-07-14 | **Entry 163 — A `rate_limit_exceeded`-family 429 with no backoff header and no account-level throttle is classified `retryable:false`, not `true`; and the coordinator's streaming SSE terminal-error writer honors the same provider `retryable` override the non-streaming writer does (runbook item 20).** Two consistency fixes deferred from the #548 r4 audit. (1) `signup_rate_limited` (gateway, `oauth.go createSignupAccount`, a per-IP `SignupAccountsPerIPPerDay` cap over a 24h window) was left `retryable:true` only because the round-3 SECURITY-MEDIUM revert that flipped `feedback_rate_limited` / `oauth_state_rate_limited` / `demo_session_rate_limited` to `false` never named it — it is the same shape (no `Retry-After`/reset header, outside the `/v1/chat/completions` 30-RPS clamp), so it moves to `gatewayPermanentCodes`. (2) SPEC-019 §8 requires the provider's `inference_response_end.retryable` override to reach the buyer; the non-streaming `writeProviderStructuredOutputError` honored it but the streaming SSE synthesis path (`writeSSEError` at the terminal-error site) dropped it, defaulting to the static `spec018Retryable(code)` — so the two transports could disagree on `retryable` for the identical terminal structured-output outcome. New `writeSSEErrorWithRetryable` threads `end.Retryable` through; `writeSSEError` delegates with a nil override (unchanged for every other call site). The override is SCOPED (`isSpec019RetryableOverrideCode`) to exactly the two codes the non-streaming `writeWSEndError` routes through `writeProviderStructuredOutputError` — `malformed_json_response` / `json_schema_validation_failed`; for `response_byte_cap_exceeded` and `provider_timeout` the provider override is IGNORED on both transports so a provider cannot flip a non-retryable cap failure to retryable (their emitted base code/retryability still differs pre-existingly — streaming keeps the literal code + its static default, non-streaming `writeWSEndError` maps them to `provider_error` — which is out of scope here). R1 audit MEDIUM: the first cut honored the override for all four `isSpec019ProviderDetailCode` values, wider than the non-streaming path. | **Classification consistency IS a security property here, and transport symmetry IS a contract property.** A `true` on a headerless, unthrottled 429 tells a conforming SDK to auto-retry with zero backoff — a DoS amplifier, the exact rationale of the round-3 revert; leaving one sibling out was an inconsistency, not a considered exception. And a buyer must see the same `retryable` verdict whether it streamed or not — a provider that marks a specific malformed-output response non-retryable (or retryable) must not have that verdict silently reverted to the static default just because the buyer used `stream:true`. Both were carried as documented follow-ups (SPEC-006 §5.2 "Known carried items" (2), and the signup-header note) rather than blocking #548; item 20 closes them. | **Effective on merge of `fix/item20-signup-retryable-streaming-override` (authored Augustas11).** Buyer-visible behavior change (gateway `signup_rate_limited` envelope now `retryable:false`; coordinator streaming SSE `retryable` now reflects the provider override on structured-output terminal errors). No money-path settlement change. SPEC-006 §5.2 (abuse-limit exception + Overrides + carried-item (2) resolved) and SPEC-019 §8 (streaming SSE writer MUST honor the override) updated. Codex 3-lane audit converged over three rounds → 0 C/H/M (R1: 1 security MEDIUM = streaming override scope wider than non-streaming, fixed by `isSpec019RetryableOverrideCode`; 1 architect MEDIUM = a false SPEC-006 header-agreement example claiming the capacity-pause codes are headerless when they ship `Retry-After: 30`, fixed; plus test-coverage findings folded into a `forwardWSStreaming` routing-path test. R2: the architect's decision-log-collision finding, a false positive at R1 — `origin/main` max entry was 140 and PR #589 did not yet touch this file — became REAL when a concurrent session pushed Entries 141–143 to open PR #589 (head `46ba1f8`, 17:11) between rounds; this entry was renumbered 141→144 to sit above #589's reservation, then to 163 at merge time when `origin/main` had advanced to Entry 162 (Entries 141–162 landed from concurrent sessions between audit and merge, including a distinct Entry 144 for the v1.8.35 maintenance boundary), per the [[concurrent-claude-sessions-commit-visibility]] discipline of re-verifying against the current head. R3: doc-precision LOWs — the scoped-out-codes comment reworded (both transports ignore the provider override; the base code/retryable each emits differs pre-existing), and the SPEC-019 clause section refs corrected from §5 to §8). Re-trigger if a new `rate_limit_exceeded`-family code is added (triage into retryable vs permanent by the header + account-clamp test), or if a backoff header is added to any of the four abuse-limit codes (then flip that code to `true` alongside the header). **Owner: operator.** |

## One-line summary to remember

**Phase 2 exists to surface specific Phase 3 design changes.** If it
doesn't produce a single "I changed Phase 3 spec line X because of Phase
2 row Y" sentence, it didn't earn its 14 days.

| 2026-07-29 | **Entry 210 — B2 makes the autotune admission ceiling a routing predicate for strict hello-gate deployments, not just an operator diagnostic.** When `proof_of_weights.require_autotune_hello_gate:true`, a provider that was admitted with an observed verified autotune ceiling becomes route-ineligible if a later heartbeat serves an uncatalogued model or a catalogued model whose `MinRAMGB` exceeds that admitted ceiling; gate-off deployments stay observe-only. The same exclusion removes the session from buyer-serving capacity, so a canary floor cannot preserve a capacity/catalog integrity violation just because the provider is sole. The 30s trust-revalidation sweep also checks session-time autotune evidence freshness and exact admitted hardware tuple binding for capped strict-gate sessions; stale, missing, mismatched, or invalid evidence makes the session non-routable without hard-killing an in-flight request. | **Changing provider state to degraded/draining was rejected** because B2 is a routing exclusion, not a liveness sanction; state remains available for canary/breaker/warmup semantics. Enforcing while `require_autotune_hello_gate:false` was rejected because A5's production posture intentionally keeps the current live network in observe mode until the strict gate is explicitly re-enabled. Clearing stale-evidence exclusion from a normal heartbeat was rejected because returning to an in-ceiling model must not hide expired or tuple-mismatched evidence. Moving catalog/evidence lookups into `pool.Registry` was rejected because the pool lock must stay free of database/catalog I/O and the WS layer owns admission-catalog context. Allowing uncatalogued targets to pass by default was rejected because lack of `MinRAMGB` is the bypass FR-HG7 exists to close. | **Effective after targeted pool/ws tests, SPEC-032 governance/index checks, zero MEDIUM+ code/security/architecture audits, required CI, exact-head approval, and squash merge.** Re-trigger if admission ceiling flags become payout/billing inputs, if stale-evidence handling changes from route exclusion to eviction/probation, if canary floor starts overriding capacity/catalog/hash violations, if model catalog lookup semantics change, if gate-off observe mode starts mutating routing, or if proof-of-weights enforcement is expanded beyond verified hardware capacity. **Owner: coordinator trust/routing enforcement.** |

| 2026-07-29 | **Entry 211 — B5 turns strict hello-gate missing evidence into a non-buyer sandbox, and makes proof-of-weights gate config reloadable without restart.** When `proof_of_weights.require_autotune_hello_gate:true`, a provider with no verified in-window autotune evidence may connect only with `AdmissionSandboxed=true`; `RoutingEligible` and `ServingCapable` both exclude it, while internal/synthetic probe eligibility remains available. Sandboxed sessions still consume normal provisional admission/session limits and do not receive newly minted durable provider credentials. Affirmative gate failures still hard-close: dependency/lookup failures are `autotune_gate_unavailable`, invalid evidence is `autotune_evidence_invalid`, uncatalogued models are `autotune_model_uncatalogued`, and over-ceiling claims are `autotune_model_cap_exceeded`. SIGHUP now reloads `proof_of_weights.*`, validates hello-gate / telemetry-drift dependencies before publish, increments a monotonic proof generation, pre-quarantines live sessions before enabling the gate, revalidates boundedly, sandboxes live sessions without admitted verified tuples, fails closed on evidence lookup errors, swaps the telemetry-drift evaluator, clears gate exclusions for credential-authorized sessions, leaves sandbox-only/no-credential sessions sandboxed on gate-disable reload, and clears disabled telemetry-drift benchmark quarantines. | **Buyer-routable probation was rejected** because missing evidence is not proof of hardware capacity and would recreate the capability-gate bypass oracle SPEC-032 explicitly rejected. Hard-closing missing evidence was rejected for B5 because it makes dual-control operator approval a hard admission blocker on a one-operator/small-fleet path; the sandbox preserves operator visibility and internal probing without buyer traffic. Minting durable credentials or auto-promoting sandbox-only sessions on gate disable was rejected because evidence absence is not credential authority and must not silently bypass an evidence-bearing provider. Bypassing provisional admission/session limits was rejected because a non-buyer sandbox must not become an unbounded socket sink. Restart-only gate changes were rejected because the strict gate is an availability control on a fragile pool; safe operation needs no-restart relax/tighten plus atomic re-gating of existing sessions. Clearing stale/ceiling/sandbox flags from ordinary heartbeat was rejected because proof exclusions must be cleared by verified revalidation or an explicit gate-disable reload, not by provider-controlled state churn. | **Effective after targeted pool/ws/coordinator tests, SPEC-032 governance/index checks, zero MEDIUM+ code/security/architecture audits, required CI, exact-head approval, and squash merge.** Re-trigger if sandboxed providers become buyer-routable, if sandbox state affects payout/billing, if sandbox sessions stop consuming provisional admission/session limits, if sandbox sessions receive durable credentials, if gate-disable reload auto-promotes sandbox-only/no-credential sessions, if internal probes start bypassing auth, if strict-gate dependency errors stop failing closed, if proof-of-weights reload stops pre-quarantining before publish, if telemetry-drift reload becomes partially published, or if gate-disable reload stops clearing credential-authorized proof gate exclusions and disabled benchmark quarantines. **Owner: coordinator trust/routing enforcement.** |

| 2026-08-04 | **Entry 218 — gpt-oss-20b admission uses a release-ordered v2 hardware-evidence contract and isolated candidate process.** The provider now emits `hardware_evidence.autotune.v2` with the Harmony probe protocol, an executable-digest binding, and immutable catalog-row identity; coordinator enqueue, verifier, persistence, and gate consumers require the same exact bindings. Automatically deploying Pearl from GitHub was rejected because coordinator production mutation remains outside this repository's release authority. | **Effective after the rollout workflow and its static release-security guards pass, then after a real v1.8.82 publication, exact Pearl recommendation deployment, immutable discovery publication, and anonymous discovery proof.** Re-trigger if stable releases become discoverable before the exact recommendation is live, if the rollout can run from a non-`main` source, if the final live recheck is removed, or if an external Pearl deployment is silently folded into this workflow. **Owner: provider lifecycle / release operations.** |
